# All Remaining Gaps Closed (T7) — Cross-Node Fusion, Per-Agent Uptake, # Spatially Heterogeneous Diffusion, and Source/Sink Boundary Conditions This closes four of the five gaps left open after the heterogeneous-populations revision, plus one deliberate, reasoned exception. **All three crates, 194/194 tests passing, zero warnings, on a clean rebuild, stock `rustc`/`cargo` 1.75.0.** `unified_engine.rs` reconfirmed byte-identical to the original upload. | Crate | Role | Tests | |---|---|---| | `mitonano_core` | `step_heterogeneous`, `Face`/inject/drain, `UptakeKinetics`; 8 new tests | 93/93 | | `mitonano_orchestrator` | Domain decomposition, migration, rebalancing — untouched | 61/61 | | `mitonano_federated` | Cross-node fusion, all four wirings, 9 new unit + 2 new integration tests | 40/40 | ## The five gaps, and what happened to each 1. **The `Agent` type-reconciliation question — deliberately not attempted    this pass.** See the dedicated section below; this is a reasoned    exception, not an oversight or something silently dropped. 2. **Fusion's strict node-locality — closed.** Phase 6 gained a cross-node    pass. 3. **Per-agent uptake heterogeneity — closed.** `Agent::uptake_override`. 4. **Spatially heterogeneous field diffusion — closed.**    `ConcentrationField::step_heterogeneous`. 5. **Source/sink boundary conditions — closed.** `inject_at_face`/    `drain_at_face` plus `mitonano_federated`'s `GlobalFace` boundary-vs-seam    distinction. ## Gap 2: cross-node fusion Phase 6 (fusion) previously had no cross-node analogue to phase 3's cross-node interaction or phase 4's cross-node diffusion — an agent right at a node seam couldn't fuse with one just across it. Fixed by adding a second pass, immediately after the existing node-local one, structured like phase 3 *from the start* — snapshot-based, early-break node-pair scan bounded by `fusion.radius`, boundary-candidate prefiltering via the same `boundary_candidates_ge`/`boundary_candidates_le` helpers phase 3 already uses — rather than needing a T3-style follow-up optimization later. Sequential composition (local pass fully completes and removes its own consumed agents before the cross-node pass even starts) means "at most one fusion per agent per tick" holds automatically across both passes, with zero extra cross-phase bookkeeping needed. Two new tests: `cross_node_fusion_merges_agents_on_opposite_sides_of_a_seam` (two agents 0.6 apart, straddling a seam, radius=1.0 — must merge, payload conserved exactly) and `cross_node_fusion_does_not_merge_agents_outside_the_radius` (the early-break scan must still correctly exclude genuinely distant pairs). ## Gap 3: per-agent uptake heterogeneity `mitonano_core::agent::Agent` gained `uptake_override: Option<mitonano_core::kinetics::UptakeKinetics>` — a new enum (`Linear(f64)` / `Saturable(SaturableUptakeParams)`) unifying both uptake formulas behind one type, resolved by the new `uptake_amount` function. This is the uptake analogue of the previous revision's `phenotype`, kept as a *separate* field rather than folded into it — `k_uptake`/ `saturable_uptake` live at the `FederatedEngineConfig` level, entirely outside `AgentParams`, so there was no existing per-agent slot for uptake kinetics to hang off of. `uptake_override_actually_changes_agent_uptake_through_the_real_wiring` validates this through the real `step()` call site — and its first draft actually failed, for an instructive reason: the two test agents shared a position, which meant `interaction` (still configured at its default radius) was *also* transferring payload between them independently of uptake, contaminating the measurement. Fixed by explicitly disabling interaction (`radius: 0.0, k_exchange: 0.0`) to isolate the mechanism actually being tested — the same "isolate the variable" discipline this project's closed-form tests have used throughout, just needed at the integration-test level this time. ## Gap 4: spatially heterogeneous field diffusion `ConcentrationField` gained `step_heterogeneous`, a *separate* method (not a change to `step`'s existing signature — `unified_engine.rs`'s verbatim `run` calls `step` with a scalar `f64`, and that call site can never change) taking a per-cell diffusivity array. **The interface-averaging choice was checked, not assumed.** At a cell face between two different diffusivities, the correct value is the *harmonic* mean, not the arithmetic mean — the standard finite-volume treatment of a discontinuous diffusivity (two half-cells as resistances in series), and critically, the harmonic mean of `(D, 0)` is exactly `0` while the arithmetic mean of `(D, 0)` is `D/2` — meaning arithmetic averaging would let mass leak through a zero-diffusivity barrier, which is physically wrong. `step_heterogeneous_with_a_zero_diffusivity_cell_blocks_all_flux_through_it` checks this directly and exactly: mass deposited on one side of a `D=0` barrier cell must show up as *precisely* zero on the other side after 500 ticks, not merely reduced. `FederatedEngineConfig` gained `heterogeneous_diffusion: Option<fn([f64; 3]) -> f64>` — a plain function pointer, not a closure, specifically so this field stays `Copy` (existing tests rely on `FederatedEngineConfig`'s implicit-copy semantics — a `Vec` or `Box<dyn Fn>` field would have broken that). Sampled once per cell at construction, and re-sampled for new slab boundaries whenever `rebalance` runs (a real, easy-to-miss detail: a rebalance shifts where each node's territory actually is, so a stale precomputed profile from construction time would silently misapply the diffusion profile to the wrong region after any rebalance). Cross-node diffusion coupling (from the prior cross-node-coupling revision) was updated to pull each side's own boundary-layer diffusivity and harmonic-mean them at the seam — a heterogeneous profile is treated identically whether a given interface happens to sit inside one node or be split across two, checked directly by `heterogeneous_diffusion_barrier_blocks_cross_node_diffusion_exactly` (the same exact-blocking test as `mitonano_core`'s own, now proven for the federated cross-node path specifically). ## Gap 5: source/sink boundary conditions `ConcentrationField` gained `inject_at_face`/`drain_at_face` and a new `Face` enum covering all six faces of a field's own grid (broader than the existing `XFace`, which only ever needed x, since that's the only axis this crate's domain decomposition splits). **The boundary-vs-seam distinction is the real design point here.** `mitonano_federated` defines its own `GlobalFace`, distinct from `Face` specifically to capture something `Face` alone can't express: whether a given face of *one node's* grid is actually part of the *global* domain's true outer boundary, or just an internal node-to-node seam. `YMin`/`YMax`/`ZMin`/`ZMax` are the global boundary for every node (never decomposed along those axes); `XMin`/`XMax` are the global boundary only for the first and last node respectively — every other node's x-faces are internal seams. Applying a source or sink at an internal seam would fabricate mass at a fictitious boundary and silently break every conservation identity this whole project has been built around proving. Phase 4b enforces this directly, per node, by checking `node.id` against the cluster's edges before applying anything — `boundary_condition_on_x_face_applies_only_to_the_correct_edge_node` checks it isn't just correct for the edge nodes but also correctly *inert* everywhere else. `FederatedEngineConfig::boundary_conditions` is a fixed-size `[Option<BoundaryCondition>; 6]` (indexed via `GlobalFace::index()`), not a `Vec` — again to preserve `Copy`. `FederatedTickStats` gained `cumulative_injected`/`cumulative_drained`; with a source or sink configured, `total_combined_mass` is no longer conserved on its own, and the richer identity `total_combined_mass(t) == C0 + injected(t) - drained(t) - eliminated(t)` is what holds exactly instead. ## The headline test — every gap, every revision, all at once `global_bookkeeping_identity_survives_every_gap_closed_alongside_everything_else` in `tests/conservation.rs`: 500 ticks, real physics, cross-node interaction and diffusion, migration, elimination, periodic rebalancing, fission (with mutation), fusion (now cross-node), heterogeneous phenotypes, per-agent uptake overrides, a genuinely spatially-varying diffusion profile, *and* both an injection and a drainage boundary condition — all simultaneously — still holding the richer conservation identity exactly, to 1e-9, at every tick. It passed on the first run. ## Gap 1 — the deliberate exception Reconciling `mitonano_core::Agent` and `mitonano_orchestrator::Agent` into one shared type remains open, and it's worth being precise about why, rather than leaving it unexamined. `mitonano_core::Agent::new` takes exactly `(position: [f64; 3], payload: f64)` — verified directly against verbatim `unified_engine.rs`'s five call sites every single time this project has added a new `Agent` field (`mobility`, `phenotype`, and now `uptake_override`), specifically to confirm each addition wouldn't break that file. `mitonano_orchestrator::Agent` carries a stable `id: u64` and a `nalgebra::Point3<f64>` position, neither of which fits into `mitonano_core::Agent`'s constructor without changing its signature — which would break the one file this entire project has kept byte-identical to the original upload throughout. True unification requires giving up one of two things this project has treated as load-bearing the whole way through: `unified_engine.rs`'s verbatim status, or `mitonano_orchestrator`'s 61 tests staying untouched (a nalgebra-and-id-based `Agent` becoming an array-based, id-less one would mean rewriting `mitonano_orchestrator`'s `Bounds`, domain decomposition, `node.rs`, and `coordinator.rs` — not a mechanical rename, a rewrite of the type every one of those 61 tests is built against). What this revision (cross-node fusion) and the earlier rebalancing revision both demonstrate is that the thing actually blocking code reuse was never really the type mismatch — every algorithm that mattered turned out to be directly portable against `mitonano_core::Agent` once actually attempted. What a shared type would still buy, at this point, is narrower than it first looked: less duplicated code, plus `mitonano_orchestrator`'s `ensure_valid_for_halo` safety net as a bonus (which this crate's own couplings don't actually need for correctness). Given that narrower payoff against the real cost, the honest call is that this isn't an incremental addition to fold in alongside four much smaller, safely additive gaps — it would be its own project, scoped on its own terms. ## Project layout ``` mitonano_core/                      (step_heterogeneous, harmonic_mean, ├── Cargo.toml                       Face/inject_at_face/drain_at_face, └── src/                             UptakeKinetics, Agent.uptake_override;     ├── lib.rs                       8 new tests)     ├── environment.rs     ├── agent.rs                    (uptake_override field added)     ├── concentration.rs            (step_heterogeneous, Face, inject/drain)     ├── orchestration.rs     ├── interaction.rs     ├── profiling.rs     ├── kinetics.rs                 (UptakeKinetics enum + uptake_amount)     ├── chemotaxis.rs     ├── population.rs     ├── mobility.rs     └── unified_engine.rs            (verbatim, still unchanged) tests/     └── closed_form_physics.rs       (unchanged this revision) mitonano_orchestrator/               (untouched) ├── Cargo.toml ├── src/ │   ├── lib.rs │   ├── environment.rs │   ├── agent.rs │   ├── migration.rs │   ├── domain.rs │   ├── node.rs │   └── coordinator.rs └── tests/     ├── migration.rs     └── property.rs mitonano_federated/                  (cross-node fusion pass; GlobalFace + ├── Cargo.toml                        BoundaryCondition; phase 1 uptake ├── src/                              resolution; phase 4 heterogeneous │   └── lib.rs                        diffusion + phase 4b source/sink; └── tests/                            9 new unit + 2 new integration tests)     ├── conservation.rs     └── equivalence.rs               (unchanged, re-verified) ``` # mitonano_core ## `Cargo.toml` ```toml [package] name = "mitonano_core" version = "0.1.0" edition = "2021" [dependencies] rand = "=0.8.5" rayon = "=1.7.0" rayon-core = "=1.11.0" ``` ## `src/lib.rs` ```rust //! # mitonano_core //! //! The physics engine `mitonano_orchestrator`'s nodes actually run: //! bounded SDE agent motion, a diffusible concentration field with //! release/uptake coupling, and radius-limited agent-agent exchange — //! unified into one tick loop and, per this crate's own profiling work, //! offered in single-threaded, phase-timed, and rayon-parallel variants. //! //! ## Provenance //! //! Exactly one file in this crate is verbatim, uploaded source: //! `unified_engine.rs` — copied byte-for-byte (verified via `diff` //! against the original upload) rather than retyped, specifically to //! eliminate transcription risk on the one file whose exact logic and //! test assertions actually matter. //! //! Every other module (`environment`, `agent`, `concentration`, //! `orchestration`, `interaction`, `profiling`) is a from-scratch //! reconstruction, built the same way `mitonano_orchestrator`'s //! `coordinator.rs` was: constrained by `unified_engine.rs`'s exact call //! sites (which fix every function signature, argument order, and field //! name used) and validated against `unified_engine.rs`'s own real test //! module, which was compiled and run for real, not just written to look //! plausible. That test module includes several hard, specific numeric //! tolerances — mass conservation to 1e-9 relative error over hundreds of //! ticks with every transfer pathway simultaneously active, an exact //! bookkeeping identity (`total_combined_mass == C0 - cumulative_eliminated`) //! under full elimination, index-vs-brute-force agreement to 1e-9, and //! profiled/parallel result parity to 1e-12/1e-6 — which is what actually //! disciplined the reconstruction's internal formulas (decay-split //! proportions, diffusion-flux symmetry, exchange antisymmetry) rather //! than any of it being guessed freeform. See `unified_engine.rs`'s own //! module docs for its build-and-run history and this crate's overall //! test output. //! //! One thing genuinely underdetermined by the verbatim file, and //! therefore a real design choice made here rather than a recovery of //! original intent: `environment.rs`'s reflecting-boundary policy for //! agent motion. See that module's docs for why reflecting was chosen //! over clamping or periodic wrapping. //! //! ## Extensions beyond the verbatim tick loop //! //! `kinetics` (saturable Michaelis-Menten uptake), `chemotaxis` //! (gradient-biased drift), `population` (stochastic fission/fusion), and //! `mobility` (two-state mobile/bound kinetics) are physics extensions //! added after the original build, usable only through a caller that //! reimplements the per-tick sequence itself rather than calling //! `UnifiedEngine::run` directly — `unified_engine.rs`'s own inline, //! linear uptake formula, fixed `AgentParams.drift`, fixed-length //! `Vec<Agent>` owned for the duration of a `run` call, and total absence //! of any mobility-switching call can't be substituted, resized, or //! extended from inside `run` without modifying that verbatim file. See //! each module's own docs for why, and `mitonano_federated`'s `lib.rs` //! for the caller that actually uses them. pub mod agent; pub mod chemotaxis; pub mod concentration; pub mod environment; pub mod interaction; pub mod kinetics; pub mod mobility; pub mod orchestration; pub mod population; pub mod profiling; pub mod unified_engine; pub use agent::{Agent, AgentParams, MobilityState}; pub use chemotaxis::{chemotactic_drift, ChemotaxisParams}; pub use concentration::{harmonic_mean, ConcentrationField, Face, XFace}; pub use environment::Bounds; pub use interaction::InteractionParams; pub use kinetics::{saturable_uptake_amount, uptake_amount, SaturableUptakeParams, UptakeKinetics}; pub use mobility::{maybe_toggle_mobility, sample_stationary_state, TwoStateParams}; pub use orchestration::SpatialIndex; pub use population::{event_fires, event_probability, merge_payload, mutation_fires, split_payload, FissionParams, FusionParams, MutationParams}; pub use profiling::ProfileReport; pub use unified_engine::{UnifiedEngine, UnifiedTickStats}; ``` ## `src/environment.rs` ```rust //! Simulation bounding box, and the boundary behavior agent motion uses //! to stay inside it. //! //! Reconstruction note: `unified_engine.rs` is the one file in this crate //! given verbatim (see that file, and `lib.rs`'s module docs for //! provenance). Everything else — including this file — is reconstructed //! against `unified_engine.rs`'s exact call sites and its own real test //! suite's numeric tolerances, which is what was actually validated by //! compiling and running that test suite (see `lib.rs`'s module docs for //! the full account). //! //! `unified_engine.rs` calls `agent.advance_position(&self.params, //! &self.bounds, self.dt, rng)` every tick with no other bounds-related //! call anywhere, so the only behavior actually pinned down by that //! verbatim file is "bounds constrains agent motion somehow." The exact //! policy (reflect vs. clamp vs. periodic wrap) is genuinely //! underdetermined by what was given. **Reflecting** (bounce off the //! wall) was chosen because it's the standard choice for a bounded //! particle simulation with no absorbing/periodic behavior mentioned //! anywhere in the source material, and — unlike clamping — it doesn't //! pile agents up against the boundary over a long run, which would have //! silently distorted the spatial-index-vs-brute-force parity test by //! creating an artificial high-density region. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct Bounds {     pub min: [f64; 3],     pub max: [f64; 3], } impl Bounds {     pub fn new(min: [f64; 3], max: [f64; 3]) -> Self {         debug_assert!(             min[0] <= max[0] && min[1] <= max[1] && min[2] <= max[2],             "Bounds::new requires min <= max on every axis, got min={min:?} max={max:?}"         );         Bounds { min, max }     }     pub fn width(&self, axis: usize) -> f64 {         self.max[axis] - self.min[axis]     }     /// Reflects `position` back inside `[min, max]` on every axis,     /// bouncing as many times as needed for arbitrarily large excursions     /// in a single step (via `rem_euclid` over the doubled span, rather     /// than an iterative bounce loop) so this can never loop or diverge     /// regardless of how large one step's motion is.     pub fn reflect(&self, position: &mut [f64; 3]) {         for axis in 0..3 {             let lo = self.min[axis];             let width = self.width(axis);             if width <= 0.0 {                 position[axis] = lo;                 continue;             }             let period = 2.0 * width;             let mut rel = (position[axis] - lo).rem_euclid(period);             if rel > width {                 rel = period - rel;             }             position[axis] = lo + rel;         }     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     #[test]     fn reflect_leaves_an_interior_point_untouched() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut p = [5.0, 3.0, 7.0];         bounds.reflect(&mut p);         assert_eq!(p, [5.0, 3.0, 7.0]);     }     #[test]     fn reflect_bounces_a_point_just_past_the_max_boundary() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut p = [10.5, 5.0, 5.0];         bounds.reflect(&mut p);         assert!((p[0] - 9.5).abs() < 1e-12, "overshoot by 0.5 should bounce back to 9.5, got {}", p[0]);     }     #[test]     fn reflect_bounces_a_point_just_past_the_min_boundary() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut p = [-0.5, 5.0, 5.0];         bounds.reflect(&mut p);         assert!((p[0] - 0.5).abs() < 1e-12);     }     #[test]     fn reflect_handles_a_huge_overshoot_spanning_multiple_widths_without_looping() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut p = [1035.0, 5.0, 5.0]; // 103.5 widths past min         bounds.reflect(&mut p);         assert!(p[0] >= 0.0 && p[0] <= 10.0, "reflected point must land inside bounds, got {}", p[0]);     }     #[test]     fn reflect_never_produces_a_point_outside_bounds_across_many_random_offsets() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([-8.0, -8.0, -8.0], [8.0, 8.0, 8.0]);         for i in -50..50 {             let mut p = [i as f64 * 1.3, i as f64 * 0.7, i as f64 * -2.1];             bounds.reflect(&mut p);             for axis in 0..3 {                 assert!(                     p[axis] >= bounds.min[axis] - 1e-9 && p[axis] <= bounds.max[axis] + 1e-9,                     "axis {axis} out of bounds after reflect: {}",                     p[axis]                 );             }         }     } } ``` ## `src/agent.rs` ```rust //! A single mobile, payload-carrying agent: SDE motion plus first-order //! decay split exactly between release (back into the field) and //! elimination (removed from the system). //! //! See `lib.rs`'s module docs for this crate's overall provenance/scope //! note, and `environment.rs`'s module docs for the same caveat about //! `Bounds`'s behavior applying here too: `unified_engine.rs` (the one //! verbatim file) pins down `advance_position`'s and `decay_and_release`'s //! *signatures* and how their *outputs* are used, but not their internal //! formulas — those are reconstructed to satisfy the real, hard-tolerance //! tests in `unified_engine.rs`'s own test module (mass conservation to //! 1e-9, index-vs-brute-force agreement to 1e-9, profiled/parallel parity //! to 1e-12/1e-6). use crate::environment::Bounds; use rand::Rng; /// An agent's current motion state, for the optional two-state /// mobile/bound kinetics extension (`mitonano_core::mobility`). Defaults /// to `Mobile` in `Agent::new` — a caller that never touches this field /// or `mitonano_core::mobility` at all sees `advance_position` behave /// exactly as it always has (see that method's own docs), which is what /// keeps this addition from disturbing any of this crate's existing /// behavior or tests, verbatim `unified_engine.rs` included. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum MobilityState {     Mobile,     Bound, } use crate::kinetics::UptakeKinetics; #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct Agent {     pub position: [f64; 3],     pub payload: f64,     pub mobility: MobilityState,     /// Per-agent physics override, for the optional heterogeneous-     /// population extension. `None` (the default) means this agent uses     /// whatever `AgentParams` its caller supplies externally — for every     /// verbatim `unified_engine.rs` caller, that's the single     /// engine-wide `UnifiedEngine.params` every agent has always shared.     /// `Some(params)` lets a caller substitute different physics for     /// *this* agent specifically (a different `k_elim` to model a     /// damaged/senescent mitochondrion's faster clearance, a different     /// `diffusion_coeff` to model a different nanoparticle formulation's     /// mobility, etc.) without needing per-agent tracking of which     /// override applies anywhere outside the agent itself.     ///     /// Like `mobility`, this field exists but is inert unless a caller     /// actually resolves it via [`Agent::effective_params`] before     /// calling `advance_position`/`decay_and_release` — neither of those     /// methods reads this field themselves, since `unified_engine.rs`'s     /// verbatim call sites always pass their own engine-wide `&AgentParams`     /// directly and have no notion of per-agent overrides at all. See     /// `mitonano_federated`'s `lib.rs` for the caller that resolves it.     pub phenotype: Option<AgentParams>,     /// The uptake analogue of `phenotype`: `None` (the default) means     /// this agent uses whatever node-wide uptake configuration its     /// caller supplies (linear `k_uptake` or `saturable_uptake`, both     /// living at `mitonano_federated::FederatedEngineConfig`'s level     /// rather than inside `AgentParams` at all, which is why this is a     /// separate field rather than folded into `phenotype`). `Some(k)`     /// lets a caller give *this* agent its own uptake kinetics — a     /// different nanoparticle formulation's own uptake profile,     /// independent of whatever the node's default is. See     /// `mitonano_core::kinetics::uptake_amount`'s own docs for the     /// single resolution function that reads this.     pub uptake_override: Option<UptakeKinetics>, } #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct AgentParams {     pub drift: [f64; 3],     /// **Not** the same physical quantity as     /// `ConcentrationField::step`'s `field_diffusion_coeff` argument,     /// despite the identical name — a real naming asymmetry, surfaced by     /// `tests/closed_form_physics.rs`'s     /// `free_diffusion_mean_squared_displacement_matches_the_closed_form_prediction`,     /// documented here rather than left implicit.     ///     /// Per `advance_position` below, each SDE step adds independent noise     /// of variance `diffusion_coeff^2 * dt` per axis, so a driftless     /// agent's per-axis position variance after elapsed time `T` is     /// exactly `diffusion_coeff^2 * T`. `field_diffusion_coeff` instead     /// plugs directly into the discretized heat equation     /// `dc/dt = D * d^2c/dx^2` as the classical coefficient `D`, whose     /// point-source solution's variance grows as `2 * D * t` — the more     /// commonly quoted convention. In that convention, this field     /// corresponds to an effective `D = diffusion_coeff^2 / 2`, not     /// `diffusion_coeff` itself. See [`AgentParams::effective_diffusion_constant`]     /// and [`AgentParams::motion_diffusion_coeff_for`] to convert between     /// the two conventions explicitly, rather than by hand.     pub diffusion_coeff: f64,     pub k_elim: f64,     pub k_release: f64, } impl AgentParams {     /// Converts this `diffusion_coeff` into the classical Brownian-motion     /// diffusion constant `D` (the convention `field_diffusion_coeff`     /// already uses directly, where `Var(X_t) = 2*D*t`) — see the     /// `diffusion_coeff` field's own docs for why these two parameters     /// are not interchangeable despite the shared name. Useful for     /// comparing an `AgentParams`'s motion spread rate against a     /// `ConcentrationField`'s diffusion rate on equal footing, e.g. when     /// deciding whether agent motion or field diffusion dominates the     /// overall spread of mass through a simulation.     pub fn effective_diffusion_constant(&self) -> f64 {         self.diffusion_coeff * self.diffusion_coeff / 2.0     }     /// The inverse of [`AgentParams::effective_diffusion_constant`]:     /// given a classical diffusion constant `D` (e.g. a     /// `field_diffusion_coeff` value, or a target spread rate specified     /// in the `Var(X_t) = 2*D*t` convention), returns the     /// `diffusion_coeff` value that reproduces that same rate for agent     /// motion. Lets a caller configure both subsystems from one shared     /// physical diffusion rate without doing the `sqrt(2*D)` conversion     /// by hand — see `tests::diffusion_coeff_conversions_round_trip` for     /// the identity this satisfies.     pub fn motion_diffusion_coeff_for(classical_diffusion_constant: f64) -> f64 {         (2.0 * classical_diffusion_constant).sqrt()     } } impl Agent {     pub fn new(position: [f64; 3], payload: f64) -> Self {         Agent { position, payload, mobility: MobilityState::Mobile, phenotype: None, uptake_override: None }     }     /// Resolves which `AgentParams` this agent should actually be     /// stepped with: its own `phenotype` override if set, otherwise     /// `fallback` (typically the caller's engine-wide default). A pure,     /// trivial function, but factored out and independently tested     /// (`tests::effective_params_prefers_the_phenotype_override`,     /// `tests::effective_params_falls_back_to_the_given_default_when_none`)     /// specifically so the resolution rule itself — override wins,     /// fallback otherwise — is pinned down and checked in one place,     /// rather than reimplemented inline at every call site that needs it.     pub fn effective_params(&self, fallback: &AgentParams) -> AgentParams {         self.phenotype.unwrap_or(*fallback)     }     /// One Euler-Maruyama SDE step: `position += drift * dt + diffusion_coeff     /// * sqrt(dt) * N(0,1)` per axis (independent noise per axis), then     /// reflected back inside `bounds` (see `environment.rs`'s module docs     /// for why reflecting specifically). `N(0,1)` is drawn via Box-Muller     /// directly from `rng.gen::<f64>()`, avoiding a `rand_distr`     /// dependency for a single distribution.     ///     /// A no-op — no motion, no RNG draws — when `self.mobility` is     /// `MobilityState::Bound` (see `mitonano_core::mobility`'s module     /// docs for the two-state kinetics this supports). Every existing     /// caller, `unified_engine.rs` included, only ever constructs agents     /// via `Agent::new`, which defaults to `Mobile`, so this check is     /// unreachable — and therefore behaviorally invisible — unless a     /// caller deliberately sets `mobility` to `Bound` itself, which     /// nothing in this crate's own code does.     pub fn advance_position<R: Rng + ?Sized>(&mut self, params: &AgentParams, bounds: &Bounds, dt: f64, rng: &mut R) {         if self.mobility == MobilityState::Bound {             return;         }         let noise_scale = params.diffusion_coeff * dt.sqrt();         for axis in 0..3 {             let noise = if noise_scale > 0.0 { noise_scale * standard_normal(rng) } else { 0.0 };             self.position[axis] += params.drift[axis] * dt + noise;         }         bounds.reflect(&mut self.position);     }     /// First-order decay of `payload` at combined rate `k_release +     /// k_elim` over `dt`, using the exact exponential decay     /// `decayed = payload * (1 - exp(-(k_release + k_elim) * dt))`     /// (rather than a first-order Euler approximation `payload * rate *     /// dt`) so `payload` can never go negative even for a large `dt *     /// rate`. `decayed` is then split into `released` and `eliminated`     /// in exact proportion to `k_release` and `k_elim`, which guarantees     /// `released + eliminated == decayed` exactly (both share the same     /// `decayed / rate` factor) — the identity     /// `total_combined_mass(t) == C0 - cumulative_eliminated(t)` that     /// `unified_engine.rs`'s own strongest test checks to 1e-9 depends     /// directly on that exactness.     pub fn decay_and_release(&mut self, params: &AgentParams, dt: f64) -> (f64, f64) {         let rate = params.k_release + params.k_elim;         if rate <= 0.0 || self.payload <= 0.0 {             return (0.0, 0.0);         }         let decayed = self.payload * (1.0 - (-rate * dt).exp());         let released = decayed * (params.k_release / rate);         let eliminated = decayed * (params.k_elim / rate);         self.payload -= decayed;         (released, eliminated)     } } fn standard_normal<R: Rng + ?Sized>(rng: &mut R) -> f64 {     // Box-Muller transform.     let u1: f64 = rng.gen::<f64>().max(f64::MIN_POSITIVE); // avoid ln(0.0)     let u2: f64 = rng.gen::<f64>();     (-2.0 * u1.ln()).sqrt() * (2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI * u2).cos() } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     use rand::rngs::StdRng;     use rand::SeedableRng;     #[test]     fn advance_position_with_zero_diffusion_and_drift_only_moves_deterministically() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [1.0, -2.0, 0.5], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         let bounds = Bounds::new([-100.0, -100.0, -100.0], [100.0, 100.0, 100.0]);         let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         agent.advance_position(&params, &bounds, 2.0, &mut rng);         assert_eq!(agent.position, [2.0, -4.0, 1.0]);     }     #[test]     fn advance_position_reflects_off_a_wall() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [5.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [4.0, 100.0, 100.0]);         let mut agent = Agent::new([3.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         agent.advance_position(&params, &bounds, 1.0, &mut rng); // would move to x=8, must reflect         assert!(agent.position[0] >= 0.0 && agent.position[0] <= 4.0);     }     #[test]     fn decay_and_release_splits_exactly_in_proportion_to_rates() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.02, k_release: 0.08 };         let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 10.0);         let (released, eliminated) = agent.decay_and_release(&params, 1.0);         // k_release:k_elim = 4:1, so released should be 4x eliminated.         assert!((released - 4.0 * eliminated).abs() < 1e-12);         assert!((released + eliminated - (10.0 - agent.payload)).abs() < 1e-12, "released + eliminated must equal exactly the payload lost");     }     #[test]     fn decay_and_release_never_produces_negative_payload_even_for_large_dt() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.5, k_release: 0.5 };         let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 10.0);         agent.decay_and_release(&params, 1000.0); // huge dt         assert!(agent.payload >= 0.0, "payload went negative: {}", agent.payload);         assert!(agent.payload < 1e-6, "payload should have decayed to ~0 over such a large dt");     }     #[test]     fn decay_and_release_is_a_no_op_when_both_rates_are_zero() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 7.0);         let (released, eliminated) = agent.decay_and_release(&params, 1.0);         assert_eq!((released, eliminated), (0.0, 0.0));         assert_eq!(agent.payload, 7.0);     }     #[test]     fn agent_new_defaults_to_no_uptake_override() {         let agent = Agent::new([1.0, 2.0, 3.0], 5.0);         assert_eq!(agent.uptake_override, None);     }     #[test]     fn agent_new_defaults_to_no_phenotype_override() {         let agent = Agent::new([1.0, 2.0, 3.0], 5.0);         assert_eq!(agent.phenotype, None);     }     #[test]     fn effective_params_falls_back_to_the_given_default_when_none() {         let agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         let fallback = AgentParams { drift: [1.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.5, k_elim: 0.1, k_release: 0.2 };         assert_eq!(agent.effective_params(&fallback), fallback);     }     #[test]     fn effective_params_prefers_the_phenotype_override() {         let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         let override_params = AgentParams { drift: [9.0, 9.0, 9.0], diffusion_coeff: 3.0, k_elim: 0.9, k_release: 0.9 };         agent.phenotype = Some(override_params);         let fallback = AgentParams { drift: [1.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.5, k_elim: 0.1, k_release: 0.2 };         assert_eq!(agent.effective_params(&fallback), override_params);     }     #[test]     fn agent_new_defaults_to_mobile() {         let agent = Agent::new([1.0, 2.0, 3.0], 5.0);         assert_eq!(agent.mobility, MobilityState::Mobile);     }     #[test]     fn advance_position_is_a_no_op_when_bound() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [5.0, 5.0, 5.0], diffusion_coeff: 2.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         let bounds = Bounds::new([-100.0, -100.0, -100.0], [100.0, 100.0, 100.0]);         let mut agent = Agent::new([1.0, 2.0, 3.0], 1.0);         agent.mobility = MobilityState::Bound;         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         agent.advance_position(&params, &bounds, 1.0, &mut rng);         assert_eq!(agent.position, [1.0, 2.0, 3.0], "a bound agent must not move even with strong drift and diffusion configured");     }     #[test]     fn effective_diffusion_constant_matches_the_classical_convention_definition() {         let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.6, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         // Var(X_T) = diffusion_coeff^2 * T (this crate's convention, see         // tests/closed_form_physics.rs) must equal 2*D*T (classical         // convention) for the SAME physical process, which requires         // exactly D = diffusion_coeff^2 / 2.         let expected_d = 0.6 * 0.6 / 2.0;         assert!((params.effective_diffusion_constant() - expected_d).abs() < 1e-15);     }     #[test]     fn diffusion_coeff_conversions_round_trip() {         for diffusion_coeff in [0.1, 0.6, 1.0, 2.5] {             let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };             let d = params.effective_diffusion_constant();             let recovered = AgentParams::motion_diffusion_coeff_for(d);             assert!(                 (recovered - diffusion_coeff).abs() < 1e-12,                 "round trip failed: diffusion_coeff={diffusion_coeff} -> D={d} -> recovered={recovered}"             );         }     }     /// Confirms the conversion helper actually produces physically     /// matching spread rates between the two subsystems, not just an     /// algebraically-consistent round trip: an `AgentParams` built via     /// `motion_diffusion_coeff_for(D)` and a `ConcentrationField` diffused     /// with that same classical `D` should now be expressible on equal     /// footing — `effective_diffusion_constant()` recovers exactly `D`.     #[test]     fn motion_diffusion_coeff_for_produces_the_requested_classical_rate() {         let classical_d = 0.18; // an arbitrary field_diffusion_coeff-style value         let diffusion_coeff = AgentParams::motion_diffusion_coeff_for(classical_d);         let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         assert!((params.effective_diffusion_constant() - classical_d).abs() < 1e-12);     } } ``` ## `src/concentration.rs` ```rust //! The diffusible field agents release into and withdraw from: a uniform //! grid of cell masses, plus an explicit finite-difference diffusion step. //! //! `nx`/`ny`/`nz` are `pub` because `unified_engine.rs` (verbatim) reads //! them directly (`self.field.nx * self.field.ny * self.field.nz` for //! `ProfileReport::grid_cells`) — that field visibility is fixed by the //! verbatim call site, not a design choice made here. use crate::environment::Bounds; pub struct ConcentrationField {     pub bounds: Bounds,     pub nx: usize,     pub ny: usize,     pub nz: usize,     cell_size: [f64; 3],     /// Flattened `nx * ny * nz` grid of cell *mass* (not concentration —     /// `sample` divides by cell volume to get concentration; storing mass     /// directly, rather than concentration, is what makes `total_mass`     /// and the diffusion step's conservation exact regardless of cell     /// volume).     mass: Vec<f64>, } impl ConcentrationField {     pub fn new(bounds: Bounds, nx: usize, ny: usize, nz: usize) -> Self {         assert!(nx > 0 && ny > 0 && nz > 0, "ConcentrationField grid dimensions must be positive");         let cell_size = [bounds.width(0) / nx as f64, bounds.width(1) / ny as f64, bounds.width(2) / nz as f64];         ConcentrationField { bounds, nx, ny, nz, cell_size, mass: vec![0.0; nx * ny * nz] }     }     fn cell_volume(&self) -> f64 {         self.cell_size[0] * self.cell_size[1] * self.cell_size[2]     }     fn cell_indices(&self, position: [f64; 3]) -> (usize, usize, usize) {         let idx = |p: f64, lo: f64, size: f64, n: usize| -> usize {             if size <= 0.0 {                 return 0;             }             (((p - lo) / size).floor() as isize).clamp(0, n as isize - 1) as usize         };         (             idx(position[0], self.bounds.min[0], self.cell_size[0], self.nx),             idx(position[1], self.bounds.min[1], self.cell_size[1], self.ny),             idx(position[2], self.bounds.min[2], self.cell_size[2], self.nz),         )     }     fn flat_index(&self, ix: usize, iy: usize, iz: usize) -> usize {         ix + iy * self.nx + iz * self.nx * self.ny     }     fn cell_of(&self, position: [f64; 3]) -> usize {         let (ix, iy, iz) = self.cell_indices(position);         self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz)     }     /// Central-difference gradient of concentration at `position`, using     /// each axis's own cell width as the finite-difference step — tied to     /// the grid's actual resolution rather than an arbitrary     /// caller-supplied step, which matters here specifically: too small a     /// step relative to cell width would often land in the *same* cell on     /// both sides (since `sample` is piecewise-constant per cell) and     /// silently return a zero gradient even in a genuinely sloped region.     /// Existing near-boundary clamping in `cell_indices` means this never     /// panics, even for a `position` right at the field's edge.     pub fn gradient(&self, position: [f64; 3]) -> [f64; 3] {         let mut grad = [0.0; 3];         for axis in 0..3 {             let h = self.cell_size[axis];             if h <= 0.0 {                 continue;             }             let mut plus = position;             plus[axis] += h;             let mut minus = position;             minus[axis] -= h;             grad[axis] = (self.sample(plus) - self.sample(minus)) / (2.0 * h);         }         grad     }     /// Concentration (mass / cell volume) at `position`'s cell.     pub fn sample(&self, position: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {         self.mass[self.cell_of(position)] / self.cell_volume()     }     /// Removes up to `desired` mass from `position`'s cell, clamped to     /// what's actually available (never goes negative), and returns the     /// amount actually removed. This clamp is what makes the withdraw     /// pathway self-canceling with `deposit` under all conditions — a     /// desired withdrawal larger than the cell holds can't manufacture     /// mass from nothing.     pub fn withdraw(&mut self, position: [f64; 3], desired: f64) -> f64 {         let idx = self.cell_of(position);         let available = self.mass[idx].max(0.0);         let actual = desired.clamp(0.0, available);         self.mass[idx] -= actual;         actual     }     pub fn deposit(&mut self, position: [f64; 3], amount: f64) {         let idx = self.cell_of(position);         self.mass[idx] += amount;     }     /// One explicit finite-difference diffusion step. For every pair of     /// axis-adjacent cells, computes a single flux value and applies it     /// as `-flux` to one cell and `+flux` to the other, into a fresh     /// buffer seeded from the current state — so total mass changes by     /// exactly zero per pair, and therefore exactly zero overall,     /// independent of floating-point summation order (this is what     /// `unified_engine.rs`'s mass-conservation tests, at 1e-9 relative     /// tolerance over hundreds of ticks, actually require: not     /// approximate conservation, exact-by-construction conservation).     /// Cells on the outer edge of the grid have no diffusion partner     /// beyond the boundary, so the boundary is implicitly zero-flux     /// (reflecting) — nothing here ever adds or removes mass at the     /// domain edge.     pub fn step(&mut self, dt: f64, diffusion_coeff: f64) {         let mut new_mass = self.mass.clone();         for iz in 0..self.nz {             for iy in 0..self.ny {                 for ix in 0..self.nx {                     let here = self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz);                     if ix + 1 < self.nx {                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, self.flat_index(ix + 1, iy, iz), self.cell_size[0], dt, diffusion_coeff);                     }                     if iy + 1 < self.ny {                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, self.flat_index(ix, iy + 1, iz), self.cell_size[1], dt, diffusion_coeff);                     }                     if iz + 1 < self.nz {                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz + 1), self.cell_size[2], dt, diffusion_coeff);                     }                 }             }         }         self.mass = new_mass;     }     fn exchange_flux(&self, new_mass: &mut [f64], a: usize, b: usize, spacing: f64, dt: f64, diffusion_coeff: f64) {         let flux = diffusion_coeff * dt * (self.mass[a] - self.mass[b]) / (spacing * spacing);         new_mass[a] -= flux;         new_mass[b] += flux;     }     /// Diffusion step with a *spatially-varying* diffusion coefficient —     /// gap 4 from the physics roadmap review ("tissue isn't uniform"):     /// real tissue has compartments (cytoplasm vs. extracellular matrix     /// vs. vesicle lumen) with genuinely different diffusivities, and the     /// boundaries between them are sharp discontinuities, not smooth     /// gradients. `diffusion_coeff_per_cell` must have exactly     /// `nx * ny * nz` entries in the same flattened layout `mass` uses     /// (`ix + iy*nx + iz*nx*ny`).     ///     /// A separate method from `step`, not a generalization of it (`step`     /// keeps its exact original signature and behavior) — `step` is what     /// verbatim `unified_engine.rs` calls with a single scalar     /// `f64`; changing that signature would break verbatim compatibility.     /// `mitonano_federated` calls whichever of the two fits a given     /// node's configuration (see that crate's `lib.rs` for the call     /// site).     ///     /// **Interface diffusivity: harmonic mean, not arithmetic.** For two     /// adjacent cells with diffusivities `D_a` and `D_b`, the flux between     /// them uses `2*D_a*D_b / (D_a + D_b)`, not `(D_a + D_b) / 2`. This is     /// the standard, physically correct choice for a finite-volume scheme     /// with a discontinuous diffusivity at a cell face — it's what you get     /// from treating the two half-cells as two resistances in series     /// (like heat conduction through two different materials in contact),     /// and critically, it correctly reduces to *zero* flux when either     /// side has `D = 0` (a true impermeable barrier), which the     /// arithmetic mean would not do (it would let mass leak through a     /// zero-diffusivity cell as long as the *other* side's `D` is     /// nonzero — physically wrong, and the reason     /// `tests::step_heterogeneous_with_a_zero_diffusivity_cell_blocks_all_flux_through_it`     /// exists: it's a direct, exact check that this implementation     /// actually blocks flux at a barrier, not merely reduces it).     pub fn step_heterogeneous(&mut self, dt: f64, diffusion_coeff_per_cell: &[f64]) {         assert_eq!(             diffusion_coeff_per_cell.len(),             self.mass.len(),             "step_heterogeneous requires exactly nx*ny*nz diffusion coefficients, one per cell"         );         let mut new_mass = self.mass.clone();         for iz in 0..self.nz {             for iy in 0..self.ny {                 for ix in 0..self.nx {                     let here = self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz);                     if ix + 1 < self.nx {                         let there = self.flat_index(ix + 1, iy, iz);                         let d = harmonic_mean(diffusion_coeff_per_cell[here], diffusion_coeff_per_cell[there]);                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, there, self.cell_size[0], dt, d);                     }                     if iy + 1 < self.ny {                         let there = self.flat_index(ix, iy + 1, iz);                         let d = harmonic_mean(diffusion_coeff_per_cell[here], diffusion_coeff_per_cell[there]);                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, there, self.cell_size[1], dt, d);                     }                     if iz + 1 < self.nz {                         let there = self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz + 1);                         let d = harmonic_mean(diffusion_coeff_per_cell[here], diffusion_coeff_per_cell[there]);                         self.exchange_flux(&mut new_mass, here, there, self.cell_size[2], dt, d);                     }                 }             }         }         self.mass = new_mass;     }     pub fn total_mass(&self) -> f64 {         self.mass.iter().sum()     }     /// The cell size (dx, dy, dz) this field's grid uses — needed by     /// cross-node coupling code (`mitonano_federated`) to compute a     /// boundary flux using the *same* formula `step`'s own interior     /// diffusion uses (see `boundary_mass`'s docs).     pub fn cell_size(&self) -> [f64; 3] {         self.cell_size     }     /// The raw cell *mass* (not concentration) of every cell in the     /// `face`'s ny*nz layer (`ix = 0` for `XFace::Min`, `ix = nx - 1` for     /// `XFace::Max`), in `iy + iz * ny` order.     ///     /// Added for cross-node field coupling (`mitonano_federated`): two     /// adjacent nodes' fields can exchange mass across their shared seam     /// by extracting each side's boundary layer with this method,     /// computing a flux, and applying it with [`add_boundary_mass`].     /// Returns *mass*, not concentration, specifically so that flux can     /// be computed with the exact same formula `step`'s own interior     /// `exchange_flux` uses (`diffusion_coeff * dt * (mass_a - mass_b) /     /// spacing^2`) — this is algebraically the same as Fick's law     /// `diffusion_coeff * (c_a - c_b) / spacing * face_area` once you     /// substitute `c = mass / (face_area * spacing)` and simplify, so     /// using mass directly here keeps the cross-node correction     /// numerically consistent with — not a different formula bolted onto     /// — the interior scheme, avoiding a discontinuity in the diffusion     /// operator right at a node seam.     ///     /// [`add_boundary_mass`]: ConcentrationField::add_boundary_mass     pub fn boundary_mass(&self, face: XFace) -> Vec<f64> {         let ix = match face {             XFace::Min => 0,             XFace::Max => self.nx - 1,         };         let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(self.ny * self.nz);         for iz in 0..self.nz {             for iy in 0..self.ny {                 out.push(self.mass[self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz)]);             }         }         out     }     /// Adds `delta[iy + iz * ny]` mass directly to the `face`'s     /// corresponding cell — purely mechanical, no computation of its own.     /// Intended to be called with a flux already computed by cross-node     /// coupling code that read both sides' [`boundary_mass`] from a     /// shared pre-diffusion snapshot, so the mass added to one node's     /// face and the mass added (with the opposite sign) to its     /// neighbor's face are exact negatives of each other by construction     /// — exact conservation across the seam, the same way `step`'s own     /// interior `exchange_flux` conserves mass between any two adjacent     /// cells within one grid.     ///     /// [`boundary_mass`]: ConcentrationField::boundary_mass     pub fn add_boundary_mass(&mut self, face: XFace, delta: &[f64]) {         let ix = match face {             XFace::Min => 0,             XFace::Max => self.nx - 1,         };         for iz in 0..self.nz {             for iy in 0..self.ny {                 let idx = self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz);                 self.mass[idx] += delta[iy + iz * self.ny];             }         }     }     /// Gap 5 from the physics roadmap review: source/sink boundary     /// conditions ("real biological systems usually have continuous     /// sources — a capillary supplying fresh mass — and sinks —     /// lymphatic drainage"). Unlike `XFace` (x-only, since that's the     /// only axis `mitonano_federated`'s domain decomposition ever splits),     /// [`Face`] covers all six faces of *this field's own* grid — whether     /// a given face is actually part of the true *global* domain boundary     /// (as opposed to an internal node-to-node seam) is a     /// `mitonano_federated`-level question this method has no way to     /// answer itself; see that crate's `lib.rs` for how it's actually     /// applied only where appropriate.     ///     /// Injects `rate * dt` total mass, split evenly across every cell on     /// `face`, and returns the actual amount injected (always exactly     /// `rate * dt` for `rate >= 0` — unlike `withdraw`, there's no     /// availability limit on injection). A caller accumulating a running     /// "total injected" bookkeeping total (as `mitonano_federated` does)     /// can just sum this return value directly.     pub fn inject_at_face(&mut self, face: Face, rate: f64, dt: f64) -> f64 {         let total = rate * dt;         if total <= 0.0 {             return 0.0;         }         let indices = self.face_indices(face);         let per_cell = total / indices.len() as f64;         for idx in indices {             self.mass[idx] += per_cell;         }         total     }     /// The sink half of [`inject_at_face`]: first-order clearance at     /// `face` — each face cell loses `k_drain * mass * dt` (clamped to     /// that cell's available mass, so this can never drive a cell     /// negative, the same availability-respecting pattern `withdraw`     /// uses). Returns the actual total mass removed across every cell on     /// `face` (at most `k_drain * total_face_mass * dt`, less if any     /// individual cell's clamp bound was hit — though for the small `dt`     /// this crate is designed around, that clamp essentially never     /// engages in practice).     pub fn drain_at_face(&mut self, face: Face, k_drain: f64, dt: f64) -> f64 {         if k_drain <= 0.0 {             return 0.0;         }         let indices = self.face_indices(face);         let mut removed = 0.0;         for idx in indices {             let desired = self.mass[idx] * k_drain * dt;             let actual = desired.min(self.mass[idx]).max(0.0);             self.mass[idx] -= actual;             removed += actual;         }         removed     }     fn face_indices(&self, face: Face) -> Vec<usize> {         let mut out = Vec::new();         match face {             Face::XMin | Face::XMax => {                 let ix = if face == Face::XMin { 0 } else { self.nx - 1 };                 for iz in 0..self.nz {                     for iy in 0..self.ny {                         out.push(self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz));                     }                 }             }             Face::YMin | Face::YMax => {                 let iy = if face == Face::YMin { 0 } else { self.ny - 1 };                 for iz in 0..self.nz {                     for ix in 0..self.nx {                         out.push(self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz));                     }                 }             }             Face::ZMin | Face::ZMax => {                 let iz = if face == Face::ZMin { 0 } else { self.nz - 1 };                 for iy in 0..self.ny {                     for ix in 0..self.nx {                         out.push(self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz));                     }                 }             }         }         out     } } /// One of the six faces of a `ConcentrationField`'s own grid, for /// [`ConcentrationField::inject_at_face`] / [`ConcentrationField::drain_at_face`]. /// See those methods' own docs, and their module-level context, for why /// this is broader than [`XFace`] (which only ever needs x, since /// `mitonano_federated`'s domain decomposition only ever splits along x — /// source/sink boundary conditions, by contrast, are meaningful on any of /// the six faces of the *global* domain, which includes y and z). #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum Face {     XMin,     XMax,     YMin,     YMax,     ZMin,     ZMax, } /// The physically correct interface diffusivity for two adjacent cells /// with diffusivities `a` and `b` — see `ConcentrationField::step_heterogeneous`'s /// own docs for why harmonic (not arithmetic) mean is the right choice. /// Returns exactly `0.0` if either input is `<= 0.0` (rather than `NaN` /// from a naive `2*a*b/(a+b)` when both are zero), matching the physical /// expectation that a zero-diffusivity cell blocks flux regardless of its /// neighbor. /// /// Public (not just an internal helper) specifically so /// `mitonano_federated`'s cross-node diffusion coupling can reuse the /// exact same formula at a node-to-node seam that this module already /// uses at ordinary interior cell faces — one definition, not two that /// could silently drift apart. pub fn harmonic_mean(a: f64, b: f64) -> f64 {     if a <= 0.0 || b <= 0.0 {         return 0.0;     }     2.0 * a * b / (a + b) } /// Which x-face of a `ConcentrationField`'s grid a boundary-layer /// operation refers to. Only x, because `mitonano_federated`'s domain /// decomposition (like `mitonano_orchestrator`'s) is 1D along x — see /// `boundary_mass`'s docs for what this is for. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum XFace {     Min,     Max, } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     fn field() -> ConcentrationField {         ConcentrationField::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 5, 5, 5)     }     #[test]     fn deposit_then_sample_reflects_the_deposited_mass() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([1.0, 1.0, 1.0], 4.0);         let vol = f.cell_volume();         assert!((f.sample([1.0, 1.0, 1.0]) - 4.0 / vol).abs() < 1e-12);     }     #[test]     fn gradient_is_exactly_zero_at_the_symmetric_center_of_a_point_source() {         // c(center - h) == c(center + h) by construction (a single         // centered deposit, sampled symmetrically about that same         // center) -- so the central-difference gradient must be exactly         // zero, not just approximately: this is an exact algebraic         // consequence of the symmetry, not a discretization-tolerance         // check.         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([5.0, 5.0, 5.0], 100.0); // exact center of the [0,10]^3 domain         let grad = f.gradient([5.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         assert_eq!(grad, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]);     }     #[test]     fn gradient_points_toward_higher_concentration() {         // A wider domain than `field()`'s default, so probe points can         // sit safely interior -- away from both the peak's own cell and         // the domain boundary, where clamping in `cell_indices` would         // distort a central difference into a boundary artifact rather         // than reflecting the field's true local slope (this was the         // actual cause of this test's first version failing: a probe         // point too close to the domain edge had its "outward" neighbor         // clamp back into the peak's own cell).         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut f = ConcentrationField::new(bounds, 10, 5, 5); // cell width 2.0 in x         f.deposit([11.0, 5.0, 5.0], 100.0); // center of the cell covering [10,12)         // x=9 sits in the cell immediately left of the peak's cell;         // stepping +h reaches the peak (high concentration), stepping -h         // reaches an empty cell further left -- concentration rises         // moving toward +x here, so the gradient should be positive.         let grad_left = f.gradient([9.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         assert!(grad_left[0] > 0.0, "left of the peak, concentration should rise toward +x, got grad_x={}", grad_left[0]);         // x=13 sits in the cell immediately right of the peak's cell;         // stepping +h reaches an empty cell further right, stepping -h         // reaches the peak -- concentration falls moving toward +x here,         // so the gradient should be negative.         let grad_right = f.gradient([13.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         assert!(grad_right[0] < 0.0, "right of the peak, concentration should fall toward +x, got grad_x={}", grad_right[0]);     }     #[test]     fn gradient_is_zero_for_a_perfectly_uniform_field() {         let mut f = field();         // Deposit the same amount in every cell -> uniform concentration.         for ix in 0..f.nx {             for iy in 0..f.ny {                 for iz in 0..f.nz {                     let x = f.bounds.min[0] + (ix as f64 + 0.5) * f.cell_size[0];                     let y = f.bounds.min[1] + (iy as f64 + 0.5) * f.cell_size[1];                     let z = f.bounds.min[2] + (iz as f64 + 0.5) * f.cell_size[2];                     f.deposit([x, y, z], 10.0);                 }             }         }         let grad = f.gradient([5.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         assert_eq!(grad, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]);     }     #[test]     fn withdraw_never_takes_more_than_available() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([1.0, 1.0, 1.0], 3.0);         let actual = f.withdraw([1.0, 1.0, 1.0], 100.0);         assert_eq!(actual, 3.0);         assert_eq!(f.total_mass(), 0.0);     }     #[test]     fn withdraw_and_deposit_are_exactly_self_canceling() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([2.0, 2.0, 2.0], 10.0);         let before = f.total_mass();         let taken = f.withdraw([2.0, 2.0, 2.0], 3.5);         f.deposit([2.0, 2.0, 2.0], taken);         assert_eq!(f.total_mass(), before);     }     #[test]     fn diffusion_step_conserves_total_mass_exactly() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([1.0, 1.0, 1.0], 20.0);         f.deposit([8.0, 8.0, 8.0], 5.0);         let before = f.total_mass();         for _ in 0..50 {             f.step(0.01, 0.2);         }         // Each individual flux exchange is exact (the same value         // subtracted from one cell and added to another), but summing         // 125 cells' worth of floating-point values via `total_mass`         // after 50 rounds of that isn't perfectly associative at the         // ~1e-14 scale — hence a tight relative tolerance here rather         // than bit-exact equality, consistent with the tolerance         // `unified_engine.rs`'s own tests use for the same kind of         // check (1e-9 relative error).         let rel_err = (f.total_mass() - before).abs() / before;         assert!(rel_err < 1e-9, "diffusion must conserve total mass: before={before} after={} rel_err={rel_err}", f.total_mass());     }     #[test]     fn diffusion_step_spreads_mass_toward_neighbors() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([5.0, 5.0, 5.0], 100.0);         let center_before = f.sample([5.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         f.step(0.01, 0.5);         let center_after = f.sample([5.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         assert!(center_after < center_before, "a concentrated deposit should lose concentration to its neighbors after diffusing");     }     #[test]     fn cell_indices_clamp_for_a_point_exactly_on_the_outer_max_edge() {         let f = field();         // Should not panic or index out of bounds.         let _ = f.sample([10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);     }     #[test]     fn boundary_mass_extracts_the_correct_layer() {         let mut f = field(); // 5x5x5 grid over [0,10]^3         f.deposit([0.5, 5.0, 5.0], 7.0); // ix=0 -> the Min-x layer         f.deposit([9.5, 5.0, 5.0], 3.0); // ix=4 -> the Max-x layer         let min_layer = f.boundary_mass(XFace::Min);         let max_layer = f.boundary_mass(XFace::Max);         assert_eq!(min_layer.len(), 5 * 5);         assert_eq!(max_layer.len(), 5 * 5);         assert!((min_layer.iter().sum::<f64>() - 7.0).abs() < 1e-12);         assert!((max_layer.iter().sum::<f64>() - 3.0).abs() < 1e-12);     }     #[test]     fn add_boundary_mass_adds_to_the_correct_layer_only() {         let mut f = field();         let delta = vec![1.0; 25];         f.add_boundary_mass(XFace::Min, &delta);         assert!((f.boundary_mass(XFace::Min).iter().sum::<f64>() - 25.0).abs() < 1e-12);         assert!((f.boundary_mass(XFace::Max).iter().sum::<f64>() - 0.0).abs() < 1e-12, "Max layer must be untouched");         assert!((f.total_mass() - 25.0).abs() < 1e-12);     }     /// The actual point of `boundary_mass`/`add_boundary_mass`: two     /// separate `ConcentrationField`s, representing adjacent nodes, can     /// exchange mass across their shared seam using the exact same     /// mass/spacing^2 flux formula `step`'s own interior diffusion uses,     /// and that exchange conserves the *combined* total mass of both     /// fields exactly — proving the mechanism these two methods exist to     /// support is actually conservative before `mitonano_federated` ever     /// uses it.     #[test]     fn cross_field_boundary_exchange_conserves_combined_mass_exactly() {         let bounds_a = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let bounds_b = Bounds::new([10.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut field_a = ConcentrationField::new(bounds_a, 5, 5, 5);         let mut field_b = ConcentrationField::new(bounds_b, 5, 5, 5);         field_a.deposit([9.5, 5.0, 5.0], 40.0); // right up against the seam         field_b.deposit([10.5, 5.0, 5.0], 5.0); // right up against the seam, other side         let combined_before = field_a.total_mass() + field_b.total_mass();         // Mirrors exactly what mitonano_federated's step() does: snapshot         // both sides' boundary mass (pre-exchange), compute one flux per         // cell using the interior formula, apply with opposite signs.         let mass_a = field_a.boundary_mass(XFace::Max);         let mass_b = field_b.boundary_mass(XFace::Min);         let spacing = field_a.cell_size()[0];         let d = 0.3;         let dt = 0.01;         let flux: Vec<f64> = mass_a.iter().zip(&mass_b).map(|(&ma, &mb)| d * dt * (ma - mb) / (spacing * spacing)).collect();         let neg_flux: Vec<f64> = flux.iter().map(|f| -f).collect();         field_a.add_boundary_mass(XFace::Max, &neg_flux);         field_b.add_boundary_mass(XFace::Min, &flux);         let combined_after = field_a.total_mass() + field_b.total_mass();         assert!(             (combined_after - combined_before).abs() < 1e-12,             "cross-field boundary exchange must conserve combined mass exactly: before={combined_before} after={combined_after}"         );         assert!(field_a.total_mass() < 40.0, "the higher-mass side should lose mass across the seam");         assert!(field_b.total_mass() > 5.0, "the lower-mass side should gain mass across the seam");     }     /// `step_heterogeneous` with a *uniform* per-cell coefficient array     /// must produce exactly the same result as ordinary `step` with the     /// same scalar value — the harmonic mean of two equal values is that     /// value itself, so this is a direct regression check that the more     /// general method reduces correctly to the simpler one in the     /// degenerate uniform case, not a divergent reimplementation.     #[test]     fn step_heterogeneous_with_uniform_coefficients_matches_ordinary_step_exactly() {         let mut f1 = field();         let mut f2 = field();         f1.deposit([3.0, 5.0, 5.0], 50.0);         f2.deposit([3.0, 5.0, 5.0], 50.0);         let d = 0.25;         let dt = 0.01;         let uniform = vec![d; 5 * 5 * 5];         for _ in 0..20 {             f1.step(dt, d);             f2.step_heterogeneous(dt, &uniform);         }         assert!((f1.total_mass() - f2.total_mass()).abs() < 1e-9);         // Spot-check a specific cell's concentration matches exactly, not         // just the aggregate total.         assert!((f1.sample([3.0, 5.0, 5.0]) - f2.sample([3.0, 5.0, 5.0])).abs() < 1e-9);         assert!((f1.sample([4.5, 5.0, 5.0]) - f2.sample([4.5, 5.0, 5.0])).abs() < 1e-9);     }     /// The direct, exact test backing the choice of harmonic (not     /// arithmetic) mean: a single cell with `D = 0.0` must block *all*     /// flux through it, in both directions, even though its neighbors on     /// both sides have large, equal, nonzero diffusivity. Mass deposited     /// on one side of the barrier must never reach the other side, over     /// many ticks — not merely "less than it would without the barrier,"     /// exactly zero.     #[test]     fn step_heterogeneous_with_a_zero_diffusivity_cell_blocks_all_flux_through_it() {         // 1D-reduced field (ny=nz=1) for a clean, unambiguous "left of         // barrier" / "right of barrier" split.         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [11.0, 1.0, 1.0]);         let mut f = ConcentrationField::new(bounds, 11, 1, 1); // cells at x=0.5,1.5,...,10.5; barrier at cell index 5 (x=5.5)         f.deposit([2.5, 0.5, 0.5], 100.0); // well to the left of the barrier         let mut d = vec![0.5; 11];         d[5] = 0.0; // the barrier cell         for _ in 0..500 {             f.step_heterogeneous(0.01, &d);         }         // Mass on the right side of the barrier (cells 6..11) must be         // exactly zero -- not diminished, exactly zero, since the         // barrier's harmonic-mean interface diffusivity is exactly 0.0         // on both faces touching it.         let right_side_mass: f64 = (6..11).map(|ix| f.sample([ix as f64 + 0.5, 0.5, 0.5]) * f.cell_size()[0]).sum();         assert_eq!(right_side_mass, 0.0, "a zero-diffusivity barrier must block all flux through it exactly, not just reduce it");         // The barrier cell itself must also have gained nothing.         assert_eq!(f.sample([5.5, 0.5, 0.5]), 0.0);         // Total mass still exactly conserved (redistributed among the         // left-side cells only).         assert!((f.total_mass() - 100.0).abs() < 1e-9);     }     #[test]     fn step_heterogeneous_rejects_a_mismatched_coefficient_array_length() {         let mut f = field();         let wrong_size = vec![0.1; 10]; // field() is 5x5x5=125 cells         let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| f.step_heterogeneous(0.01, &wrong_size)));         assert!(result.is_err(), "step_heterogeneous must reject a coefficient array of the wrong length rather than silently misreading the grid");     }     #[test]     fn inject_at_face_adds_exactly_rate_times_dt_split_evenly() {         let mut f = field(); // 5x5x5         let mass_before = f.total_mass();         let injected = f.inject_at_face(Face::YMax, 10.0, 0.5); // rate=10, dt=0.5 -> 5.0 total         assert_eq!(injected, 5.0);         assert!((f.total_mass() - mass_before - 5.0).abs() < 1e-12);         // Split evenly across the 5*5=25 cells on the YMax face.         let per_cell_expected = 5.0 / 25.0;         let sample_x = f.cell_size()[0] * 0.5; // center of the first x-cell         let y_max_edge = f.bounds.max[1] - f.cell_size()[1] * 0.5;         let c = f.sample([sample_x, y_max_edge, f.cell_size()[2] * 0.5]);         assert!((c * f.cell_size()[0] * f.cell_size()[1] * f.cell_size()[2] - per_cell_expected).abs() < 1e-9);     }     #[test]     fn inject_at_face_only_affects_the_targeted_face() {         let mut f = field();         f.inject_at_face(Face::ZMin, 100.0, 1.0);         // XMin/XMax faces should be untouched (their cells overlap ZMin         // only at the very edge, but bulk of those faces' mass should be zero).         let x_max_face_mass: f64 = {             let cs = f.cell_size();             let mut total = 0.0;             for iy in 0..5 {                 for iz in 1..5 {                     // deliberately skip iz=0 (the ZMin layer, which does legitimately overlap XMax at iz=0)                     let x = f.bounds.max[0] - cs[0] * 0.5;                     let y = f.bounds.min[1] + cs[1] * (iy as f64 + 0.5);                     let z = f.bounds.min[2] + cs[2] * (iz as f64 + 0.5);                     total += f.sample([x, y, z]) * cs[0] * cs[1] * cs[2];                 }             }             total         };         assert_eq!(x_max_face_mass, 0.0, "injection at ZMin must not add mass to XMax cells outside the ZMin layer itself");     }     #[test]     fn drain_at_face_removes_exactly_the_requested_fraction_when_available() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([f.cell_size()[0] * 0.5, f.cell_size()[1] * 0.5, f.cell_size()[2] * 0.5], 100.0); // in the XMin,YMin,ZMin corner cell         let mass_before = f.total_mass();         let removed = f.drain_at_face(Face::XMin, 0.1, 1.0); // k_drain=0.1, dt=1.0 -> 10% of face mass         assert!((removed - mass_before * 0.1).abs() < 1e-9);         assert!((f.total_mass() - (mass_before - removed)).abs() < 1e-9);     }     #[test]     fn drain_at_face_never_removes_more_than_available_in_a_cell() {         let mut f = field();         f.deposit([f.cell_size()[0] * 0.5, f.cell_size()[1] * 0.5, f.cell_size()[2] * 0.5], 1.0);         // k_drain*dt=100.0 would ask to remove 100x the available mass --         // must clamp to what's actually there, never go negative.         let removed = f.drain_at_face(Face::XMin, 100.0, 1.0);         assert!(removed <= 1.0 + 1e-9);         assert!(f.total_mass() >= -1e-12, "total mass must never go negative");     }     #[test]     fn inject_and_drain_are_no_ops_for_nonpositive_rates() {         let mut f = field();         assert_eq!(f.inject_at_face(Face::XMax, 0.0, 1.0), 0.0);         assert_eq!(f.inject_at_face(Face::XMax, -5.0, 1.0), 0.0);         assert_eq!(f.drain_at_face(Face::XMax, 0.0, 1.0), 0.0);         assert_eq!(f.total_mass(), 0.0);     } } ``` ## `src/orchestration.rs` ```rust //! Uniform-grid spatial index: buckets agent indices by cell so //! radius-limited neighbor queries don't need an O(n^2) scan. //! //! `unified_engine.rs`'s own test suite (verbatim) includes the strongest //! constraint this module has to satisfy: //! `spatial_index_matches_brute_force_with_full_feedback_active`, which //! runs 200 ticks of the *entire* engine (motion, uptake, release, //! elimination, and agent-agent exchange all simultaneously active) twice //! — once routing interaction through this index, once through //! `interaction::step_agent_interactions_brute_force`'s direct O(n^2) //! distance check — and requires agent payloads and field mass to match //! to 1e-9 at every tick. That test only passes if this index's neighbor //! queries return *exactly* the same candidate set brute force would, //! every tick, including as agents move across cell boundaries between //! rebuilds. use crate::agent::Agent; use crate::environment::Bounds; pub struct SpatialIndex {     pub bounds: Bounds,     pub nx: usize,     pub ny: usize,     pub nz: usize,     cell_size: [f64; 3],     buckets: Vec<Vec<usize>>, } impl SpatialIndex {     pub fn new(bounds: Bounds, nx: usize, ny: usize, nz: usize) -> Self {         assert!(nx > 0 && ny > 0 && nz > 0, "SpatialIndex grid dimensions must be positive");         let cell_size = [bounds.width(0) / nx as f64, bounds.width(1) / ny as f64, bounds.width(2) / nz as f64];         SpatialIndex { bounds, nx, ny, nz, cell_size, buckets: vec![Vec::new(); nx * ny * nz] }     }     fn cell_indices(&self, position: [f64; 3]) -> (usize, usize, usize) {         let idx = |p: f64, lo: f64, size: f64, n: usize| -> usize {             if size <= 0.0 {                 return 0;             }             (((p - lo) / size).floor() as isize).clamp(0, n as isize - 1) as usize         };         (             idx(position[0], self.bounds.min[0], self.cell_size[0], self.nx),             idx(position[1], self.bounds.min[1], self.cell_size[1], self.ny),             idx(position[2], self.bounds.min[2], self.cell_size[2], self.nz),         )     }     fn flat_index(&self, ix: usize, iy: usize, iz: usize) -> usize {         ix + iy * self.nx + iz * self.nx * self.ny     }     /// Re-buckets every agent index by its current position. Must be     /// called after agents move and before any neighbor query for that     /// tick — `unified_engine.rs` (verbatim) always calls this     /// immediately before `step_agent_interactions`, every tick.     pub fn rebuild(&mut self, agents: &[Agent]) {         for bucket in &mut self.buckets {             bucket.clear();         }         for (i, agent) in agents.iter().enumerate() {             let (ix, iy, iz) = self.cell_indices(agent.position);             let idx = self.flat_index(ix, iy, iz);             self.buckets[idx].push(i);         }     }     /// Every agent index (from `agents`) within `radius` of `position`,     /// found by scanning only the cells that could possibly contain such     /// an agent (a padded block of cells around `position`'s own cell —     /// wide enough that no in-radius agent living outside it is possible,     /// erring generous rather than tight), then filtering by exact     /// distance. May include `position`'s own agent if it lies at     /// distance 0 from itself — callers are responsible for     /// self-exclusion (see `interaction.rs`, which excludes by index).     pub fn neighbors_within(&self, agents: &[Agent], position: [f64; 3], radius: f64) -> Vec<usize> {         let (cx, cy, cz) = self.cell_indices(position);         let cell_radius = |size: f64| -> isize {             if size <= 0.0 {                 0             } else {                 (radius / size).ceil() as isize + 1             }         };         let (rx, ry, rz) = (cell_radius(self.cell_size[0]), cell_radius(self.cell_size[1]), cell_radius(self.cell_size[2]));         let radius_sq = radius * radius;         let mut result = Vec::new();         for dz in -rz..=rz {             let iz = cz as isize + dz;             if iz < 0 || iz >= self.nz as isize {                 continue;             }             for dy in -ry..=ry {                 let iy = cy as isize + dy;                 if iy < 0 || iy >= self.ny as isize {                     continue;                 }                 for dx in -rx..=rx {                     let ix = cx as isize + dx;                     if ix < 0 || ix >= self.nx as isize {                         continue;                     }                     for &j in &self.buckets[self.flat_index(ix as usize, iy as usize, iz as usize)] {                         if squared_distance(agents[j].position, position) <= radius_sq {                             result.push(j);                         }                     }                 }             }         }         result     } } fn squared_distance(a: [f64; 3], b: [f64; 3]) -> f64 {     let dx = a[0] - b[0];     let dy = a[1] - b[1];     let dz = a[2] - b[2];     dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     fn make_agents(positions: &[[f64; 3]]) -> Vec<Agent> {         positions.iter().map(|&p| Agent::new(p, 1.0)).collect()     }     #[test]     fn neighbors_within_finds_agents_inside_radius_and_excludes_those_outside() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([-10.0, -10.0, -10.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let mut index = SpatialIndex::new(bounds, 5, 5, 5);         let agents = make_agents(&[[0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [0.5, 0.0, 0.0], [5.0, 5.0, 5.0]]);         index.rebuild(&agents);         let found = index.neighbors_within(&agents, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         assert!(found.contains(&0), "self should be found at distance 0");         assert!(found.contains(&1), "agent at distance 0.5 should be within radius 1.0");         assert!(!found.contains(&2), "agent far away should not be found");     }     /// Regression coverage for the exact property the T2/T3 crates'     /// `spatial_index_matches_brute_force` tests rely on: an index-based     /// query must find every agent brute force would, even ones sitting     /// in a *different* grid cell right at the query cell's edge.     #[test]     fn neighbors_within_matches_brute_force_across_many_random_configurations() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([-10.0, -10.0, -10.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);
