This holds as a /// direct consequence of mass conservation plus the diffusion equation's /// structure — it does *not* require the discretized delta-function to /// have already relaxed into anything resembling a Gaussian *shape* (see /// the next test for a shape check, which does need that), which is why /// this is checked from a fairly early time onward. #[test] fn point_source_diffusion_variance_matches_the_closed_form_2dt_prediction() {     let d = 0.2;     let global = Bounds::new([-50.0, -1.0, -1.0], [50.0, 1.0, 1.0]);     let nx = 201;     let cell_width = global.width(0) / nx as f64;     let mass = 1000.0;     let dt = 0.02;     let variance_of = |field: &ConcentrationField| -> f64 {         // Discrete second moment about the origin. The mean is exactly 0         // by construction (deposit was exactly centered, and both the         // grid and the diffusion scheme are symmetric under x -> -x), so         // this second moment about 0 *is* the variance directly, with no         // separate mean-subtraction step needed.         let mut weighted = 0.0;         let mut total = 0.0;         for i in 0..nx {             let x = global.min[0] + (i as f64 + 0.5) * cell_width;             let m = field.sample([x, 0.0, 0.0]) * cell_width; // cell_volume == cell_width * 1 * 1             weighted += x * x * m;             total += m;         }         weighted / total     };     for &n_steps in &[100usize, 200, 400] {         let mut field = ConcentrationField::new(global, nx, 1, 1);         field.deposit([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], mass);         for _ in 0..n_steps {             field.step(dt, d);         }         let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;         let expected_var = 2.0 * d * t;         let observed_var = variance_of(&field);         let rel_err = (observed_var - expected_var).abs() / expected_var;         assert!(             rel_err < 0.05,             "t={t}: observed variance={observed_var} vs closed-form 2*D*t={expected_var} (rel_err={rel_err})"         );     } } /// Complements the variance test: at a time late enough for the /// discretized delta-function to have relaxed into the continuum /// solution's actual *shape* (sigma spanning several cell widths, so the /// grid resolves the curve rather than just its second moment), the /// profile itself should match a Gaussian: `c(x) / c(0) == exp(-x^2 / /// (2*sigma^2))` at `x = sigma` and `x = 2*sigma`. #[test] fn point_source_diffusion_profile_matches_the_gaussian_shape_once_relaxed() {     let d = 0.2;     let global = Bounds::new([-50.0, -1.0, -1.0], [50.0, 1.0, 1.0]);     // nx=201 (matching the variance test above) turned out to be too     // coarse for a *shape* comparison specifically: sampling at exactly     // one cell width can land far enough from the true continuum point     // to introduce >10% error even though the aggregate variance (which     // integrates over the whole profile, averaging out local sampling     // error) still matches closed-form well. Doubling resolution to     // nx=401 and extending to t=16 (empirically checked before choosing     // these numbers, not guessed) brings pointwise error down to ~1-6%.     let mut field = ConcentrationField::new(global, 401, 1, 1);     let mass = 1000.0;     field.deposit([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], mass);     let dt = 0.02;     let n_steps = 800; // t=16.0 -> sigma=sqrt(2*0.2*16)~2.530, resolved by ~0.2494-wide cells     for _ in 0..n_steps {         field.step(dt, d);     }     let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;     let sigma = (2.0 * d * t).sqrt();     let c0 = field.sample([0.0, 0.0, 0.0]);     let c_sigma = field.sample([sigma, 0.0, 0.0]);     let c_2sigma = field.sample([2.0 * sigma, 0.0, 0.0]);     let ratio_sigma = c_sigma / c0;     let ratio_2sigma = c_2sigma / c0;     let expected_ratio_sigma = (-0.5f64).exp(); // ~0.6065     let expected_ratio_2sigma = (-2.0f64).exp(); // ~0.1353     assert!(         (ratio_sigma - expected_ratio_sigma).abs() / expected_ratio_sigma < 0.08,         "c(sigma)/c(0)={ratio_sigma} vs Gaussian exp(-0.5)={expected_ratio_sigma}"     );     assert!(         (ratio_2sigma - expected_ratio_2sigma).abs() / expected_ratio_2sigma < 0.15,         "c(2*sigma)/c(0)={ratio_2sigma} vs Gaussian exp(-2)={expected_ratio_2sigma}"     ); } /// Closes the fission/fusion closed-form gap: with every existing agent /// independently rolling `event_fires` each tick (no fusion, no death), /// the resulting population is exactly a Yule (pure birth) process, whose /// *expected* count at time `T` has the well-known closed form /// `N(t) = N(0) * exp(rate * T)` — the same exponential-growth law used /// throughout population biology and radioactive-decay-in-reverse alike. /// This directly drives `mitonano_core::population::event_fires` itself /// (not `mitonano_federated`'s population management), so it validates /// the underlying stochastic primitive in isolation, before any /// id-assignment or spatial bookkeeping gets involved. #[test] fn fission_population_count_matches_the_yule_process_closed_form_growth() {     let rate = 0.5;     let dt = 0.05;     let n_steps = 40; // T = 2.0     let n0: u64 = 3000; // large enough for the law of large numbers to keep relative fluctuation small     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(778899);     let mut count = n0;     for _ in 0..n_steps {         let current = count;         let mut births = 0u64;         for _ in 0..current {             if event_fires(rate, dt, &mut rng) {                 births += 1;             }         }         count += births;     }     let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;     let expected = n0 as f64 * (rate * t).exp();     let rel_err = (count as f64 - expected).abs() / expected;     assert!(rel_err < 0.1, "count={count} vs closed-form N0*exp(rate*T)={expected} (rel_err={rel_err})"); } /// Complements the fission growth test with the fusion analogue: for two /// agents permanently within fusion range (a controlled, isolated pair, /// deliberately not a whole population, to get a clean closed form), the /// time until they first fuse is exactly Exponentially distributed with /// rate `rate` (the memoryless property of a Poisson process applied to a /// single pair rather than a growing population) — mean `1/rate`. Uses a /// small `dt` relative to `1/rate` so the (real, calculable, but here /// deliberately made negligible) discretization bias between "checked /// only at tick boundaries" and "a truly continuous process" stays well /// under the statistical tolerance. #[test] fn fusion_time_to_first_event_matches_the_exponential_distribution_mean() {     let rate = 0.3;     let dt = 0.01; // small relative to 1/rate ~ 3.33, keeping discretization bias negligible     let max_steps = 5000; // generous upper bound (T=50, vastly longer than the ~3.33 expected mean)     let n_trials = 4000;     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(112233);     let mut total_time = 0.0;     for _ in 0..n_trials {         let mut steps = 0u32;         loop {             steps += 1;             if event_fires(rate, dt, &mut rng) || steps as usize >= max_steps {                 break;             }         }         total_time += steps as f64 * dt;     }     let observed_mean = total_time / n_trials as f64;     let expected_mean = 1.0 / rate;     let rel_err = (observed_mean - expected_mean).abs() / expected_mean;     assert!(rel_err < 0.05, "observed mean time-to-fusion={observed_mean} vs closed-form 1/rate={expected_mean} (rel_err={rel_err})"); } /// Closes the two-state (mobile/bound) kinetics closed-form gap — see /// `mitonano_core::mobility`'s module docs for the full derivation. When /// every agent's initial mobility state is drawn from the two-state /// Markov chain's *stationary* distribution (via `sample_stationary_state`) /// rather than starting uniformly `Mobile`, the ensemble mean-squared /// displacement satisfies `MSD(T) = diffusion_coeff^2 * p_mobile_eq * T` /// **exactly, at every `T`** — not just as a long-time asymptote. Checked /// at three different elapsed times, the same structure as the ordinary /// free-diffusion MSD test, confirming both the correct scale (the /// `p_mobile_eq` reduction factor) and the correct linear-in-time growth. #[test] fn two_state_ensemble_msd_matches_the_exact_closed_form_when_initialized_at_stationarity() {     let diffusion_coeff = 0.6;     let two_state = TwoStateParams { k_on: 0.4, k_off: 0.6 }; // p_mobile_eq = 0.6     let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };     let bounds = Bounds::new([-1.0e6, -1.0e6, -1.0e6], [1.0e6, 1.0e6, 1.0e6]);     let dt = 0.01;     let n_replicates = 8000;     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(31415926);     for &n_steps in &[50usize, 100, 200] {         let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;         let mut sum_sq = 0.0f64;         for _ in 0..n_replicates {             let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             agent.mobility = sample_stationary_state(&two_state, &mut rng);             for _ in 0..n_steps {                 agent.mobility = maybe_toggle_mobility(agent.mobility, &two_state, dt, &mut rng);                 agent.advance_position(&params, &bounds, dt, &mut rng);             }             sum_sq += agent.position[0] * agent.position[0];         }         let msd = sum_sq / n_replicates as f64;         let expected_msd = diffusion_coeff * diffusion_coeff * two_state.p_mobile_equilibrium() * t;         let rel_err = (msd - expected_msd).abs() / expected_msd;         assert!(             rel_err < 0.12,             "T={t}: MSD={msd} vs closed-form diffusion_coeff^2*p_mobile_eq*T={expected_msd} (rel_err={rel_err})"         );     } } /// Complements the exact stationary-state test with the qualitative /// "crossover" behavior the trapping literature usually describes: /// starting every agent `Mobile` (a non-stationary initial condition), /// the *short-time* effective diffusion rate (`MSD(T)/T`) should be /// measurably higher than the *long-time* rate, since no agent has had /// time to bind yet at short T, while by long T the population has /// relaxed toward the reduced stationary-state rate /// `diffusion_coeff^2 * p_mobile_eq` this file's other two-state test /// checks exactly. There's no single exact closed form covering every T /// in this non-stationary case, so this checks the qualitative ordering /// and confirms the long-time rate specifically approaches the known /// stationary asymptote. #[test] fn two_state_starting_all_mobile_shows_higher_short_time_than_long_time_effective_diffusion() {     let diffusion_coeff = 0.6;     let two_state = TwoStateParams { k_on: 1.5, k_off: 1.5 }; // p_mobile_eq = 0.5, fast relaxation (1/(k_on+k_off)=0.33)     let params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };     let bounds = Bounds::new([-1.0e6, -1.0e6, -1.0e6], [1.0e6, 1.0e6, 1.0e6]);     let dt = 0.01;     let n_replicates = 8000;     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(2718281);     let msd_at = |n_steps: usize, rng: &mut StdRng| -> f64 {         let mut sum_sq = 0.0f64;         for _ in 0..n_replicates {             let mut agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             agent.mobility = MobilityState::Mobile; // deliberately non-stationary start             for _ in 0..n_steps {                 agent.mobility = maybe_toggle_mobility(agent.mobility, &two_state, dt, rng);                 agent.advance_position(&params, &bounds, dt, rng);             }             sum_sq += agent.position[0] * agent.position[0];         }         sum_sq / n_replicates as f64     };     let short_n_steps = 5; // T=0.05, well under 1/(k_on+k_off)=0.33 -- most agents haven't switched yet     let long_n_steps = 800; // T=8.0, vastly longer than the relaxation time -- population well-relaxed to stationarity     let short_msd = msd_at(short_n_steps, &mut rng);     let long_msd = msd_at(long_n_steps, &mut rng);     let short_rate = short_msd / (short_n_steps as f64 * dt);     let long_rate = long_msd / (long_n_steps as f64 * dt);     assert!(         short_rate > long_rate,         "short-time effective diffusion rate ({short_rate}) should exceed the long-time rate ({long_rate}) -- the crossover this module's docs describe"     );     // The long-time rate should have relaxed close to the known     // stationary asymptote diffusion_coeff^2 * p_mobile_eq.     let expected_long_rate = diffusion_coeff * diffusion_coeff * two_state.p_mobile_equilibrium();     let rel_err = (long_rate - expected_long_rate).abs() / expected_long_rate;     assert!(rel_err < 0.12, "long-time rate={long_rate} vs stationary asymptote={expected_long_rate} (rel_err={rel_err})");     // The short-time rate should be close to the *unreduced* rate     // (diffusion_coeff^2), since at T=0.05 with k_on=1.5 the expected     // fraction of agents that have switched at all is tiny.     assert!(         short_rate > 0.85 * diffusion_coeff * diffusion_coeff,         "short-time rate ({short_rate}) should still be close to the full unreduced diffusion_coeff^2={} before much switching has happened",         diffusion_coeff * diffusion_coeff     ); } /// Closes the heterogeneous-population closed-form gap: two agents with /// deliberately different phenotypes — resolved via /// `Agent::effective_params` rather than each just being handed different /// `AgentParams` directly, so this exercises the actual resolution /// mechanism `mitonano_federated` uses, not just two independent /// simulations that happen to use different parameters — should each /// decay at *their own* independent exponential rate simultaneously, not /// some averaged or otherwise coupled rate. Since `decay_and_release` /// already uses the exact closed-form-consistent per-step formula /// `payload * (1 - exp(-rate*dt))` (confirmed by inspection, not just /// assumed), this is a deterministic single-trajectory comparison, not a /// Monte Carlo one — no statistical tolerance needed, just floating-point /// precision. #[test] fn heterogeneous_phenotypes_decay_at_their_own_independent_exact_rates() {     let fallback = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.02, k_release: 0.03 }; // total rate 0.05     let healthy = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100.0); // no phenotype override -> uses fallback (rate 0.05)     let mut damaged = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100.0);     damaged.phenotype = Some(AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.25, k_release: 0.05 }); // total rate 0.30, much faster clearance     let dt = 0.01;     let n_steps = 300;     let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;     let mut healthy = healthy;     let mut damaged = damaged;     for _ in 0..n_steps {         let healthy_params = healthy.effective_params(&fallback);         healthy.decay_and_release(&healthy_params, dt);         let damaged_params = damaged.effective_params(&fallback);         damaged.decay_and_release(&damaged_params, dt);     }     let expected_healthy = 100.0 * (-0.05 * t).exp();     let expected_damaged = 100.0 * (-0.30 * t).exp();     assert!(         (healthy.payload - expected_healthy).abs() / expected_healthy < 1e-9,         "healthy (fallback rate 0.05): payload={} vs closed-form={}",         healthy.payload,         expected_healthy     );     assert!(         (damaged.payload - expected_damaged).abs() / expected_damaged < 1e-9,         "damaged (override rate 0.30): payload={} vs closed-form={}",         damaged.payload,         expected_damaged     );     // The two must have visibly diverged, not coincidentally matched --     // confirms the override is actually doing something, not silently     // falling back to the same rate for both.     assert!(healthy.payload > damaged.payload * 2.0, "the damaged phenotype's much faster clearance should have visibly outpaced the healthy one"); } /// The motion analogue: two subpopulations with different `diffusion_coeff` /// (resolved the same way, via `effective_params`) should each show MSD /// growing at *their own* rate simultaneously in one mixed population — /// Monte Carlo this time, since motion is stochastic, using the same /// closed form (`MSD(T) = diffusion_coeff^2 * T`) this file's ordinary /// free-diffusion test already validates, now checked for two different /// rates coexisting in the same run rather than assumed to generalize. #[test] fn heterogeneous_phenotypes_diffuse_at_their_own_independent_rates() {     let fallback = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.3, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };     let fast_phenotype = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.9, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };     let bounds = Bounds::new([-1.0e6, -1.0e6, -1.0e6], [1.0e6, 1.0e6, 1.0e6]);     let dt = 0.01;     let n_steps = 150;     let t = n_steps as f64 * dt;     let n_replicates = 8000;     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(864299);     let mut sum_sq_default = 0.0f64;     let mut sum_sq_fast = 0.0f64;     for _ in 0..n_replicates {         let mut default_agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0); // no override -> uses fallback         let mut fast_agent = Agent::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         fast_agent.phenotype = Some(fast_phenotype);         for _ in 0..n_steps {             let p1 = default_agent.effective_params(&fallback);             default_agent.advance_position(&p1, &bounds, dt, &mut rng);             let p2 = fast_agent.effective_params(&fallback);             fast_agent.advance_position(&p2, &bounds, dt, &mut rng);         }         sum_sq_default += default_agent.position[0] * default_agent.position[0];         sum_sq_fast += fast_agent.position[0] * fast_agent.position[0];     }     let msd_default = sum_sq_default / n_replicates as f64;     let msd_fast = sum_sq_fast / n_replicates as f64;     let expected_default = fallback.diffusion_coeff * fallback.diffusion_coeff * t;     let expected_fast = fast_phenotype.diffusion_coeff * fast_phenotype.diffusion_coeff * t;     assert!(         (msd_default - expected_default).abs() / expected_default < 0.1,         "default phenotype: MSD={msd_default} vs closed-form={expected_default}"     );     assert!(         (msd_fast - expected_fast).abs() / expected_fast < 0.1,         "fast phenotype: MSD={msd_fast} vs closed-form={expected_fast}"     );     assert!(msd_fast > msd_default * 2.0, "the fast phenotype's 3x diffusion_coeff should produce visibly (~9x) more MSD than the default"); } ``` # mitonano_orchestrator (unchanged) ## `Cargo.toml` ```toml [package] name = "mitonano_orchestrator" version = "0.1.0" edition = "2021" [dependencies] nalgebra = "0.32" ``` ## `src/lib.rs` ```rust //! # mitonano_orchestrator //! //! A tested, compiling implementation of the federated domain-decomposition //! orchestrator described in the uploaded source material //! (`Federated_Orchestrator_Unvalidated.txt`) — validated and, in several //! places, fixed. //! //! ## What was wrong with the source material //! //! The material did not compile, for multiple independent reasons: //! //! 1. `node.rs`'s `handle_local_agent_movement` had //!    `let target_node = /* find neighbor via decomposition */;` — a //!    comment where an expression was required. Not valid Rust. //! 2. `lib.rs` declared `pub mod domain; pub mod node; pub mod coordinator; //!    pub mod comm; pub mod migration;` but `node.rs` and `domain.rs` //!    both `use crate::agent::Agent` / `crate::engine::SimulationEngine` / //!    `crate::environment::Bounds` — modules never declared in `lib.rs`. //!    Unresolved-module errors. //! 3. `SubDomain::bounds.contains_with_halo(...)` was called but never //!    defined anywhere. //! 4. `SimulationEngine::new_default()` was called but the type was never //!    defined anywhere. //! 5. `coordinator.rs`'s `handle_migrations` had //!    `for node in nodes { ... nodes.get_mut(target) ... }` — two //!    overlapping mutable borrows of the same slice. A genuine //!    borrow-checker violation, not a style issue. //! //! Beyond the compile errors, two logic bugs were present even in the //! *intended* behavior: //! //! 6. `MitoNode` had no way to actually answer "which node does this //!    point belong to now" — the very thing bug #1's comment gestured at //!    — because it held no reference to the domain decomposition at all. //! 7. The comment `// Remove migrated agents` sat above code that never //!    removed them, meaning a migrating agent would end up duplicated: //!    present in both the source node's list and the target node's list. //! //! All seven are fixed in this crate — see `node.rs` and `coordinator.rs`'s //! module docs for the specifics of each fix, and `tests/migration.rs` for //! end-to-end validation that migration actually conserves agents and //! routes them correctly across a multi-node cluster. //! //! ## Scope, stated plainly //! //! This validates and fixes the **orchestration logic**: domain //! decomposition, per-node stepping, migration, and load-imbalance //! detection. It does **not** reproduce the full `mitonano_core` //! simulation engine (SDE motion, PK decay, field diffusion, agent-agent //! interaction) — `Agent` and `LocalEngine` here are deliberately minimal //! stand-ins (see `agent.rs`'s module docs) sufficient to drive and test //! the orchestration logic honestly. It also does **not** implement actual //! network/process-level distribution (`comm.rs`, using something like //! `crossbeam-channel` or `mpi`) or field halo exchange — both are called //! out as explicit future work in the source material's own "Next //! Immediate Steps" list, and remain so here (see `coordinator.rs`'s //! `exchange_boundaries` docs). Everything in this crate currently runs //! single-process, in-memory, sequentially across nodes — "federated" in //! architecture/data-partitioning only, not yet in actual deployment. //! //! ## T3 revision //! //! Driven by the "Next Steps & Improvements (T3 Direction)" document. //! Both gaps documented above (and previously left open in //! `coordinator.rs`) are now fixed rather than merely detected — see //! `coordinator.rs`'s module docs for the T2 to T3 changelog in full, //! including which further T3-document suggestions (performance //! optimization, dynamic node count, fault tolerance, workspace //! restructuring, etc.) were deliberately deferred rather than attempted //! and left half-validated. pub mod agent; pub mod coordinator; pub mod domain; pub mod environment; pub mod migration; pub mod node; use nalgebra::Vector3; use crate::coordinator::{ClusterStats, CoordinatorConfig, OrchestratorCoordinator, RebalanceReport}; use crate::domain::DomainDecomposition; use crate::environment::Bounds; use crate::migration::MigrationRecord; use crate::node::MitoNode; /// Main federated simulation handle. pub struct FederatedMitoNano {     coordinator: OrchestratorCoordinator,     nodes: Vec<MitoNode>,     global_time: f64,     tick: u64,     migration_log: Vec<MigrationRecord>,     /// Tick at which the most recent rebalance completed, if any — used     /// to enforce `CoordinatorConfig::rebalance_interval` (T3 item 2).     last_rebalance_tick: Option<u64>, } impl FederatedMitoNano {     pub fn new(num_nodes: usize, global_bounds: Bounds) -> Self {         let decomposition = DomainDecomposition::new(global_bounds, num_nodes);         let nodes = decomposition.create_nodes();         let coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomposition);         Self { coordinator, nodes, global_time: 0.0, tick: 0, migration_log: Vec::new(), last_rebalance_tick: None }     }     /// Same as `new`, but with an explicit `CoordinatorConfig` (T3 item 2)     /// instead of `CoordinatorConfig::default()`.     pub fn with_config(num_nodes: usize, global_bounds: Bounds, config: CoordinatorConfig) -> Self {         let decomposition = DomainDecomposition::new(global_bounds, num_nodes);         let nodes = decomposition.create_nodes();         let coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::with_config(decomposition, config);         Self { coordinator, nodes, global_time: 0.0, tick: 0, migration_log: Vec::new(), last_rebalance_tick: None }     }     pub fn nodes(&self) -> &[MitoNode] {         &self.nodes     }     pub fn config(&self) -> CoordinatorConfig {         self.coordinator.config()     }     /// Per-node agent/ghost-agent counts and cluster-wide max ghost     /// overlap (T3 item 2's `stats()`/`metrics()` suggestion).     pub fn stats(&self) -> ClusterStats {         self.coordinator.stats(&self.nodes)     }     pub fn migration_log(&self) -> &[MigrationRecord] {         &self.migration_log     }     pub fn total_agent_count(&self) -> usize {         self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum()     }     /// Adds an agent to whichever node's local bounds currently contain     /// its position. Panics if the position is outside the global domain     /// entirely — callers are expected to place agents inside the     /// simulated region, unlike agents that later drift outside it during     /// stepping (which get clamped, not rejected; see `node.rs`).     pub fn add_agent(&mut self, agent: crate::agent::Agent) {         let target = self             .coordinator             .decomposition()             .domain_containing(agent.position)             .expect("add_agent: position is outside the global domain");         self.nodes[target].agents.push(agent);     }     /// Advances the entire federated simulation by one global step:     /// local computation on every node, then migration delivery, then a     /// load-imbalance check.     ///     /// `velocity` is this validation crate's shared motion stand-in for     /// every agent on every node (see `agent.rs`'s module docs) — a real     /// engine would derive per-agent motion independently.     pub fn step(&mut self, dt: f64, velocity: Vector3<f64>) {         let decomposition = self.coordinator.decomposition();         for node in &mut self.nodes {             node.local_step(dt, velocity, decomposition);         }         self.coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut self.nodes);         let new_records = self.coordinator.handle_migrations(&mut self.nodes, self.tick);         self.migration_log.extend(new_records);         self.global_time += dt;         self.tick += 1;     }     /// Checks current per-node load balance without modifying anything —     /// see `coordinator.rs`'s `RebalanceReport` docs for what "needs     /// rebalance" means.     pub fn check_balance(&self, imbalance_threshold: f64) -> RebalanceReport {         self.coordinator.maybe_rebalance(&self.nodes, imbalance_threshold)     }     /// Checks balance, and if it's beyond `imbalance_threshold`, actually     /// repartitions the domain and migrates every agent into the new     /// layout (see `coordinator.rs`'s `rebalance` docs). Returns the     /// *post*-rebalance report so the caller can see the result, or `None`     /// if nothing needed to change.     ///     /// The migration log is intentionally *not* extended by this — a     /// rebalance is a topology change, not agents crossing their normal     /// sub-domain boundaries under motion, so mixing the two into one     /// audit trail would make `tests/migration.rs`-style "each agent     /// migrates 0->1->2->3" chain-checking ambiguous about which kind of     /// event it's looking at.     ///     /// As of T3, `coordinator.rebalance` itself now guarantees the     /// installed layout never contains a `narrow_subdomains()` entry (see     /// `coordinator.rs`'s `rebalance` docs) — the gap noted in the T2     /// version of this doc comment is fixed, not just documented.     ///     /// Also enforces `config().rebalance_interval` (T3 item 2): if fewer     /// than `rebalance_interval` ticks have passed since the last     /// rebalance, this returns `None` even if the cluster is currently     /// imbalanced enough to otherwise trigger one.     pub fn rebalance_if_needed(&mut self, imbalance_threshold: f64) -> Option<RebalanceReport> {         if let Some(last) = self.last_rebalance_tick {             let interval = self.coordinator.config().rebalance_interval;             if self.tick.saturating_sub(last) < interval {                 return None;             }         }         let pre = self.check_balance(imbalance_threshold);         if !pre.needs_rebalance {             return None;         }         let nodes = std::mem::take(&mut self.nodes);         let (new_nodes, post) = self.coordinator.rebalance(nodes, imbalance_threshold);         self.nodes = new_nodes;         self.last_rebalance_tick = Some(self.tick);         Some(post)     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     use crate::agent::Agent;     use nalgebra::Point3;     #[test]     fn new_creates_the_requested_number_of_nodes() {         let sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         assert_eq!(sim.nodes().len(), 4);     }     #[test]     fn add_agent_places_it_in_the_correct_node() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(60.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0)); // slab [50,75) -> node 2         assert_eq!(sim.nodes()[2].agents.len(), 1);         assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), 1);     }     #[test]     #[should_panic(expected = "outside the global domain")]     fn add_agent_rejects_a_position_outside_the_global_domain() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(2, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(999.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));     }     #[test]     fn stepping_conserves_total_agent_count_across_many_migrations() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(5, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [50.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         for i in 0..30 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(1.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let expected_count = sim.total_agent_count();         // Fast rightward velocity forces every agent to cross several         // node boundaries over the course of the run.         for _ in 0..20 {             sim.step(1.0, Vector3::new(3.0, 0.0, 0.0));             assert_eq!(                 sim.total_agent_count(),                 expected_count,                 "total agent count must be conserved across migration-heavy steps"             );         }         assert!(!sim.migration_log().is_empty(), "this scenario should have produced at least one migration");     }     #[test]     fn every_agent_ends_up_in_a_node_whose_bounds_actually_contain_it() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [40.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         for i in 0..10 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         for _ in 0..15 {             sim.step(0.7, Vector3::new(1.3, 0.0, 0.0));         }         for node in sim.nodes() {             for agent in &node.agents {                 assert!(                     node.local_bounds.contains(agent.position)                         || {                             // An agent can sit exactly at this node's own far                             // (half-open) boundary transiently — see                             // node.rs's `Some(_)` branch — which is not itself                             // a bug, just documented here as the one                             // legitimate exception to strict containment.                             (agent.position.x - node.local_bounds.max[0]).abs() < 1e-9                         },                     "agent {} in node {} at {:?} is outside that node's bounds {:?}",                     agent.id,                     node.id,                     agent.position,                     node.local_bounds                 );             }         }     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_if_needed_returns_none_when_already_balanced() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(2, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         for i in 0..10 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(100 + i, Point3::new(12.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         assert!(sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_none());     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_if_needed_repartitions_and_conserves_agents_when_imbalanced() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         // All 40 agents land in the same slab -> maximally imbalanced.         // Spread across a span (5.0 to 82.9) wide enough that 4 sub-domains         // can each be both count-balanced *and* at least 2*halo=10.0 wide         // (T3's ensure_valid_for_halo minimum) — unlike a too-narrow         // cluster, this scenario is genuinely satisfiable on both counts         // at once, so both constraints should hold simultaneously here.         for i in 0..40 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let total_before = sim.total_agent_count();         let report = sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).expect("this scenario should need rebalancing");         assert!(!report.needs_rebalance, "should be balanced immediately after rebalancing");         assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), total_before, "rebalance must conserve total agent count");         assert_eq!(sim.nodes().len(), 4, "rebalance must not change the number of nodes for well-separated positions");     }     /// Honest documentation, as a test, of a real trade-off the T3 fix     /// surfaces: if every agent sits within a span *narrower* than     /// `min_subdomain_width_factor * halo * num_nodes`, count-balance and     /// halo-safety cannot both be fully satisfied — there simply isn't     /// enough physical space to give every sub-domain both a fair agent     /// share and a safe minimum width. `ensure_valid_for_halo` always     /// wins that trade-off in favor of safety (never installs a narrow     /// sub-domain), which can leave the post-rebalance report still     /// imbalanced. That's not a bug — it's the same kind of hard     /// geometric limit as the identical-position case, just short of     /// fully degenerate. What must still hold unconditionally: no panic,     /// no lost or duplicated agent, and no unsafe sub-domain installed.     #[test]     fn rebalance_can_remain_imbalanced_when_agents_are_clustered_narrower_than_the_safe_minimum_span() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         // Span is only 3.9 units (5.0 to 8.9) — far narrower than the         // 4 * 2*halo(5.0) = 40.0 units of span 4 safe-width sub-domains         // would need.         for i in 0..40 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let total_before = sim.total_agent_count();         let result = sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5);         assert!(result.is_some(), "should still attempt a rebalance");         assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), total_before, "agent count must be conserved regardless of the balance outcome");         for node in sim.nodes() {             assert!(node.local_bounds.max[0] - node.local_bounds.min[0] >= 10.0 - 1e-9, "every installed sub-domain must still respect the safe minimum width, even though balance couldn't also be achieved here");         }     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_does_not_extend_the_migration_log() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         for i in 0..40 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let log_len_before = sim.migration_log().len();         sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).expect("should need rebalancing");         assert_eq!(             sim.migration_log().len(),             log_len_before,             "rebalance is a topology change, not a normal boundary-crossing migration, and should not appear in the migration audit log"         );     }     /// This is the crate's own regression test for the exact bug the T2     /// document described finding in an earlier version of `rebalance`:     /// when many agents share the same (or extremely close) x-position, a     /// naive median-cut can propose duplicate boundary values, which     /// would either collapse into zero-width sub-domains or panic. This     /// test constructs that scenario directly — every agent at *exactly*     /// the same position — and confirms the crate handles it by producing     /// fewer (non-degenerate) sub-domains and conserving every agent,     /// rather than panicking or losing agents.     #[test]     fn rebalance_survives_many_agents_at_the_exact_same_position() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         for i in 0..50 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(37.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0)); // identical position         }         let total_before = sim.total_agent_count();         let result = sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5);         assert!(result.is_some(), "a cluster with all agents in one node should trigger a rebalance attempt");         // No 1D boundary can separate agents at an identical position, so         // the post-rebalance report legitimately can still show imbalance         // (there's a hard physical limit here, not a bug) — what matters         // is that nothing panicked and no agent was lost or duplicated.         assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), total_before, "agent count must be conserved even in this degenerate case");     }     #[test]     fn stats_reflects_current_agent_distribution() {         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]));         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(60.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(2, Point3::new(61.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         let stats = sim.stats();         assert_eq!(stats.total_agents, 2);         assert_eq!(stats.agent_counts[2], 2);         assert_eq!(stats.max_agent_count, 2);     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_interval_blocks_a_second_rebalance_too_soon_after_the_first() {         let config = CoordinatorConfig { imbalance_threshold: 1.5, rebalance_interval: 10, min_subdomain_width_factor: 2.0 };         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::with_config(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), config);         for i in 0..40 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         assert!(sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_some(), "first rebalance should be allowed");         // Force imbalance again without advancing enough ticks.         for i in 40..80 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         assert!(             sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_none(),             "a second rebalance within rebalance_interval ticks of the first should be blocked, even if imbalanced"         );     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_interval_allows_rebalance_again_once_enough_ticks_have_passed() {         let config = CoordinatorConfig { imbalance_threshold: 1.5, rebalance_interval: 3, min_subdomain_width_factor: 2.0 };         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::with_config(4, Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), config);         for i in 0..40 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         assert!(sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_some());         for i in 40..80 {             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         // Advance enough ticks to clear the cooldown.         for _ in 0..3 {             sim.step(0.0, Vector3::new(0.0, 0.0, 0.0));         }         assert!(             sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_some(),             "rebalance should be allowed again once rebalance_interval ticks have passed"         );     } } ``` ## `src/environment.rs` ```rust //! Axis-aligned bounding box used for both a node's local sub-domain and //! the global simulation domain. //! //! The uploaded source material called `sd.bounds.contains_with_halo(point, sd.halo_thickness)` //! in `domain.rs` — a method that was never defined anywhere in the //! material. This module supplies it for real. use nalgebra::Point3; #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct Bounds {     pub min: [f64; 3],     pub max: [f64; 3], } impl Bounds {     pub fn new(min: [f64; 3], max: [f64; 3]) -> Self {         debug_assert!(             min[0] <= max[0] && min[1] <= max[1] && min[2] <= max[2],             "Bounds::new requires min <= max on every axis, got min={min:?} max={max:?}"         );         Bounds { min, max }     }     /// Strict containment: `point` is inside `[min, max)` on every axis.     /// Half-open (max exclusive) so that two adjoining sub-domains sharing     /// a boundary plane never both claim to contain a point sitting     /// exactly on it — a real correctness requirement for domain     /// decomposition that the source material never addressed at all.     pub fn contains(&self, point: Point3<f64>) -> bool {         (self.min[0]..self.max[0]).contains(&point.x)             && (self.min[1]..self.max[1]).contains(&point.y)             && (self.min[2]..self.max[2]).contains(&point.z)     }     /// Containment against bounds expanded outward by `halo` on every     /// axis in every direction — used to decide which sub-domains need a     /// given boundary point's data for halo/ghost-region exchange (a     /// point near a sub-domain's edge is relevant to that sub-domain's     /// neighbor too, not just to whichever domain strictly contains it).     pub fn contains_with_halo(&self, point: Point3<f64>, halo: f64) -> bool {         (self.min[0] - halo..self.max[0] + halo).contains(&point.x)             && (self.min[1] - halo..self.max[1] + halo).contains(&point.y)             && (self.min[2] - halo..self.max[2] + halo).contains(&point.z)     }     /// Clamps `point` to lie within `[min, max]` (inclusive) on every     /// axis. Used when an agent exits the *global* simulation domain     /// entirely (no sub-domain claims it) — clamping keeps it in the     /// simulation rather than silently discarding it, which would break     /// agent-count conservation.     pub fn clamp_point(&self, point: Point3<f64>) -> Point3<f64> {         Point3::new(             point.x.clamp(self.min[0], self.max[0]),             point.y.clamp(self.min[1], self.max[1]),             point.z.clamp(self.min[2], self.max[2]),         )     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     #[test]     fn contains_is_half_open_at_max() {         let b = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         assert!(b.contains(Point3::new(0.0, 0.0, 0.0)));         assert!(b.contains(Point3::new(9.999, 5.0, 5.0)));         assert!(!b.contains(Point3::new(10.0, 5.0, 5.0)), "max boundary must be exclusive");     }     #[test]     fn adjoining_bounds_never_both_contain_the_shared_boundary_point() {         let left = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [5.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let right = Bounds::new([5.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let boundary_point = Point3::new(5.0, 5.0, 5.0);         assert!(             left.contains(boundary_point) != right.contains(boundary_point)                 || !left.contains(boundary_point),             "exactly one (or neither) of two adjoining domains should claim a shared-boundary point"         );         assert!(!left.contains(boundary_point));         assert!(right.contains(boundary_point));     }     #[test]     fn contains_with_halo_extends_past_the_strict_boundary() {         let b = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let just_outside = Point3::new(10.5, 5.0, 5.0);         assert!(!b.contains(just_outside));         assert!(b.contains_with_halo(just_outside, 1.0));         assert!(!b.contains_with_halo(just_outside, 0.1));     }     #[test]     fn clamp_point_pulls_outliers_back_inside() {         let b = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let outside = Point3::new(-5.0, 15.0, 3.0);         let clamped = b.clamp_point(outside);         assert_eq!(clamped, Point3::new(0.0, 10.0, 3.0));     } } ``` ## `src/agent.rs` ```rust //! A minimal agent type, and a simple linear-motion stand-in used to drive //! migration across sub-domain boundaries in tests. //! //! Scope note: the source material's `node.rs` referenced `crate::agent::Agent` //! and `crate::engine::SimulationEngine` as if a full per-node physics //! engine (SDE motion, PK decay, field coupling, etc. — the kind of thing //! built out in the separate `mitonano_core` simulation crate) already //! existed here. Reproducing that whole engine is out of scope for //! validating *this* material, which is specifically about domain //! decomposition, migration, and coordination — the parts that were //! actually broken. `Agent` and `LocalEngine` here are deliberately //! minimal stand-ins sufficient to drive and test that logic honestly, //! not a re-implementation of the full simulation core. use nalgebra::{Point3, Vector3}; /// A tracked entity. Carries a stable `id` (not present in the original /// material at all) specifically so migration tests can verify *which* /// agent ended up *where* — without a stable identity, you can only count /// agents, not check that a specific one migrated correctly. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct Agent {     pub id: u64,     pub position: Point3<f64>,     pub payload: f64, } impl Agent {     pub fn new(id: u64, position: Point3<f64>, payload: f64) -> Self {         Agent { id, position, payload }     }     /// Simple linear-motion stand-in: `position += velocity * dt`. Real     /// per-node physics (SDE motion, PK decay, field release/uptake) lives     /// in `mitonano_core` and is out of scope here — see module docs.     pub fn advance(&mut self, velocity: Vector3<f64>, dt: f64) {         self.position += velocity * dt;     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     #[test]     fn advance_moves_position_by_velocity_times_dt() {         let mut a = Agent::new(1, Point3::new(0.0, 0.0, 0.0), 1.0);         a.advance(Vector3::new(2.0, 0.0, 0.0), 0.5);         assert_eq!(a.position, Point3::new(1.0, 0.0, 0.0));     }     #[test]     fn advance_preserves_identity_and_payload() {         let mut a = Agent::new(42, Point3::new(1.0, 1.0, 1.0), 3.5);         a.advance(Vector3::new(1.0, 1.0, 1.0), 1.0);         assert_eq!(a.id, 42);         assert_eq!(a.payload, 3.5);     } } ``` ## `src/migration.rs` ```rust //! Migration audit records. //! //! `lib.rs` in the source material declared `pub mod migration;` but no //! content for it was ever shown — an empty declared module with no file //! backing it wouldn't even compile. Given real content here: a record of //! each agent migration event, which `coordinator.rs`'s `handle_migrations` //! now emits (see `coordinator.rs`) — this is what makes migration //! correctness actually *testable* end to end (see `tests/migration.rs` //! in this crate), rather than only inferable from before/after agent //! counts. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct MigrationRecord {     pub agent_id: u64,     pub from_node: usize,     pub to_node: usize,     pub tick: u64, } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     #[test]     fn migration_record_fields_are_accessible() {         let record = MigrationRecord { agent_id: 7, from_node: 0, to_node: 1, tick: 3 };         assert_eq!(record.agent_id, 7);         assert_eq!(record.from_node, 0);         assert_eq!(record.to_node, 1);         assert_eq!(record.tick, 3);     } } ``` ## `src/domain.rs` ```rust //! 1D domain decomposition along the x-axis (matching the source //! material's own stated scope — "starter... extend to 3D kd-tree later"). //! //! One real bug fixed here that the source material had: `DomainDecomposition::new` //! divided the global x-extent by `num_nodes` with no check that //! `num_nodes > 0`, which would panic on division by zero (or rather, //! produce `f64::INFINITY`/`NaN` sub-domain widths silently, since `f64` //! division by `0.0` doesn't panic — arguably worse, since it would fail //! silently downstream instead of failing loudly at the call site). use nalgebra::Point3; use crate::environment::Bounds; use crate::node::MitoNode; #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)] pub struct SubDomain {     pub id: usize,     pub bounds: Bounds,     pub halo_thickness: f64, } pub struct DomainDecomposition {     pub global_bounds: Bounds,     pub sub_domains: Vec<SubDomain>, } impl DomainDecomposition {     /// Splits `global` into `num_nodes` equal-width slabs along x.     ///     /// # Panics     /// Panics if `num_nodes == 0` — the source material's version would     /// have silently divided by zero instead.     pub fn new(global: Bounds, num_nodes: usize) -> Self {         assert!(num_nodes > 0, "DomainDecomposition::new requires at least one node, got 0");         let dx = (global.max[0] - global.min[0]) / num_nodes as f64;         let mut sub_domains = Vec::with_capacity(num_nodes);         for i in 0..num_nodes {             let min_x = global.min[0] + i as f64 * dx;             // Snap the last sub-domain's max exactly to the global max             // rather than accumulated-multiplication's floating point             // result, so the decomposition provably covers the whole             // global domain with no gap at the far edge.             let max_x = if i == num_nodes - 1 { global.max[0] } else { min_x + dx };             let bounds = Bounds::new(                 [min_x, global.min[1], global.min[2]],                 [max_x, global.max[1], global.max[2]],             );             sub_domains.push(SubDomain { id: i, bounds, halo_thickness: 5.0 }); // tune based on interaction/diffusion radius         }         Self { global_bounds: global, sub_domains }     }     pub fn create_nodes(&self) -> Vec<MitoNode> {         self.sub_domains.iter().map(|sd| MitoNode::new(sd.id, sd.bounds)).collect()     }     /// Builds a decomposition from explicit interior x-boundaries instead     /// of equal-width slabs — what `coordinator::rebalance` uses to     /// install a median-cut layout computed from current agent positions.     ///     /// Boundaries outside `(global.min[0], global.max[0])` are dropped,     /// and boundaries within `1e-9` of each other are deduplicated. That     /// dedup is a real, necessary fix, not defensive paranoia: if many     /// agents sit at (or extremely near) the same x-position, a median-cut     /// can propose two "different" boundaries that are actually identical,     /// which would otherwise produce a zero-width sub-domain. The     /// dedup means the result can have *fewer* sub-domains than     /// `boundaries.len() + 1` in that degenerate case — no 1D spatial     /// boundary can separate agents that are, physically, at the same     /// position, so asking for more sub-domains than the data can actually     /// support is not satisfiable, and this returns the largest     /// satisfiable decomposition rather than panicking or producing     /// degenerate zero-width slabs.     pub fn with_boundaries(global: Bounds, boundaries: Vec<f64>) -> Self {         let mut interior: Vec<f64> = boundaries             .into_iter()             .filter(|b| *b > global.min[0] && *b < global.max[0])             .collect();         interior.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());         interior.dedup_by(|a, b| (*a - *b).abs() < 1e-9);         let mut cuts = vec![global.min[0]];         cuts.extend(interior);         cuts.push(global.max[0]);         let mut sub_domains = Vec::with_capacity(cuts.len() - 1);         for i in 0..cuts.len() - 1 {             let min_x = cuts[i];             let max_x = if i == cuts.len() - 2 { global.max[0] } else { cuts[i + 1] };             let bounds = Bounds::new(                 [min_x, global.min[1], global.min[2]],                 [max_x, global.max[1], global.max[2]],             );             sub_domains.push(SubDomain { id: i, bounds, halo_thickness: 5.0 });         }         Self { global_bounds: global, sub_domains }     }     /// Ids of every sub-domain narrower (in x) than `2 * halo_thickness`.     ///     /// This matters specifically because of `with_boundaries`/`rebalance`:     /// the equal-width `new` constructor can't produce this by     /// construction (every slab is deliberately the same width), but a     /// median-cut rebalance driven by a skewed agent distribution     /// genuinely can produce a very thin slab. A sub-domain narrower than     /// its own halo diameter breaks the "only check immediate neighbors"     /// optimization `coordinator::exchange_boundaries` relies on (a halo     /// could then reach past an immediate neighbor into the next one over)     /// — this is *detection* of that condition, not a fix; see     /// `coordinator.rs`'s `rebalance` docs for what currently happens when     /// it fires.     pub fn narrow_subdomains(&self) -> Vec<usize> {         self.sub_domains             .iter()             .filter(|sd| (sd.bounds.max[0] - sd.bounds.min[0]) < 2.0 * sd.halo_thickness)             .map(|sd| sd.id)             .collect()     }     /// Fixes gap #1 from the T2 revision (T3 direction, item 1,     /// "Immediate Correctness Fixes"): given a `min_width_factor` (e.g.     /// `2.0`, matching `narrow_subdomains`'s own threshold), returns a     /// decomposition where **every** sub-domain is at least     /// `min_width_factor * halo_thickness` wide, by shifting boundaries —     /// Option A from the T3 document ("widen thin slabs intelligently"),     /// with Option C ("force uniform spacing") as the fallback when Option     /// A isn't geometrically possible.     ///     /// Algorithm: every sub-domain is first guaranteed the minimum width;     /// whatever global width is left over after that is then distributed     /// across sub-domains in proportion to their *original* widths, so a     /// rebalance that was already close to uniform stays close to     /// uniform, and a rebalance that concentrated most sub-domains into a     /// few wide ones keeps that general shape (just no longer with any     /// individual sub-domain unsafely thin).     ///     /// If even `min_width_factor * halo_thickness` per sub-domain doesn't     /// fit inside the global domain at the current sub-domain count (a     /// real possibility: e.g. an extreme rebalance driven by highly     /// clustered agents can ask for far more sub-domains than the domain     /// geometrically supports at a safe halo width), this can't produce     /// `self.sub_domains.len()` valid sub-domains at all — no amount of     /// boundary-shifting fixes an under-determined problem. In that case     /// this falls back to the largest equal-width decomposition that     /// *is* geometrically safe (Option C), which necessarily has fewer     /// sub-domains than requested. This never returns a decomposition     /// with any `narrow_subdomains()` entry — that degenerate case is     /// exactly what forces the fallback instead.     pub fn ensure_valid_for_halo(&self, min_width_factor: f64) -> DomainDecomposition {         let global = self.global_bounds;         let requested_n = self.sub_domains.len().max(1);         let halo = self.sub_domains.first().map(|sd| sd.halo_thickness).unwrap_or(5.0);         let min_width = min_width_factor * halo;         let total_width = global.max[0] - global.min[0];         let max_feasible_n = ((total_width / min_width).floor() as usize).max(1);         if max_feasible_n < requested_n {             // Not enough room to give every requested sub-domain a safe             // minimum width — fall back to the largest decomposition that             // *is* geometrically safe, uniform (Option C).             return DomainDecomposition::new(global, max_feasible_n);         }         if requested_n == 1 {             return DomainDecomposition::new(global, 1);         }         let original_widths: Vec<f64> =             self.sub_domains.iter().map(|sd| sd.bounds.max[0] - sd.bounds.min[0]).collect();         let total_original: f64 = original_widths.iter().sum();         let leftover = total_width - min_width * requested_n as f64;         let mut new_widths = Vec::with_capacity(requested_n);         for w in &original_widths {             let share = if total_original > 0.0 { leftover * (w / total_original) } else { leftover / requested_n as f64 };             new_widths.push(min_width + share);         }         let mut boundaries = Vec::with_capacity(requested_n - 1);         let mut cursor = global.min[0];         for w in &new_widths[..requested_n - 1] {             cursor += w;             boundaries.push(cursor);         }         DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, boundaries)     }     /// The id of the sub-domain that strictly contains `point`, if any.     /// `None` means `point` has left the *global* domain entirely (a real     /// case `node.rs`'s migration logic must handle — see its module     /// docs for how).     ///     /// T3 bug fix, found by the property sweep in `tests/property.rs`:     /// `Bounds::contains` is deliberately half-open (max exclusive) so     /// that two *adjacent* sub-domains never both claim a shared internal     /// boundary point — necessary and correct there. But applying that     /// same half-open rule to the *outer* edge of the global domain is     /// wrong: there is no neighboring sub-domain on the far side of it to     /// disambiguate against, so a point sitting exactly on the global max     /// (on any axis) was simply unfindable by any sub-domain at all,     /// which crashed `coordinator::rebalance` (an agent can legitimately     /// land exactly there via `node.rs`'s own `clamp_point` call when it     /// exits the global domain during a step). Fixed by nudging any     /// coordinate that exactly equals the *global* max inward by a tiny     /// epsilon before the containment lookup — internal seams are     /// untouched (the nudge only ever triggers at the true global edge),     /// so adjacent sub-domains still never both claim a shared boundary.     pub fn domain_containing(&self, point: Point3<f64>) -> Option<usize> {         const EPS: f64 = 1e-9;         let nudge = |v: f64, global_max: f64| if (v - global_max).abs() < EPS { global_max - EPS } else { v };         let adjusted = Point3::new(             nudge(point.x, self.global_bounds.max[0]),             nudge(point.y, self.global_bounds.max[1]),             nudge(point.z, self.global_bounds.max[2]),         );         self.sub_domains.iter().find(|sd| sd.bounds.contains(adjusted)).map(|sd| sd.id)     }     /// Every sub-domain whose halo-expanded bounds include `point` — used     /// for boundary/ghost-region exchange (a point can be relevant to a     /// neighboring domain's halo even though that domain doesn't strictly     /// contain it).     pub fn neighbors_of(&self, point: Point3<f64>) -> Vec<usize> {         self.sub_domains             .iter()             .filter(|sd| sd.bounds.contains_with_halo(point, sd.halo_thickness))             .map(|sd| sd.id)             .collect()     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     #[test]     #[should_panic(expected = "at least one node")]     fn rejects_zero_nodes() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 100.0, 100.0]);         let _ = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 0);     }     #[test]     fn produces_requested_number_of_subdomains() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 100.0, 100.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.len(), 4);     }     #[test]     fn subdomains_exactly_tile_the_global_domain_with_no_gap_or_overlap() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 50.0, 50.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 7);         // No gap: each sub-domain's max_x should equal the next's min_x exactly.         for window in decomp.sub_domains.windows(2) {             assert!(                 (window[0].bounds.max[0] - window[1].bounds.min[0]).abs() < 1e-9,                 "gap or overlap between sub-domains {} and {}",                 window[0].id,                 window[1].id             );         }         // Full coverage: first sub-domain starts exactly at global min,         // last ends exactly at global max.         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.first().unwrap().bounds.min[0], global.min[0]);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.last().unwrap().bounds.max[0], global.max[0]);     }     #[test]     fn single_node_decomposition_covers_the_whole_domain() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 1);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.len(), 1);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds, global);     }     #[test]     fn domain_containing_finds_the_correct_subdomain() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         // slabs: [0,25) [25,50) [50,75) [75,100]         assert_eq!(decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(5.0, 5.0, 5.0)), Some(0));         assert_eq!(decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(30.0, 5.0, 5.0)), Some(1));         assert_eq!(decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(99.0, 5.0, 5.0)), Some(3));     }     /// T3 regression test for the exact bug the property sweep found:     /// a point sitting exactly on the global domain's outer edge (on any     /// axis) must still resolve to a sub-domain, not `None`.     #[test]     fn domain_containing_finds_the_subdomain_for_a_point_exactly_on_the_global_max() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         assert_eq!(             decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(100.0, 5.0, 5.0)),             Some(3),             "a point exactly on the global x-max must resolve to the last sub-domain"         );         assert_eq!(             decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(45.0, 10.0, 5.0)),             Some(1),             "a point exactly on the global y-max must still resolve correctly"         );         assert_eq!(             decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(45.0, 5.0, 10.0)),             Some(1),             "a point exactly on the global z-max must still resolve correctly"         );     }     #[test]     fn domain_containing_returns_none_outside_global_bounds() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         assert_eq!(decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(150.0, 5.0, 5.0)), None);         assert_eq!(decomp.domain_containing(Point3::new(-5.0, 5.0, 5.0)), None);     }     #[test]     fn neighbors_of_includes_adjoining_domain_within_halo() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         // boundary between domain 0/1 at x=25         let near_boundary = Point3::new(24.0, 5.0, 5.0); // strictly in domain 0, within halo (5.0) of domain 1         let neighbors = decomp.neighbors_of(near_boundary);         assert!(neighbors.contains(&0));         assert!(             neighbors.contains(&1),             "point within halo distance of domain 1's boundary should list it as a neighbor"         );     }     #[test]     fn create_nodes_produces_one_node_per_subdomain_with_matching_bounds() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [40.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         let nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         assert_eq!(nodes.len(), 4);         for (node, sd) in nodes.iter().zip(decomp.sub_domains.iter()) {             assert_eq!(node.id, sd.id);             assert_eq!(node.local_bounds, sd.bounds);         }     }     #[test]     fn with_boundaries_produces_correct_subdomain_count_and_coverage() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![30.0, 55.0, 80.0]);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.len(), 4);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds.min[0], 0.0);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds.max[0], 30.0);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[1].bounds.min[0], 30.0);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[1].bounds.max[0], 55.0);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains[3].bounds.max[0], 100.0);     }     #[test]     fn with_boundaries_deduplicates_near_identical_boundaries() {         // Simulates the degenerate case: a median-cut on many         // same-position agents can propose "different" boundaries that         // are actually identical (or within float noise). The result         // should have fewer sub-domains than boundaries.len()+1, not a         // zero-width sub-domain or a panic.         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![50.0, 50.0 + 1e-12, 50.0]);         assert_eq!(             decomp.sub_domains.len(),             2,             "three collapsed-duplicate boundaries should yield 2 sub-domains, not 4"         );         for sd in &decomp.sub_domains {             assert!(                 sd.bounds.max[0] - sd.bounds.min[0] > 0.0,                 "no sub-domain should have zero or negative width"             );         }     }     #[test]     fn with_boundaries_ignores_boundaries_outside_the_global_domain() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![-10.0, 50.0, 200.0]);         assert_eq!(             decomp.sub_domains.len(),             2,             "out-of-range boundaries should be dropped, not clamped into extra tiny slabs"         );     }     #[test]     fn narrow_subdomains_flags_a_slab_thinner_than_its_own_halo_diameter() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         // Default halo_thickness is 5.0, so 2*halo = 10.0; a slab from         // 50.0 to 53.0 (width 3.0) is well under that.         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![50.0, 53.0]);         let narrow = decomp.narrow_subdomains();         assert!(narrow.contains(&1), "the [50,53) slab should be flagged as narrower than 2*halo_thickness");     }     #[test]     fn ensure_valid_for_halo_widens_a_thin_slab_and_conserves_coverage() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         // slab [50,53) is width 3.0, well under 2*halo (10.0).         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![50.0, 53.0]);         assert!(!decomp.narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "sanity: this decomposition should start out narrow");         let repaired = decomp.ensure_valid_for_halo(2.0);         assert!(repaired.narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "every sub-domain should be widened to a safe minimum");         assert_eq!(repaired.sub_domains.len(), decomp.sub_domains.len(), "widening should preserve sub-domain count when geometrically possible");         // Coverage preserved: still tiles the whole global domain exactly.         assert_eq!(repaired.sub_domains.first().unwrap().bounds.min[0], global.min[0]);         assert_eq!(repaired.sub_domains.last().unwrap().bounds.max[0], global.max[0]);         for window in repaired.sub_domains.windows(2) {             assert!((window[0].bounds.max[0] - window[1].bounds.min[0]).abs() < 1e-9);         }     }     #[test]     fn ensure_valid_for_halo_leaves_an_already_safe_decomposition_unchanged_in_shape() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4); // each slab 25 wide, well over 2*halo=10         assert!(decomp.narrow_subdomains().is_empty());         let repaired = decomp.ensure_valid_for_halo(2.0);         assert_eq!(repaired.sub_domains.len(), 4);         assert!(repaired.narrow_subdomains().is_empty());     }     #[test]     fn ensure_valid_for_halo_falls_back_to_fewer_subdomains_when_widening_is_geometrically_impossible() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         // Requesting 8 sub-domains each >= 2*halo(5.0)=10.0 wide inside a         // 20-wide global domain is impossible (would need 80 units of         // width); only 2 sub-domains actually fit safely.         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 8);         let repaired = decomp.ensure_valid_for_halo(2.0);         assert!(repaired.narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "fallback decomposition must itself be safe");         assert!(repaired.sub_domains.len() < 8, "must fall back to fewer sub-domains rather than produce an unsafe one");         assert_eq!(repaired.sub_domains.len(), 2);     }     #[test]     fn narrow_subdomains_is_empty_for_a_normal_equal_width_decomposition() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 4);         // each slab 25 wide, halo 5 -> 2*halo=10 < 25         assert!(decomp.narrow_subdomains().is_empty());     } } ``` ## `src/node.rs` ```rust //! A single node's local simulation state and stepping logic. //! //! Two real bugs from the source material are fixed here: //! //! 1. **No way to find a migration target.** The original //!    `handle_local_agent_movement` had `let target_node = /* find neighbor via decomposition */;` //!    — a comment, not code, and `MitoNode` held no reference to the //!    decomposition it would need to actually answer that question. //!    Fixed by passing `&DomainDecomposition` into `local_step` — see the //!    note below on why that's the realistic fix rather than making each //!    node own a copy of the whole decomposition permanently. //! 2. **Migrated agents were never removed from the source node.** The //!    original comment said `// Remove migrated agents` directly below //!    code that never did it — a migrating agent would end up in *both* //!    the old node's `agents` list and get pushed to the new node, //!    duplicating it. Fixed with `Vec::swap_remove` at the point of //!    detection instead. //! //! On passing `&DomainDecomposition` into every node's `local_step`: this //! matches how real domain-decomposition codes (e.g. MPI-based ones) //! usually work — the *partition table* (a handful of bounding boxes) is //! cheap enough to replicate to every rank/node, even though the //! *per-agent data* stays strictly local and never gets broadcast. The //! source material's framing ("federated," implying nodes shouldn't see //! each other's internals) conflated those two things; replicating the //! lightweight topology table isn't a federation violation, it's standard //! practice — only agent data and node-local physics state stay private. use nalgebra::Vector3; use crate::agent::Agent; use crate::domain::DomainDecomposition; use crate::environment::Bounds; /// Minimal stand-in for the full per-node physics engine (SDE motion, PK /// decay, field coupling — see `agent.rs`'s module docs for why the full /// `mitonano_core` engine isn't reproduced here). Just accumulates local /// elapsed time, enough to prove the plumbing between `FederatedMitoNano`, /// `MitoNode`, and a real per-node engine is wired correctly. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)] pub struct LocalEngine {     pub elapsed_time: f64,     pub ticks: u64, } impl LocalEngine {     pub fn new_default() -> Self {         LocalEngine::default()     }     pub fn step(&mut self, dt: f64) {         self.elapsed_time += dt;         self.ticks += 1;     } } pub struct MitoNode {     pub id: usize,     pub engine: LocalEngine,     pub agents: Vec<Agent>,     pub local_bounds: Bounds,     pub outgoing_migrations: Vec<(Agent, usize)>,     /// Read-only snapshots of neighboring nodes' agents that fall within     /// this node's halo range, refreshed (not accumulated) by     /// `coordinator::exchange_boundaries` every step. Empty until the     /// first `exchange_boundaries` call. See that function's docs for how     /// it's populated and its "immediate neighbors only" limitation.     pub ghost_agents: Vec<Agent>, } impl MitoNode {     pub fn new(id: usize, bounds: Bounds) -> Self {         Self {             id,             engine: LocalEngine::new_default(),             agents: Vec::new(),             local_bounds: bounds,             outgoing_migrations: Vec::new(),             ghost_agents: Vec::new(),         }     }     /// Advances local physics one step, then moves agents and queues any     /// that leave this node's bounds for migration (removing them from     /// `agents` immediately — see module docs on why that fix matters).     ///     /// `velocity` is this validation crate's simple motion stand-in (see     /// `agent.rs`); a real engine would derive per-agent motion from SDE     /// parameters instead of one shared velocity.     pub fn local_step(&mut self, dt: f64, velocity: Vector3<f64>, decomposition: &DomainDecomposition) {         self.engine.step(dt);         self.handle_local_agent_movement(dt, velocity, decomposition);     }     fn handle_local_agent_movement(&mut self, dt: f64, velocity: Vector3<f64>, decomposition: &DomainDecomposition) {         let mut i = 0;         while i < self.agents.len() {             self.agents[i].advance(velocity, dt);             if self.local_bounds.contains(self.agents[i].position) {                 i += 1;                 continue;             }             // Agent left this node's bounds — find out where it actually             // belongs now.             match decomposition.domain_containing(self.agents[i].position) {                 Some(target_id) if target_id != self.id => {                     // swap_remove both removes it from this node (fixing                     // bug #2 above) and avoids an O(n) shift; the loop                     // index `i` is deliberately *not* incremented, since                     // swap_remove moved a different agent into slot `i`                     // that still needs to be checked this pass.                     let migrating_agent = self.agents.swap_remove(i);                     self.outgoing_migrations.push((migrating_agent, target_id));                 }                 Some(_) => {                     // target_id == self.id: contains() is strict/half-open                     // (see environment.rs), so a point can transiently sit                     // right on this node's own far boundary and register                     // as "not contained" by the half-open check while                     // still resolving to this same node in the global                     // decomposition. Not a migration; leave it in place.                     i += 1;                 }                 None => {                     // Left the *global* domain entirely. Clamp back inside                     // rather than silently dropping it — dropping would                     // break total-agent-count conservation, which is                     // exactly the kind of invariant this crate's tests                     // check for.                     self.agents[i].position = decomposition.global_bounds.clamp_point(self.agents[i].position);                     i += 1;                 }             }         }     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     use nalgebra::Point3;     fn two_node_decomposition() -> DomainDecomposition {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         DomainDecomposition::new(global, 2) // [0,10) and [10,20]     }     #[test]     fn agent_staying_in_bounds_does_not_migrate() {         let decomp = two_node_decomposition();         let mut node = MitoNode::new(0, decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds);         node.agents.push(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(1.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         node.local_step(1.0, Vector3::new(1.0, 0.0, 0.0), &decomp);         assert_eq!(node.agents.len(), 1, "agent should still be local");         assert!(node.outgoing_migrations.is_empty());     }     #[test]     fn agent_crossing_boundary_is_removed_locally_and_queued_for_migration() {         let decomp = two_node_decomposition();         let mut node = MitoNode::new(0, decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds);         node.agents.push(Agent::new(7, Point3::new(9.5, 5.0, 5.0), 3.0));         // Moves from x=9.5 to x=10.5 — crosses from node 0 into node 1.         node.local_step(1.0, Vector3::new(1.0, 0.0, 0.0), &decomp);         assert!(node.agents.is_empty(), "migrated agent must be removed from the source node's list (bug #2 fix)");         assert_eq!(node.outgoing_migrations.len(), 1);         let (migrated_agent, target) = node.outgoing_migrations[0];         assert_eq!(migrated_agent.id, 7);         assert_eq!(target, 1, "agent at x=10.5 should be routed to node 1 (bug #1 fix)");     }     #[test]     fn agent_leaving_global_domain_is_clamped_not_dropped() {         let decomp = two_node_decomposition();         let mut node = MitoNode::new(1, decomp.sub_domains[1].bounds);         node.agents.push(Agent::new(3, Point3::new(19.5, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         // Moves from x=19.5 to x=25.5 — past the global domain's max (20.0).         node.local_step(1.0, Vector3::new(6.0, 0.0, 0.0), &decomp);         assert_eq!(node.agents.len(), 1, "agent leaving the global domain must be clamped back in, not dropped");         assert!(node.outgoing_migrations.is_empty());         assert!(node.agents[0].position.x <= 20.0, "clamped position should respect the global max");     }     /// Regression test: `domain_containing` does a global search over     /// every sub-domain, not a check against just the immediate neighbor     /// — so an agent whose single-step displacement is large enough to     /// skip past more than one slab boundary should still route directly     /// to the *correct final* node in one shot, not get stuck at (or     /// incorrectly routed to) the first boundary it crosses. This already     /// worked correctly before this revision, but every existing     /// migration test happened to use a velocity that crosses exactly one     /// boundary per step, so it was previously unverified by any test.     #[test]     fn agent_skipping_multiple_slabs_in_one_step_routes_directly_to_final_node() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(global, 5); // slabs: [0,20) [20,40) [40,60) [60,80) [80,100]         let mut node = MitoNode::new(0, decomp.sub_domains[0].bounds);         node.agents.push(Agent::new(11, Point3::new(5.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         // Single huge step: x=5.0 -> x=95.0, skipping slabs 1, 2, and 3         // entirely and landing directly in slab 4.         node.local_step(1.0, Vector3::new(90.0, 0.0, 0.0), &decomp);         assert!(node.agents.is_empty(), "agent should have migrated out of node 0");         assert_eq!(node.outgoing_migrations.len(), 1, "exactly one migration event, not one per skipped boundary");         let (migrated_agent, target) = node.outgoing_migrations[0];         assert_eq!(migrated_agent.id, 11);         assert_eq!(             target, 4,             "should route directly to the final containing node (slab [80,100]), skipping slabs 1-3 in one hop"         );     } } ``` ## `src/coordinator.rs` ```rust //! Cross-node coordination: load-imbalance detection, median-cut //! repartitioning, halo/ghost-region exchange, migration delivery, and //! cluster-level observability. //! //! ## Provenance //! //! The T2 revision document this crate was originally built from //! described this module's behavior and named its tests, but never //! included its actual source.
