This file was a from-scratch T2 //! implementation against that prose description; it has since been //! revised for T3 against the "Next Steps & Improvements" document, //! specifically its item 1 ("Immediate Correctness Fixes — Highest //! Priority") and parts of item 2 ("Architectural & API Improvements"). //! //! ## T2 → T3 changelog //! //! **Fixed (T3 item 1 — the two coupled gaps):** //! - `rebalance` now calls `DomainDecomposition::ensure_valid_for_halo` //!   on the median-cut layout before applying it, so a rebalance can no //!   longer install a sub-domain narrower than a safe halo width. See //!   `domain.rs`'s `ensure_valid_for_halo` docs for the widen-or-fall-back //!   algorithm (T3 doc's Option A with Option C as fallback). //! - `exchange_boundaries` no longer assumes "immediate neighbor" is //!   sufficient. It now scans outward from each node in both directions //!   and stops only once a neighbor's *unexpanded* bounds are farther //!   than that node's halo distance away — correct regardless of //!   sub-domain width, not dependent on gap #1 staying fixed. This is a //!   direct fix, not the "assert/log when narrow subdomains exist" //!   fallback the T3 document offered as the minimum viable option. //! //! **Added (T3 item 2 — architecture/observability):** //! - `CoordinatorConfig`: `imbalance_threshold`, `rebalance_interval`, //!   `min_subdomain_width_factor` (T3 doc's suggested struct, verbatim //!   field set). `OrchestratorCoordinator::with_config` / //!   `OrchestratorCoordinator::config` accessors. `rebalance` now uses //!   `config.min_subdomain_width_factor` instead of a hardcoded `2.0`. //! - `stats()`: per-node agent-count histogram, ghost-agent counts, and //!   max halo overlap, addressing the T3 doc's "stats()/metrics()" //!   suggestion. //! //! **Deliberately deferred to a later revision** (T3 document sections 3, //! 4, 5, 6 — performance, property/fuzz testing, new features, workspace //! restructuring): sampling/incremental rebalance, a `NodeSlice` trait for //! distributed backends, dynamic node count, 2D/3D decomposition, //! checkpointing, fault tolerance, `rayon`/`serde` integration. None of //! these are implemented here. Listing them as deferred rather than //! silently omitting them is the same "what changed, what was //! reconstructed" discipline the T3 document itself asks for in section 6. //! Property-based tests (proptest) for count-conservation, containment, //! and no-ghost-duplication *are* implemented — see `tests/property.rs`. use crate::agent::Agent; use crate::domain::DomainDecomposition; use crate::migration::MigrationRecord; use crate::node::MitoNode; /// T3 doc's suggested configuration struct, field set taken verbatim. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct CoordinatorConfig {     /// `imbalance_ratio` threshold above which `maybe_rebalance` reports     /// `needs_rebalance = true`.     pub imbalance_threshold: f64,     /// Minimum number of ticks that must pass between the end of one     /// rebalance and the start of the next. `0` means no cooldown     /// (every call to `FederatedMitoNano::rebalance_if_needed` is free to     /// rebalance again immediately if still imbalanced).     pub rebalance_interval: u64,     /// Multiplied by a sub-domain's halo thickness to get its required     /// minimum width. Passed straight through to     /// `DomainDecomposition::ensure_valid_for_halo`.     pub min_subdomain_width_factor: f64, } impl Default for CoordinatorConfig {     fn default() -> Self {         CoordinatorConfig { imbalance_threshold: 1.5, rebalance_interval: 0, min_subdomain_width_factor: 2.0 }     } } /// Snapshot of per-node load balance, returned by both `maybe_rebalance` /// (read-only check) and `rebalance` (post-repartition result). /// /// `imbalance_ratio` is `max_count / max(min_count, 1)` — the `max(_, 1)` /// avoids a division by zero when the least-loaded node is empty (a very /// common case, not an edge case to special-case away) while still /// reporting a large, threshold-exceeding ratio in that situation rather /// than an undefined one. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct RebalanceReport {     pub needs_rebalance: bool,     pub max_node: usize,     pub min_node: usize,     pub max_count: usize,     pub min_count: usize,     pub imbalance_ratio: f64, } /// Cluster-level observability snapshot (T3 doc's `stats()`/`metrics()` /// suggestion): per-node agent counts, per-node ghost-agent counts, and /// the cluster-wide maximum of the two, for dashboards/logging without /// requiring a full `maybe_rebalance` call. #[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct ClusterStats {     pub agent_counts: Vec<usize>,     pub ghost_counts: Vec<usize>,     pub total_agents: usize,     pub max_agent_count: usize,     pub min_agent_count: usize,     pub max_ghost_overlap: usize, } pub struct OrchestratorCoordinator {     decomposition: DomainDecomposition,     config: CoordinatorConfig, } impl OrchestratorCoordinator {     pub fn new(decomposition: DomainDecomposition) -> Self {         Self { decomposition, config: CoordinatorConfig::default() }     }     pub fn with_config(decomposition: DomainDecomposition, config: CoordinatorConfig) -> Self {         Self { decomposition, config }     }     pub fn decomposition(&self) -> &DomainDecomposition {         &self.decomposition     }     pub fn config(&self) -> CoordinatorConfig {         self.config     }     pub fn set_config(&mut self, config: CoordinatorConfig) {         self.config = config;     }     /// Real halo exchange, correct regardless of sub-domain width (T3 fix     /// for gap #2 — see module docs).     ///     /// For each node, scans outward in both directions from its own     /// index. Because sub-domains are sorted along x by construction, a     /// neighbor's *unexpanded* bounds moving strictly farther away as the     /// scan continues means it's safe to stop the moment a neighbor is     /// farther than this node's halo distance — every sub-domain past it     /// in that direction is farther still. This holds no matter how thin     /// any individual sub-domain is, so it doesn't depend on gap #1     /// (`ensure_valid_for_halo`) staying fixed — it's correct even if a     /// caller bypasses `rebalance` entirely and hands this an adversarial     /// decomposition.     ///     /// `ghost_agents` is replaced, not appended to, on every call — a     /// long-running simulation that accumulated ghosts forever would leak     /// memory and silently duplicate ghost state.     pub fn exchange_boundaries(&mut self, nodes: &mut [MitoNode]) {         let n = nodes.len();         let mut new_ghosts: Vec<Vec<Agent>> = vec![Vec::new(); n];         for i in 0..n {             let halo = self.decomposition.sub_domains[i].halo_thickness;             let bounds = nodes[i].local_bounds;             // Scan left.             let mut j = i;             while j > 0 {                 j -= 1;                 if nodes[j].local_bounds.max[0] < bounds.min[0] - halo {                     break; // this and every further-left node are out of halo range                 }                 for agent in &nodes[j].agents {                     if bounds.contains_with_halo(agent.position, halo) {                         new_ghosts[i].push(*agent);                     }                 }             }             // Scan right.             let mut j = i;             while j + 1 < n {                 j += 1;                 if nodes[j].local_bounds.min[0] > bounds.max[0] + halo {                     break; // this and every further-right node are out of halo range                 }                 for agent in &nodes[j].agents {                     if bounds.contains_with_halo(agent.position, halo) {                         new_ghosts[i].push(*agent);                     }                 }             }         }         for (i, node) in nodes.iter_mut().enumerate() {             node.ghost_agents = std::mem::take(&mut new_ghosts[i]);         }     }     /// Delivers every node's queued `outgoing_migrations` to their target     /// nodes and returns an audit record for each. Migrations are     /// collected from every node first, then delivered in a second pass —     /// avoids the overlapping-mutable-borrow bug the T1 source material     /// had (`for node in nodes { ... nodes.get_mut(target) ... }`).     pub fn handle_migrations(&mut self, nodes: &mut [MitoNode], tick: u64) -> Vec<MigrationRecord> {         let mut records = Vec::new();         let mut deliveries: Vec<(Agent, usize)> = Vec::new();         for node in nodes.iter_mut() {             for (agent, target) in node.outgoing_migrations.drain(..) {                 records.push(MigrationRecord { agent_id: agent.id, from_node: node.id, to_node: target, tick });                 deliveries.push((agent, target));             }         }         for (agent, target) in deliveries {             nodes[target].agents.push(agent);         }         records     }     /// Checks current per-node load balance without modifying anything.     /// "Needs rebalance" means `imbalance_ratio > imbalance_threshold`.     pub fn maybe_rebalance(&self, nodes: &[MitoNode], imbalance_threshold: f64) -> RebalanceReport {         let counts: Vec<usize> = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).collect();         let (max_node, &max_count) = counts             .iter()             .enumerate()             .max_by_key(|(_, &c)| c)             .expect("maybe_rebalance requires at least one node");         let (min_node, &min_count) = counts             .iter()             .enumerate()             .min_by_key(|(_, &c)| c)             .expect("maybe_rebalance requires at least one node");         let imbalance_ratio = max_count as f64 / (min_count.max(1) as f64);         let needs_rebalance = imbalance_ratio > imbalance_threshold;         RebalanceReport { needs_rebalance, max_node, min_node, max_count, min_count, imbalance_ratio }     }     /// Per-node agent and ghost-agent counts, plus the cluster-wide     /// maximum ghost overlap — a lightweight observability snapshot that     /// doesn't require picking an imbalance threshold the way     /// `maybe_rebalance` does.     pub fn stats(&self, nodes: &[MitoNode]) -> ClusterStats {         let agent_counts: Vec<usize> = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).collect();         let ghost_counts: Vec<usize> = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.ghost_agents.len()).collect();         let total_agents = agent_counts.iter().sum();         let max_agent_count = agent_counts.iter().copied().max().unwrap_or(0);         let min_agent_count = agent_counts.iter().copied().min().unwrap_or(0);         let max_ghost_overlap = ghost_counts.iter().copied().max().unwrap_or(0);         ClusterStats { agent_counts, ghost_counts, total_agents, max_agent_count, min_agent_count, max_ghost_overlap }     }     /// Repartitions the domain via 1D median-cut on agents' current x     /// positions, into the same number of sub-domains as before (unless     /// `ensure_valid_for_halo` must fall back to fewer — see below), and     /// migrates every agent into the new layout. Returns the new nodes     /// and a post-repartition `RebalanceReport`.     ///     /// The median-cut computes `num_nodes - 1` interior boundaries at the     /// positions that would split the sorted agent list into equal-count     /// groups, then hands them to `DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries`     /// (which absorbs the duplicate-boundary degenerate case — see that     /// function's docs), and then — this is the T3 fix — the *result* is     /// passed through `ensure_valid_for_halo(config.min_subdomain_width_factor)`     /// before being applied, so a rebalance can no longer install an     /// unsafely thin sub-domain. See `domain.rs`'s `ensure_valid_for_halo`     /// docs for exactly what happens when widening isn't geometrically     /// possible (it falls back to fewer, safe sub-domains rather than     /// producing an invalid one).     pub fn rebalance(&mut self, nodes: Vec<MitoNode>, imbalance_threshold: f64) -> (Vec<MitoNode>, RebalanceReport) {         let num_nodes = nodes.len();         let global = self.decomposition.global_bounds;         let mut all_agents: Vec<Agent> = nodes.into_iter().flat_map(|n| n.agents).collect();         all_agents.sort_by(|a, b| a.position.x.partial_cmp(&b.position.x).unwrap());         let mut boundaries = Vec::new();         if num_nodes > 1 && !all_agents.is_empty() {             let n = all_agents.len();             for i in 1..num_nodes {                 let idx = (i * n / num_nodes).clamp(1, n - 1);                 let boundary = (all_agents[idx - 1].position.x + all_agents[idx].position.x) / 2.0;                 boundaries.push(boundary);             }         }         let median_cut = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, boundaries);         // T3 fix for gap #1: never apply a decomposition with an unsafely         // thin sub-domain. Widens if geometrically possible, otherwise         // falls back to fewer, safe sub-domains.         let new_decomposition = median_cut.ensure_valid_for_halo(self.config.min_subdomain_width_factor);         debug_assert!(             new_decomposition.narrow_subdomains().is_empty(),             "ensure_valid_for_halo must never return a decomposition with a narrow sub-domain"         );         let mut new_nodes = new_decomposition.create_nodes();         for agent in all_agents {             // Every agent came from inside the global domain already, so             // this should always find a home — but if float edge cases             // ever put a position exactly on the outer boundary in a way             // domain_containing rejects, clamp rather than drop, for the             // same agent-count-conservation reason node.rs clamps.             let target = new_decomposition                 .domain_containing(agent.position)                 .or_else(|| {                     let clamped = global.clamp_point(agent.position);                     new_decomposition.domain_containing(clamped)                 })                 .expect("rebalance: agent position could not be placed in the new decomposition");             new_nodes[target].agents.push(agent);         }         self.decomposition = new_decomposition;         let report = self.maybe_rebalance(&new_nodes, imbalance_threshold);         (new_nodes, report)     } } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     use crate::environment::Bounds;     use nalgebra::Point3;     fn four_node_decomposition() -> DomainDecomposition {         DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 4)     }     #[test]     fn exchange_boundaries_populates_ghost_agents_within_halo_range() {         let decomp = four_node_decomposition(); // halo=5.0, slabs [0,25)[25,50)[50,75)[75,100]         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         // Sits in node 0, within halo (5.0) of the 25.0 boundary into node 1.         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(23.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         // Sits in node 1, far from any boundary.         nodes[1].agents.push(Agent::new(2, Point3::new(37.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         assert!(             nodes[1].ghost_agents.iter().any(|a| a.id == 1),             "node 1 should see node 0's boundary agent as a ghost"         );         assert!(             nodes[0].ghost_agents.is_empty(),             "node 0's agent is not within halo range of anything in node 1"         );     }     #[test]     fn exchange_boundaries_refreshes_rather_than_accumulates_across_calls() {         let decomp = four_node_decomposition();         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(23.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         let first_len = nodes[1].ghost_agents.len();         assert_eq!(first_len, 1);         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         assert_eq!(             nodes[1].ghost_agents.len(),             first_len,             "ghost_agents must be replaced, not appended to, each call"         );     }     #[test]     fn interior_node_sees_ghosts_from_both_left_and_right_neighbors_simultaneously() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [90.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 3);         // slabs: [0,30)[30,60)[60,90], halo=5.0         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(28.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0)); // within 5 of x=30         nodes[2].agents.push(Agent::new(2, Point3::new(62.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0)); // within 5 of x=60         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         assert!(nodes[1].ghost_agents.iter().any(|a| a.id == 1), "middle node should see the left neighbor's ghost");         assert!(nodes[1].ghost_agents.iter().any(|a| a.id == 2), "middle node should see the right neighbor's ghost");         assert_eq!(nodes[1].ghost_agents.len(), 2);     }     /// T3 regression test: unlike the T2 "immediate neighbor only"     /// version, this must find a ghost across a thin *intermediate*     /// sub-domain that a strictly-adjacent-index scan would have missed     /// entirely.     #[test]     fn exchange_boundaries_reaches_past_a_thin_intermediate_subdomain() {         let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);         // Node 1 is only 1.0 wide (much thinner than 2*halo=10.0) —         // deliberately adversarial, bypassing rebalance/ensure_valid_for_halo         // entirely, to test exchange_boundaries's own correctness in         // isolation regardless of gap #1's status.         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries(global, vec![30.0, 31.0, 60.0]);         assert_eq!(decomp.sub_domains.len(), 4); // [0,30)[30,31)[31,60)[60,100]         assert!(!decomp.narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "sanity: node 1 must actually be thin for this test to mean anything");         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         // Agent in node 0, within halo (5.0) of node 2's boundary at         // x=31 — but only by reaching straight past thin node 1.         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(9, Point3::new(29.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         assert!(             nodes[2].ghost_agents.iter().any(|a| a.id == 9),             "node 2 must see node 0's agent as a ghost even though node 1 sits between them"         );     }     #[test]     fn handle_migrations_delivers_agent_to_correct_target_and_logs_it() {         let decomp = four_node_decomposition();         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         let agent = Agent::new(9, Point3::new(30.0, 5.0, 5.0), 2.0);         nodes[0].outgoing_migrations.push((agent, 1));         let records = coordinator.handle_migrations(&mut nodes, 4);         assert_eq!(records.len(), 1);         assert_eq!(records[0], MigrationRecord { agent_id: 9, from_node: 0, to_node: 1, tick: 4 });         assert!(nodes[0].outgoing_migrations.is_empty());         assert_eq!(nodes[1].agents.len(), 1);         assert_eq!(nodes[1].agents[0].id, 9);     }     #[test]     fn handle_migrations_handles_many_simultaneous_migrations_without_panicking() {         let decomp = four_node_decomposition();         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         for from in 0..4 {             for to in 0..4 {                 if from == to {                     continue;                 }                 let agent = Agent::new((from * 10 + to) as u64, Point3::new(1.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0);                 nodes[from].outgoing_migrations.push((agent, to));             }         }         let records = coordinator.handle_migrations(&mut nodes, 1);         assert_eq!(records.len(), 12, "4 nodes x 3 targets each = 12 migrations");         assert_eq!(nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum::<usize>(), 12);         for node in &nodes {             assert_eq!(node.agents.len(), 3, "each node should have received one agent from each of the other 3");         }     }     #[test]     fn maybe_rebalance_does_not_flag_a_balanced_cluster() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 2);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         for i in 0..10 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));             nodes[1].agents.push(Agent::new(100 + i, Point3::new(12.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let report = coordinator.maybe_rebalance(&nodes, 1.5);         assert!(!report.needs_rebalance);         assert_eq!(report.imbalance_ratio, 1.0);     }     #[test]     fn maybe_rebalance_flags_a_genuinely_imbalanced_cluster() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 2);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         for i in 0..40 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let report = coordinator.maybe_rebalance(&nodes, 1.5);         assert!(report.needs_rebalance);         assert_eq!(report.max_node, 0);         assert_eq!(report.min_node, 1);         assert_eq!(report.min_count, 0);     }     #[test]     fn stats_reports_per_node_agent_and_ghost_counts() {         let decomp = four_node_decomposition();         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(1, Point3::new(23.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(2, Point3::new(2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         nodes[1].agents.push(Agent::new(3, Point3::new(37.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         coordinator.exchange_boundaries(&mut nodes);         let stats = coordinator.stats(&nodes);         assert_eq!(stats.agent_counts, vec![2, 1, 0, 0]);         assert_eq!(stats.total_agents, 3);         assert_eq!(stats.max_agent_count, 2);         assert_eq!(stats.min_agent_count, 0);         assert_eq!(stats.ghost_counts[1], 1, "node 1 should have exactly one ghost (agent 1, within halo of x=25)");         assert_eq!(stats.max_ghost_overlap, 1);     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_produces_well_separated_layout_for_well_separated_agents() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 4);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         // Span (5.0 to 82.9) wide enough that both count-balance and the         // T3 halo-safety minimum (2*halo=10.0 per sub-domain) are         // simultaneously achievable — see the analogous lib.rs test         // `rebalance_can_remain_imbalanced_when_agents_are_clustered_narrower_than_the_safe_minimum_span`         // for the case where they aren't.         for i in 0..40 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 2.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let total_before: usize = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum();         let (new_nodes, report) = coordinator.rebalance(nodes, 1.5);         assert_eq!(new_nodes.len(), 4);         assert_eq!(new_nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum::<usize>(), total_before);         assert!(!report.needs_rebalance, "well-separated agents should balance evenly across 4 nodes");         assert!(coordinator.decomposition().narrow_subdomains().is_empty());     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_survives_many_agents_at_the_exact_same_position() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 4);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         for i in 0..50 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(37.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let total_before: usize = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum();         let (new_nodes, _report) = coordinator.rebalance(nodes, 1.5);         assert_eq!(             new_nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum::<usize>(),             total_before,             "agent count must be conserved even in this degenerate case"         );         assert!(new_nodes.len() <= 4, "duplicate positions cannot yield more sub-domains than requested");         assert!(coordinator.decomposition().narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "T3: rebalance must never install a narrow sub-domain, even in this degenerate case");     }     /// T3 regression test for the exact bug class gap #1 named: a highly     /// skewed agent distribution that pushes a *naive* median-cut into     /// producing a sub-domain thinner than its own halo diameter. Before     /// the T3 fix, `rebalance` would have installed this directly;     /// now `ensure_valid_for_halo` must intervene.     #[test]     fn rebalance_never_installs_a_subdomain_narrower_than_the_safe_minimum() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 4);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::new(decomp);         // 39 agents tightly clustered right at the start, 1 agent far         // away — a median-cut on this would put three boundaries within         // a couple of units of x=5, each cutting off a near-zero-width         // slab.         for i in 0..39 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.01, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(39, Point3::new(95.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         let total_before: usize = nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum();         let (new_nodes, _report) = coordinator.rebalance(nodes, 1.5);         assert!(coordinator.decomposition().narrow_subdomains().is_empty(), "T3 fix must prevent any narrow sub-domain from being installed");         assert_eq!(new_nodes.iter().map(|n| n.agents.len()).sum::<usize>(), total_before, "agent count must still be conserved");     }     #[test]     fn config_defaults_match_the_t2_hardcoded_behavior() {         let config = CoordinatorConfig::default();         assert_eq!(config.imbalance_threshold, 1.5);         assert_eq!(config.rebalance_interval, 0);         assert_eq!(config.min_subdomain_width_factor, 2.0);     }     #[test]     fn with_config_uses_a_custom_min_subdomain_width_factor() {         let decomp = DomainDecomposition::new(Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]), 4);         let mut nodes = decomp.create_nodes();         let custom = CoordinatorConfig { imbalance_threshold: 1.5, rebalance_interval: 0, min_subdomain_width_factor: 4.0 };         let mut coordinator = OrchestratorCoordinator::with_config(decomp, custom);         for i in 0..40 {             nodes[0].agents.push(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(5.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let (_new_nodes, _report) = coordinator.rebalance(nodes, 1.5);         // With halo=5.0 and factor=4.0, minimum width is 20.0 -> at most         // 5 sub-domains fit in a 100-wide domain, but well within range         // of 4, so it should still succeed at 4 without falling back.         assert!(coordinator.decomposition().narrow_subdomains().is_empty());         for sd in &coordinator.decomposition().sub_domains {             assert!(sd.bounds.max[0] - sd.bounds.min[0] >= 20.0 - 1e-9);         }     } } ``` ## `tests/migration.rs` ```rust //! End-to-end validation of the fixed orchestration pipeline across a //! realistic multi-node cluster: agents start in one node, cross several //! sub-domain boundaries over many steps under constant velocity, and the //! test checks the outcome against an *analytically computed* expected //! final node and migration count — not just "it ran without panicking." use mitonano_orchestrator::agent::Agent; use mitonano_orchestrator::environment::Bounds; use mitonano_orchestrator::FederatedMitoNano; use nalgebra::{Point3, Vector3}; #[test] fn agents_migrate_across_multiple_nodes_to_the_analytically_correct_destination() {     // 5 nodes, each a 20-unit-wide slab along x: [0,20) [20,40) [40,60) [60,80) [80,100].     let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]);     let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(5, global);     let n_agents = 12;     for i in 0..n_agents {         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(1.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));     }     assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), n_agents as usize);     let velocity = Vector3::new(5.0, 0.0, 0.0);     let dt = 1.0;     let n_steps = 15;     for _ in 0..n_steps {         sim.step(dt, velocity);         // Conservation must hold after *every single step*, not just at the end.         assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), n_agents as usize);     }     // Analytically: final x = 1.0 + velocity.x * dt * n_steps = 1.0 + 5.0*1.0*15 = 76.0,     // which falls in slab [60,80) -> node 3.     let final_x = 1.0 + velocity.x * dt * n_steps as f64;     assert_eq!(final_x, 76.0);     let expected_final_node = 3;     for (node_idx, node) in sim.nodes().iter().enumerate() {         if node_idx == expected_final_node {             assert_eq!(node.agents.len(), n_agents as usize, "all agents should have landed in node {expected_final_node}");             for agent in &node.agents {                 assert!((agent.position.x - final_x).abs() < 1e-9);             }         } else {             assert!(node.agents.is_empty(), "node {node_idx} should be empty at the end of this scenario");         }     }     // Each agent crosses exactly 3 boundaries (at x=20, x=40, x=60) on the     // way from x=1 to x=76, so the migration log should have exactly     // 3 * n_agents entries, and each agent's own sub-sequence should be     // the consecutive chain 0->1->2->3 with no skips or repeats.     let log = sim.migration_log();     assert_eq!(log.len(), 3 * n_agents as usize);     for agent_id in 0..n_agents {         let mut agent_records: Vec<_> = log.iter().filter(|r| r.agent_id == agent_id).collect();         agent_records.sort_by_key(|r| r.tick);         assert_eq!(agent_records.len(), 3, "agent {agent_id} should migrate exactly 3 times");         let expected_chain = [(0, 1), (1, 2), (2, 3)];         for (record, &(expected_from, expected_to)) in agent_records.iter().zip(expected_chain.iter()) {             assert_eq!(record.from_node, expected_from);             assert_eq!(record.to_node, expected_to);         }         // Ticks must be strictly increasing (no two migrations for the         // same agent logged at the same tick, and in causal order).         for pair in agent_records.windows(2) {             assert!(pair[0].tick < pair[1].tick);         }     } } #[test] fn imbalanced_migration_is_detected_by_check_balance() {     // 2 nodes; every agent starts in node 0 and never moves far enough to     // migrate, so all load stays concentrated in node 0 — a real     // imbalance the coordinator's check should catch.     let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [20.0, 10.0, 10.0]);     let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(2, global);     for i in 0..40 {         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(1.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));     }     // Small back-and-forth motion: never crosses the x=10 boundary.     for step in 0..10 {         let vx = if step % 2 == 0 { 1.0 } else { -1.0 };         sim.step(0.5, Vector3::new(vx, 0.0, 0.0));     }     assert!(sim.migration_log().is_empty(), "agents in this scenario should never cross the boundary");     let report = sim.check_balance(1.5);     assert!(report.needs_rebalance);     assert_eq!(report.max_node, 0);     assert_eq!(report.min_node, 1);     assert_eq!(report.min_count, 0); } #[test] fn single_node_cluster_behaves_as_a_degenerate_case_with_no_migrations() {     // A 1-node "cluster" should behave exactly like a non-distributed     // simulation: agents move freely, nothing ever migrates (there's     // nowhere else to go), and the balance check trivially reports perfect     // balance.     let global = Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [50.0, 10.0, 10.0]);     let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(1, global);     for i in 0..5 {         sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i, Point3::new(10.0, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));     }     for _ in 0..20 {         sim.step(1.0, Vector3::new(1.0, 0.0, 0.0));     }     assert!(sim.migration_log().is_empty());     assert_eq!(sim.total_agent_count(), 5);     let report = sim.check_balance(1.5);     assert!(!report.needs_rebalance);     assert_eq!(report.imbalance_ratio, 1.0); } ``` ## `tests/property.rs` ```rust //! Property-style tests (T3 "Next Steps" document, section 4: "Testing & //! Verification Upgrades" — "Property-based testing (proptest or //! quickcheck)"), covering exactly the three properties that document //! names: //! //! - Agent count conservation across any rebalance + migrations. //! - Every agent ends in a subdomain that actually contains it. //! - No ghost duplication or loss. //! //! ## Why this isn't actually using `proptest` //! //! `proptest` was added as a dev-dependency and attempted first, as the //! T3 document specifically suggests. It couldn't be made to build in //! this environment: with `proptest = "1.4"` (a caret requirement), //! cargo resolved the newest compatible `1.x` release, whose dependency //! graph pulled in a `getrandom` release requiring Rust's 2024 edition — //! which stock `rustc`/`cargo` 1.75.0 (the version this crate targets; //! see `coordinator.rs`'s module docs) cannot even parse the manifest //! for, let alone compile. Pinning `proptest = "=1.4.0"` exactly, and //! separately pinning `rand`/`getrandom` to older releases directly, //! didn't resolve it either — something else in that version's graph //! still pulled in the newer, incompatible `getrandom`. Rather than pin //! down every transitive version by hand (fragile, and not the point of //! this exercise) or silently drop the property-testing requirement //! entirely, this file implements the same three properties as a //! deterministic, seeded parameter sweep with no new dependency — same //! testing intent (broad, systematic coverage across many generated //! scenarios instead of a handful of hand-picked ones), zero new //! toolchain risk. //! //! The seeded generator below is a small SplitMix64 PRNG — enough to //! generate varied node counts, agent placements, velocities, and step //! counts deterministically and reproducibly (a real proptest integration //! would additionally get automatic shrinking on failure; this doesn't, //! which is the one real capability gap versus the T3 document's original //! suggestion). use mitonano_orchestrator::agent::Agent; use mitonano_orchestrator::environment::Bounds; use mitonano_orchestrator::FederatedMitoNano; use nalgebra::{Point3, Vector3}; /// Minimal SplitMix64 PRNG — deterministic, dependency-free, sufficient /// for generating varied test scenarios. struct SplitMix64 {     state: u64, } impl SplitMix64 {     fn new(seed: u64) -> Self {         SplitMix64 { state: seed }     }     fn next_u64(&mut self) -> u64 {         self.state = self.state.wrapping_add(0x9E3779B97F4A7C15);         let mut z = self.state;         z = (z ^ (z >> 30)).wrapping_mul(0xBF58476D1CE4E5B9);         z = (z ^ (z >> 27)).wrapping_mul(0x94D049BB133111EB);         z ^ (z >> 31)     }     fn range_usize(&mut self, lo: usize, hi_exclusive: usize) -> usize {         lo + (self.next_u64() as usize) % (hi_exclusive - lo)     }     fn range_f64(&mut self, lo: f64, hi: f64) -> f64 {         let unit = (self.next_u64() >> 11) as f64 / (1u64 << 53) as f64; // [0,1)         lo + unit * (hi - lo)     } } fn global_bounds() -> Bounds {     Bounds::new([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], [100.0, 10.0, 10.0]) } const N_CASES: u64 = 200; /// Total agent count must be conserved across any sequence of steps /// (which can trigger migrations) and any rebalance, for any node count /// and any starting placement. #[test] fn agent_count_is_conserved_across_arbitrary_steps_and_rebalances() {     for seed in 0..N_CASES {         let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(seed ^ 0xA11CE);         let num_nodes = rng.range_usize(1, 7);         let n_agents = rng.range_usize(0, 40);         let vx = rng.range_f64(-8.0, 8.0);         let n_steps = rng.range_usize(0, 12);         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(num_nodes, global_bounds());         for i in 0..n_agents {             let x = rng.range_f64(0.0, 100.0);             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i as u64, Point3::new(x, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         let expected = sim.total_agent_count();         for step in 0..n_steps {             sim.step(1.0, Vector3::new(vx, 0.0, 0.0));             assert_eq!(                 sim.total_agent_count(),                 expected,                 "seed={seed} num_nodes={num_nodes} n_agents={n_agents} vx={vx} failed at step {step}"             );         }         if sim.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_some() {             assert_eq!(                 sim.total_agent_count(),                 expected,                 "seed={seed} num_nodes={num_nodes} n_agents={n_agents}: rebalance did not conserve agent count"             );         }     } } /// Every agent must end up in a node whose bounds actually contain it /// (with the one documented half-open-boundary exception carried over /// from the example-based test of the same property in `lib.rs`). #[test] fn every_agent_is_contained_by_its_own_nodes_bounds() {     for seed in 0..N_CASES {         let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(seed ^ 0xB0BA);         let num_nodes = rng.range_usize(1, 7);         let n_agents = rng.range_usize(0, 30);         let vx = rng.range_f64(-8.0, 8.0);         let n_steps = rng.range_usize(0, 10);         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(num_nodes, global_bounds());         for i in 0..n_agents {             let x = rng.range_f64(0.0, 100.0);             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i as u64, Point3::new(x, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         for _ in 0..n_steps {             sim.step(1.0, Vector3::new(vx, 0.0, 0.0));         }         for node in sim.nodes() {             for agent in &node.agents {                 let contained = node.local_bounds.contains(agent.position)                     || (agent.position.x - node.local_bounds.max[0]).abs() < 1e-9;                 assert!(                     contained,                     "seed={seed}: agent {} in node {} at {:?} is outside {:?}",                     agent.id, node.id, agent.position, node.local_bounds                 );             }         }     } } /// A node's `ghost_agents` must never contain the same agent id twice /// (duplication would mean double-counting that agent's contribution /// during force/interaction calculations that consult ghosts). #[test] fn ghost_agents_never_contain_duplicate_ids_within_a_single_node() {     for seed in 0..N_CASES {         let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(seed ^ 0x6407);         let num_nodes = rng.range_usize(2, 7);         let n_agents = rng.range_usize(0, 30);         let mut sim = FederatedMitoNano::new(num_nodes, global_bounds());         for i in 0..n_agents {             let x = rng.range_f64(0.0, 100.0);             sim.add_agent(Agent::new(i as u64, Point3::new(x, 5.0, 5.0), 1.0));         }         // A zero-motion step still runs exchange_boundaries without         // disturbing agent placement, isolating the ghost-population         // logic from migration.         sim.step(0.0, Vector3::new(0.0, 0.0, 0.0));         for node in sim.nodes() {             let mut ids: Vec<u64> = node.ghost_agents.iter().map(|a| a.id).collect();             let before = ids.len();             ids.sort_unstable();             ids.dedup();             assert_eq!(ids.len(), before, "seed={seed}: node {} has a duplicate ghost agent id", node.id);         }     } } ``` # mitonano_federated ## `Cargo.toml` ```toml [package] name = "mitonano_federated" version = "0.1.0" edition = "2021" [dependencies] mitonano_core = { path = "../mitonano_core" } rand = "=0.8.5" ``` ## `src/lib.rs` ```rust //! # mitonano_federated //! //! Branch B: the actual integration of `mitonano_core`'s real physics //! engine (`UnifiedEngine` — bounded SDE motion, field release/uptake, //! agent-agent exchange, field diffusion) with `mitonano_orchestrator`'s //! domain-decomposition approach. Every node in [`FederatedUnifiedEngine`] //! runs a genuine, persistent `UnifiedEngine` instance — not the //! `LocalEngine`/toy-`Agent` stand-in `mitonano_orchestrator`'s own //! `node.rs` uses (and is explicit in its own docs about being a //! stand-in for exactly this). //! //! ## Why this is a new crate rather than a change to `mitonano_orchestrator` //! //! `mitonano_orchestrator` has 61 passing tests built against its own //! `Agent` (nalgebra `Point3` + `id` + `payload`) and `MitoNode` //! (`LocalEngine` stand-in). `mitonano_core::agent::Agent` is a different, //! independently-validated type (`[f64; 3]` position, no `id`, real SDE //! physics) whose shape is fixed by `unified_engine.rs`'s verbatim call //! sites — it can't be made identical to the orchestrator's `Agent` //! without breaking one side or the other. Rather than force a merge that //! would either break 61 already-verified orchestrator tests or bend //! `mitonano_core`'s validated shape to fit them, this crate re-implements //! the *coordination logic* — domain decomposition, migration, per-tick //! orchestration — fresh, against real `mitonano_core` types directly. //! `mitonano_orchestrator` remains untouched and fully passing as the //! validated proof of the orchestration logic in isolation; //! `mitonano_core` remains untouched and fully passing as the validated //! proof of the physics in isolation; this crate is what proves they //! *compose* correctly. //! //! ## What's proven here that neither `mitonano_core` nor //! `mitonano_orchestrator` proves alone //! //! `mitonano_core::unified_engine`'s own tests prove mass conservation //! for a single, non-distributed engine. `mitonano_orchestrator`'s tests //! prove agent-count conservation for toy agents with no real physics. //! Neither proves that splitting the real physics across multiple //! independently-stepped engines, with agents migrating between them and //! genuinely interacting *across* node boundaries mid-simulation, still //! conserves mass end to end. That's exactly what `tests/conservation.rs` //! checks: real SDE motion, real field coupling, real agent-agent //! exchange (both within and across nodes), real diffusion (both within //! and across nodes), and real migration, all simultaneously active, //! still satisfying the same bookkeeping identity //! `total_combined_mass(t) == C0 - cumulative_eliminated(t)` that //! `mitonano_core`'s own strongest single-engine test checks — now summed //! across every node's engine and field. //! //! ## Revision history //! //! **T1** (first integration pass): every node ran a real `UnifiedEngine` //! via its own `run()` call, but agent-agent interaction and field //! diffusion were each strictly node-local — an agent near a seam did not //! exchange payload with, or diffuse mass toward, anything on the other //! side of it. Two gaps, both documented and left open rather than //! hidden. //! //! **T3** (this revision) closes every gap T2 left open, plus one //! `mitonano_core`-level gap from an earlier roadmap review, without //! modifying the verbatim `unified_engine.rs` at all: //! //! - **Closed-form validation for pairwise interaction.** Agent motion //!   and field diffusion were already checked against independently- //!   derivable theory (`mitonano_core::tests::closed_form_physics`); //!   pairwise agent-agent exchange had only ever been checked via //!   conservation and antisymmetry. That file's new //!   `two_agent_pairwise_exchange_matches_the_closed_form_exponential_relaxation` //!   test closes that gap. //! - **Saturable (Michaelis-Menten) uptake**, via `mitonano_core::kinetics` //!   — an optional replacement for `unified_engine.rs`'s linear //!   `k_uptake * c * dt` formula, substituted at this crate's own `step` //!   call site (`FederatedEngineConfig::saturable_uptake`), since the //!   verbatim file's inline formula can't itself be changed. `None` //!   preserves the original linear behavior exactly. //! - **Chemotaxis**, via `mitonano_core::chemotaxis` — an optional //!   gradient-biased drift, substituted the same way //!   (`FederatedEngineConfig::chemotaxis`). `None` preserves the original //!   fixed-drift behavior exactly. //! - **Rebalancing.** `check_balance` / `rebalance_if_needed` port //!   `mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator`'s proven median-cut algorithm //!   directly against `mitonano_core` types (see `rebalance`'s own docs //!   for why this is a port rather than a shared call, and for the real, //!   new problem this crate's version had to solve that the //!   orchestrator's never did: every node here also carries a real //!   `ConcentrationField`, so a rebalance must conservatively remap field //!   mass across the new slab boundaries too, not just relocate agents). //! - **Ghost-agent candidate filtering** (from a `mitonano_core` roadmap //!   review, not T2 itself): cross-node interaction now pre-filters each //!   side down to boundary-adjacent candidates before the pairwise //!   distance check, instead of a full `O(agents_i × agents_j)` cross //!   product — see `boundary_candidates_ge`/`boundary_candidates_le`'s //!   docs. A pure work-reduction refactor; `tests/equivalence.rs`, //!   left completely unmodified throughout, re-verifies the result is //!   bit-for-bit unaffected. //! //! **T2** (prior revision) fixed the two gaps T1 left open: //! //! - **Cross-node agent-agent interaction.** `step` no longer calls //!   `UnifiedEngine::run` as an atomic black box — it reimplements that //!   method's exact per-tick sequence directly (see `step`'s own docs for //!   the full phase breakdown), which is what makes it possible to insert //!   a genuine cross-node interaction phase between the local-interaction //!   phase and the diffusion phase. Every node pair whose slabs are //!   within `interaction.radius` of each other now has its agents //!   actually exchange payload across the seam, using the identical //!   `k_exchange * (payload_j - payload_i) * dt` formula //!   `mitonano_core::interaction` uses locally, computed from a snapshot //!   taken before any interaction phase mutates payload — exactly //!   conservative, order-independent, the same antisymmetry guarantee //!   `mitonano_core::interaction`'s own docs describe. //! - **Cross-node field diffusion.** `mitonano_core::concentration` grew //!   two new methods, `boundary_mass` and `add_boundary_mass` (see their //!   docs), specifically so two adjacent nodes' fields could exchange //!   mass across their shared seam using the *exact same* //!   `(mass_a - mass_b) / spacing^2` formula the interior diffusion //!   scheme already uses internally — not a different, bolted-on formula //!   at the boundary, which would have made the scheme numerically //!   discontinuous right at every node seam. //! //! **T1** (first integration pass): every node ran a real `UnifiedEngine` //! via its own `run()` call, but agent-agent interaction and field //! diffusion were each strictly node-local — an agent near a seam did not //! exchange payload with, or diffuse mass toward, anything on the other //! side of it. Two gaps, both documented and left open rather than //! hidden. //! //! **T4** (this revision) adds the first genuinely new *behavior* rather //! than a fix or an optimization: stochastic agent fission (division) and //! fusion (merging), via `mitonano_core::population`. Like T3's //! `saturable_uptake` and `chemotaxis`, these are opt-in //! (`FederatedEngineConfig::fission` / `::fusion`, both `None` by //! default, preserving every prior revision's behavior exactly) and can //! only exist at this crate's own `step` call site, not inside //! `unified_engine.rs`'s `run` — that method owns a fixed-length //! `Vec<Agent>` for the duration of a call and has no mechanism to insert //! or remove elements mid-run. See `step`'s own docs (phases 5 and 6) for //! the exact mechanics — payload conservation is exact by construction in //! both directions (`split_payload`/`merge_payload`, see //! `mitonano_core::population`'s own docs), and the standard "Poisson //! process fires at least once in `dt`" formula //! (`1 - exp(-rate*dt)`) is used for the fire/no-fire decision rather //! than the small-`dt` linear approximation used elsewhere in this //! codebase's uptake formula, specifically because fission/fusion rates //! are more likely to be configured large enough relative to `dt` that //! the approximation's error (and its potential to exceed a valid //! probability of 1.0) would actually matter. //! //! **T5** (this revision) adds two-state (mobile/bound) kinetics — the //! mechanism behind "anomalous transport via trapping" — via //! `mitonano_core::mobility`. Like T4's fission/fusion, this is opt-in //! (`FederatedEngineConfig::two_state`, `None` by default, preserving //! every prior revision's behavior exactly) and can only exist at this //! crate's own `step` call site: `Agent::advance_position` was made //! mobility-aware (a no-op when `Bound`) without disturbing its //! signature or its behavior for the `Mobile` default every existing //! caller — `unified_engine.rs` included — already uses, but nothing //! decides *when* to switch state without a caller like this one, since //! nothing in the verbatim file ever calls //! `mitonano_core::mobility::maybe_toggle_mobility`. See `step`'s own //! docs (now folded into phase 1, immediately before motion, so //! `advance_position` sees this tick's current state) and //! `mitonano_core::mobility`'s own module docs for the model and its //! exact closed-form validation (an ensemble initialized at the two-state //! Markov chain's stationary distribution has MSD growing as exactly //! `diffusion_coeff^2 * p_mobile_eq * T` at *every* T, not just //! asymptotically — a stronger, cleaner closed form than the more //! commonly described "short-time vs long-time crossover," which this //! revision also checks qualitatively as a complementary, non-stationary //! scenario). //! //! **T6** (this revision) adds heterogeneous agent populations — //! biological roadmap item #3: real populations aren't homogeneous //! (healthy vs. damaged mitochondria have different clearance rates, //! different nanoparticle formulations have different diffusion/uptake //! profiles). `mitonano_core::agent::Agent` gained a `phenotype: //! Option<AgentParams>` field and an `effective_params` resolver //! (override if set, otherwise a caller-supplied fallback); `step`'s //! phase 1 now resolves each agent's own effective params first and uses //! them as the base for *both* motion and decay/release, instead of //! every agent in a node sharing exactly the one engine-wide //! `AgentParams` that's all `unified_engine.rs`'s verbatim `run` has ever //! supported. As with every extension since T3, this required checking //! — not assuming — that adding a new `Agent` field couldn't break //! verbatim compatibility: `unified_engine.rs` only ever constructs //! agents via `Agent::new(position, payload)` (five call sites, all //! 2-argument, confirmed directly) and never via struct-literal syntax //! naming fields, so a new field defaulting to `None` in `Agent::new` is //! safe, the same reasoning T5's `mobility` field relied on. //! //! T6 also composes phenotypes directly with T4's fission, exactly as the //! roadmap review that motivated this feature described: a fission //! daughter inherits the parent's `phenotype` by default, and //! `FissionParams` gained an optional `mutation: Option<MutationParams>` //! — with some probability per division event (not a per-tick Poisson //! rate; mutation is checked once per fission event), the new daughter's //! phenotype diverges to a caller-supplied `damaged_phenotype` instead of //! inheriting, while the parent itself is left untouched. This is the //! natural mechanism for modeling mitophagy-style quality control //! (damaged mitochondria arising stochastically through division, then //! subject to their own, typically faster, elimination dynamics) without //! hardcoding any specific biological meaning into the library itself — //! `damaged_phenotype` is just an `AgentParams` a caller supplies. //! //! **T7** (this revision) closes every remaining gap from T6's list //! except the structural one (see below, which explains why that one is //! a deliberate exception, not an oversight): //! //! - **Gap 2, cross-node fusion.** Phase 6 gained a second pass, //!   immediately after the existing node-local one, structured like phase //!   3's cross-node interaction from the start (snapshot-based, //!   early-break node-pair scan, boundary-candidate prefiltering) rather //!   than needing a T3-style follow-up optimization later. Sequential //!   composition with the local pass — not simultaneous — means an agent //!   consumed locally is already gone by the time the cross-node pass //!   runs, so "at most one fusion per agent per tick" holds automatically //!   across both passes with no extra bookkeeping. //! - **Gap 3, per-agent uptake heterogeneity.** `mitonano_core::agent::Agent` //!   gained `uptake_override: Option<mitonano_core::kinetics::UptakeKinetics>` //!   (a new enum unifying the linear and saturable formulas behind one //!   type, resolved by the new `uptake_amount` function) — the uptake //!   analogue of T6's `phenotype`, kept as a separate field rather than //!   folded into `phenotype` itself since `k_uptake`/`saturable_uptake` //!   live at the `FederatedEngineConfig` level, outside `AgentParams` //!   entirely. //! - **Gap 4, spatially heterogeneous field diffusion.** //!   `mitonano_core::concentration::ConcentrationField` gained //!   `step_heterogeneous`, a separate method (not a change to `step`'s //!   existing verbatim-compatible signature) taking a per-cell //!   diffusivity array and using the harmonic mean — not the arithmetic //!   mean — at each interface, the physically correct choice for a //!   discontinuous diffusivity (it's what makes a `D=0` cell act as a //!   true impermeable barrier, which the arithmetic mean would not do; //!   see that method's own docs and //!   `tests::step_heterogeneous_with_a_zero_diffusivity_cell_blocks_all_flux_through_it` //!   for the direct, exact check backing that choice). `FederatedEngineConfig` //!   gained `heterogeneous_diffusion: Option<fn([f64; 3]) -> f64>` — a //!   plain function pointer, not a closure, specifically so this field //!   stays `Copy` like the rest of the struct — sampled once per cell at //!   construction (and re-sampled for new slab boundaries whenever //!   `rebalance` runs). Cross-node diffusion coupling (T2's phase 4) was //!   updated to use each side's own boundary-layer diffusivity, //!   harmonic-mean-averaged at the seam the same way an ordinary interior //!   face is — a heterogeneous profile is treated identically whether a //!   given interface happens to sit inside one node or be split across //!   two. //! - **Gap 5, source/sink boundary conditions.** //!   `ConcentrationField` gained `inject_at_face`/`drain_at_face` and a //!   new `Face` enum (all six faces of a field's own grid, broader than //!   `XFace`, which only ever needed x). `mitonano_federated` added its //!   own `GlobalFace` — distinct from `Face` specifically to capture a //!   fact `Face` alone can't express: whether a given face is genuinely //!   part of the *global* domain boundary, or just an internal //!   node-to-node seam. `YMin`/`YMax`/`ZMin`/`ZMax` are the global //!   boundary for every node (this crate never decomposes along y or z); //!   `XMin`/`XMax` are the global boundary only for the first and last //!   node respectively. `FederatedEngineConfig::boundary_conditions` is a //!   fixed-size `[Option<BoundaryCondition>; 6]` (not a `Vec`, again to //!   stay `Copy`), applied in a new phase 4b that checks each node's own //!   `id` against the cluster's edges before applying anything — see //!   `step`'s own docs. `FederatedTickStats` gained `cumulative_injected`/ //!   `cumulative_drained`; with a source or sink configured, //!   `total_combined_mass` is no longer conserved on its own, and the //!   richer identity `total_combined_mass(t) == C0 + injected(t) - //!   drained(t) - eliminated(t)` is what holds exactly instead — checked //!   directly in `tests/conservation.rs`'s //!   `global_bookkeeping_identity_survives_every_gap_closed_alongside_everything_else`, //!   the same test that also exercises every other gap (2-6, T1-T7) all //!   simultaneously, closing this revision the same way every previous //!   one closed: not just "it compiles" but "the whole thing still adds //!   up under the most complex conditions this crate can construct." //! //! ## Gap 1 — deliberately not attempted this pass, and why //! //! Reconciling `mitonano_core::Agent` and `mitonano_orchestrator::Agent` //! into one shared type remains the one item left open, and it's worth //! being precise about *why*, rather than leaving it as an unexamined //! leftover. //! //! The two types are irreconcilable as they stand for a reason baked in //! from the start of this whole project: `mitonano_core::Agent::new` //! takes exactly `(position: [f64; 3], payload: f64)` — verified directly //! against verbatim `unified_engine.rs`'s five call sites every time this //! project has added a new `Agent` field (T5's `mobility`, T6's //! `phenotype`, T7's `uptake_override`), specifically to confirm each //! addition wouldn't break that file. `mitonano_orchestrator::Agent` //! carries a stable `id: u64` and a `nalgebra::Point3<f64>` position, //! neither of which fits into `mitonano_core::Agent`'s constructor //! without changing its signature — which would break the one file this //! entire project has kept byte-identical to the original upload since //! before `mitonano_orchestrator` even existed. True unification, in //! either direction, requires giving up one of two things this project //! has treated as load-bearing throughout: `unified_engine.rs`'s verbatim //! status, or `mitonano_orchestrator`'s 61 tests staying untouched and //! independently valid (a nalgebra-`Point3`-and-`id`-based `Agent` would //! need to become an array-based, id-less one throughout `mitonano_orchestrator`'s //! `Bounds`, domain decomposition, `node.rs`, and `coordinator.rs` — not //! a mechanical rename, a rewrite of the type every one of those 61 tests //! is built against). //! //! What T3's `rebalance` and this revision's cross-node fusion both //! demonstrate is that the *thing actually blocking code reuse* was never //! really the type mismatch itself — every algorithm that mattered //! (median-cut rebalancing, boundary-candidate-filtered cross-node //! pairing) turned out to be directly portable against //! `mitonano_core::Agent` once actually attempted, with no dependency on //! `mitonano_orchestrator`'s specific type. What a shared type would //! still buy, at this point, is narrower than it first appeared: less //! duplicated code, and `mitonano_orchestrator`'s `ensure_valid_for_halo` //! safety net as a bonus (which, per T3's own docs, this crate's //! couplings don't actually need for correctness, since cross-node //! interaction already handles arbitrary gaps and diffusion is //! nearest-neighbor by construction). Given that narrower payoff against //! the real cost — rewriting a 61-test crate's core type or breaking this //! project's one verbatim-compatibility guarantee — the honest call is //! that this isn't worth doing as an incremental addition to an existing //! revision. It would be its own project, scoped and reasoned about on //! its own terms, not a gap to casually close alongside four much //! smaller, safely additive ones. //! //! ## What's still open after T7 //! //! Gap 1, as just described. Beyond that, everything from the original //! physics and biological-enhancements roadmap reviews has been //! addressed in some form across T1 through T7. //! //! ## The coupling is exact, not merely approximately conservative — //! verified directly, not assumed //! //! Both cross-node couplings (T2) are linear, additive corrections //! computed from a single pre-tick snapshot shared with each node's own //! local pass — the same structure `mitonano_core::interaction`'s //! antisymmetric formula and `mitonano_core::concentration`'s interior //! flux formula already use internally. That structure means a federated //! split isn't just *conservative* relative to one monolithic engine over //! the combined domain — it's *identical* to it, term for term, not an //! approximation with a bounded error. This isn't asserted from the //! algebra alone; `tests/equivalence.rs` checks it directly, isolating //! each coupling mechanism (field diffusion across a seam; agent //! interaction across multiple seams) and comparing a federated run //! cell-for-cell / agent-for-agent against a monolithic `UnifiedEngine` //! over the same combined domain, matching to 1e-9 over hundreds of //! ticks — not merely "total mass conserved," but "produces the same //! field profile and the same per-agent payloads a single engine would //! have produced." Rebalancing (T3) is a different kind of operation — //! not a per-tick physics correction but a one-shot repartition — so it's //! checked differently: directly, for exact mass conservation, in //! `tests::rebalance_conserves_total_combined_mass_exactly` and //! `tests/conservation.rs`'s //! `global_bookkeeping_identity_survives_periodic_rebalancing_during_a_real_run`. use mitonano_core::agent::{Agent, AgentParams}; use mitonano_core::chemotaxis::{chemotactic_drift, ChemotaxisParams}; use mitonano_core::concentration::{ConcentrationField, Face, XFace}; use mitonano_core::environment::Bounds; use mitonano_core::harmonic_mean; use mitonano_core::interaction::{step_agent_interactions, InteractionParams}; use mitonano_core::kinetics::{saturable_uptake_amount, uptake_amount, SaturableUptakeParams}; use mitonano_core::mobility::{maybe_toggle_mobility, TwoStateParams}; use mitonano_core::orchestration::SpatialIndex; use mitonano_core::population::{event_fires, merge_payload, mutation_fires, split_payload, FissionParams, FusionParams}; use mitonano_core::unified_engine::UnifiedEngine; use rand::Rng; /// Indices of every position whose x-coordinate is `>= threshold` — /// candidates for reaching *rightward* across a boundary. A pure, /// directly unit-testable building block for phase 3's cross-node /// interaction candidate filtering (see `step`'s docs): exists /// specifically so "this actually narrows the candidate set, not just /// adds bookkeeping overhead" can be checked deterministically (see /// `tests::boundary_candidate_filters_shrink_a_spread_out_population`), /// rather than only inferred from wall-clock timing, which would be /// unreliable in a shared/sandboxed environment. fn boundary_candidates_ge(positions: &[[f64; 3]], threshold: f64) -> Vec<usize> {     (0..positions.len()).filter(|&i| positions[i][0] >= threshold).collect() } /// Indices of every position whose x-coordinate is `<= threshold` — /// candidates for reaching *leftward* across a boundary. See /// `boundary_candidates_ge`'s docs; this is its mirror image. fn boundary_candidates_le(positions: &[[f64; 3]], threshold: f64) -> Vec<usize> {     (0..positions.len()).filter(|&i| positions[i][0] <= threshold).collect() } /// Extracts one x-layer's values (at fixed `ix`) from a flat per-cell /// array in `mitonano_core::ConcentrationField`'s own `ix + iy*nx + /// iz*nx*ny` layout, in `iy + iz*ny` order — the same layer-ordering /// convention `ConcentrationField::boundary_mass` already uses, so the /// result lines up index-for-index with `boundary_mass`'s own output /// (both phase 4's cross-node diffusion coupling and its tests rely on /// this alignment). fn extract_x_layer(values: &[f64], nx: usize, ny: usize, nz: usize, ix: usize) -> Vec<f64> {     let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(ny * nz);     for iz in 0..nz {         for iy in 0..ny {             out.push(values[ix + iy * nx + iz * nx * ny]);         }     }     out } /// Snapshot of per-node agent-count balance, returned by both /// `check_balance` (read-only) and `rebalance_if_needed` (post-repartition /// result) — same shape and semantics as /// `mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator::RebalanceReport`. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct RebalanceReport {     pub needs_rebalance: bool,     pub max_node: usize,     pub min_node: usize,     pub max_count: usize,     pub min_count: usize,     pub imbalance_ratio: f64, } /// Audit record for one agent crossing from one node's engine into /// another's, analogous to `mitonano_orchestrator::migration::MigrationRecord`. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct MigrationRecord {     pub agent_id: u64,     pub from_node: usize,     pub to_node: usize,     pub tick: u64, } /// Aggregated mass-bookkeeping snapshot across every node, summing each /// node's own `UnifiedTickStats`-shaped quantities — the distributed /// analogue of `mitonano_core::unified_engine::UnifiedTickStats`. /// /// With `boundary_conditions` configured (gap 5), `total_combined_mass` /// is no longer conserved on its own — mass can now enter or leave at the /// global domain boundary, not just via `unified_engine.rs`'s own /// elimination pathway. The richer identity that *does* still hold /// exactly is `total_combined_mass(t) == C0 + cumulative_injected(t) - /// cumulative_drained(t) - cumulative_eliminated(t)` — see /// `tests/conservation.rs`'s /// `global_bookkeeping_identity_survives_source_sink_boundary_conditions_alongside_everything_else` /// for the direct check. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct FederatedTickStats {     pub tick: u64,     pub total_agent_payload: f64,     pub total_field_mass: f64,     pub total_combined_mass: f64,     pub cumulative_eliminated: f64,     pub cumulative_injected: f64,     pub cumulative_drained: f64, } /// One of the six faces of the *global* simulation domain — gap 5 from /// the physics roadmap review: source/sink boundary conditions ("real /// biological systems usually have continuous sources — a capillary /// supplying fresh mass — and sinks — lymphatic drainage"). /// /// Distinct from `mitonano_core::concentration::Face`, which identifies a /// face of one node's own local grid without any notion of whether that /// face is actually part of the true global boundary or just an internal /// node-to-node seam. `GlobalFace` exists specifically to make that /// distinction explicit: `YMin`/`YMax`/`ZMin`/`ZMax` are the global /// boundary for *every* node (this crate's domain decomposition never /// splits along y or z), but `XMin`/`XMax` are the global boundary only /// for the very first and very last node respectively — every other /// node's x-faces are internal seams, and applying a source/sink there /// would inject or drain mass at a fictitious boundary, silently breaking /// the mass-conservation identity every other test in this crate checks. /// See `step`'s own docs for exactly how this distinction is enforced. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum GlobalFace {     XMin,     XMax,     YMin,     YMax,     ZMin,     ZMax, } impl GlobalFace {     /// The index into `FederatedEngineConfig::boundary_conditions` (a     /// fixed-size `[Option<BoundaryCondition>; 6]`, not a `Vec` — see     /// that field's own docs for why) corresponding to this face.     pub fn index(self) -> usize {         match self {             GlobalFace::XMin => 0,             GlobalFace::XMax => 1,             GlobalFace::YMin => 2,             GlobalFace::YMax => 3,             GlobalFace::ZMin => 4,             GlobalFace::ZMax => 5,         }     }     fn to_core_face(self) -> Face {         match self {             GlobalFace::XMin => Face::XMin,             GlobalFace::XMax => Face::XMax,             GlobalFace::YMin => Face::YMin,             GlobalFace::YMax => Face::YMax,             GlobalFace::ZMin => Face::ZMin,             GlobalFace::ZMax => Face::ZMax,         }     } } /// A source, a sink, or both, at one `GlobalFace`. `injection_rate` (mass /// per unit time) and `drain_rate` (a first-order clearance fraction per /// unit time) are independent — a face can have either, both, or (as /// `Default` gives, all-`None`) neither. See /// `mitonano_core::concentration::ConcentrationField::inject_at_face` / /// `::drain_at_face` for the exact formulas applied. #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Default)] pub struct BoundaryCondition {     pub injection_rate: Option<f64>,     pub drain_rate: Option<f64>, } /// One node's slice of the federated simulation: a real, persistent /// `UnifiedEngine` plus that node's local x-slab bounds (for migration /// detection) and a parallel `agent_ids` list (since /// `mitonano_core::Agent` carries no id — see module docs — identity is /// tracked here, alongside `engine.agents`, index-for-index, and moved /// together on every push/`swap_remove`). pub struct FederatedNode {     pub id: usize,     pub local_bounds: Bounds,     pub engine: UnifiedEngine,     pub agent_ids: Vec<u64>,     /// Precomputed per-cell diffusion coefficients for this node's field     /// (one entry per cell, same flattened layout `ConcentrationField`     /// itself uses internally), if `FederatedEngineConfig::heterogeneous_diffusion`     /// was configured — `None` means this node's field uses the single     /// uniform `field_diffusion_coeff` scalar exactly as before. Sampled     /// once at construction (see `FederatedUnifiedEngine::new`), not     /// re-evaluated every tick — the profile is fixed in space for the     /// lifetime of the node, matching how tissue compartments don't     /// typically move.     pub diffusion_coeff_field: Option<Vec<f64>>, } /// The distributed simulation: `num_nodes` [`FederatedNode`]s, each /// running a real `UnifiedEngine` over an equal-width x-slab of /// `global_bounds`, with agents migrating between adjacent nodes' /// engines as their real SDE motion carries them across a slab boundary. pub struct FederatedUnifiedEngine {     pub global_bounds: Bounds,     pub nodes: Vec<FederatedNode>,     pub k_uptake: f64,     pub tick: u64,     saturable_uptake: Option<SaturableUptakeParams>,     chemotaxis: Option<ChemotaxisParams>,     fission: Option<FissionParams>,     fusion: Option<FusionParams>,     two_state: Option<TwoStateParams>,     heterogeneous_diffusion: Option<fn([f64; 3]) -> f64>,     boundary_conditions: [Option<BoundaryCondition>; 6],     next_generated_id: u64,     field_grid: (usize, usize, usize),     index_grid: (usize, usize, usize),     cumulative_eliminated: f64,     cumulative_injected: f64,     cumulative_drained: f64,     migration_log: Vec<MigrationRecord>, } /// Everything needed to construct one node's `UnifiedEngine`, common to /// every node in the cluster (same physics parameters everywhere — only /// the spatial slab and, therefore, the field's extent differ node to /// node). #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)] pub struct FederatedEngineConfig {     pub agent_params: AgentParams,     pub interaction: InteractionParams,     pub field_diffusion_coeff: f64,     pub dt: f64,     pub k_uptake: f64,     /// Per-node `ConcentrationField` grid resolution (that node's local     /// slab, not the global domain).     pub field_grid: (usize, usize, usize),     /// Per-node `SpatialIndex` grid resolution (also local-slab-sized).     pub index_grid: (usize, usize, usize),     /// When `Some`, replaces the linear `k_uptake * c * dt` uptake     /// formula with `mitonano_core::kinetics::saturable_uptake_amount`     /// (`k_uptake` is then ignored). `None` preserves the original linear     /// formula exactly. See `mitonano_core::kinetics`'s module docs for     /// why this can only be substituted at this call site and not inside     /// `UnifiedEngine::run` itself.     pub saturable_uptake: Option<SaturableUptakeParams>,     /// When `Some`, augments each agent's drift with     /// `mitonano_core::chemotaxis::chemotactic_drift` (local field     /// gradient scaled by `coeff`) before every motion step. `None`     /// preserves the original fixed-drift behavior exactly. See     /// `mitonano_core::chemotaxis`'s module docs for why this can only be     /// substituted at this call site and not inside `Agent::advance_position`     /// itself.     pub chemotaxis: Option<ChemotaxisParams>,     /// When `Some`, each agent independently rolls     /// `mitonano_core::population::event_fires` every tick and splits in     /// two if it fires (see `step`'s own docs for the fission phase).     /// `None` (the default) means the population never grows —     /// `unified_engine.rs`'s own tick loop owns a fixed-length `Vec<Agent>`     /// for the duration of a `run` call and has no way to insert     /// elements mid-run, so this, like `saturable_uptake` and     /// `chemotaxis`, can only exist at this call site.     pub fission: Option<FissionParams>,     /// When `Some`, qualifying near-agent pairs (within `radius`) may     /// merge each tick (see `step`'s own docs for the fusion phase).     /// `None` (the default) means the population never shrinks by     /// fusion (it can still shrink via `unified_engine.rs`'s own     /// elimination pathway, which is unaffected by this).     pub fusion: Option<FusionParams>,     /// When `Some`, every agent independently rolls     /// `mitonano_core::mobility::maybe_toggle_mobility` every tick,     /// possibly switching between `Mobile` and `Bound` (see `step`'s own     /// docs for exactly where this happens relative to motion). `None`     /// (the default) means every agent stays `Mobile` forever — the     /// same behavior as before this field existed, since `Agent::new`     /// already defaults to `Mobile` and nothing else in this crate     /// changes that unless this field says to.     pub two_state: Option<TwoStateParams>,     /// Gap 4 from the physics roadmap review: real tissue isn't spatially     /// uniform in its diffusivity (cytoplasm vs. extracellular matrix vs.     /// vesicle lumen). When `Some(f)`, every node's field diffuses with a     /// per-cell coefficient sampled from `f` once at construction (`f`     /// applied to each cell's center position, in *global* coordinates —     /// the same function works correctly regardless of which node a cell     /// belongs to), via     /// `mitonano_core::concentration::ConcentrationField::step_heterogeneous`,     /// instead of the single uniform `field_diffusion_coeff` scalar every     /// node otherwise shares. A plain function pointer (not a closure),     /// so this field can stay `Copy` like the rest of this struct — see     /// `step`'s own docs for how this also affects cross-node diffusion     /// coupling, not just each node's own interior.     pub heterogeneous_diffusion: Option<fn([f64; 3]) -> f64>,     /// Gap 5: source/sink boundary conditions. Indexed by     /// `GlobalFace::index()` (a fixed-size array, not a `Vec`, so this     /// field stays `Copy`) — `None` at a given index means no source or     /// sink on that face. See `GlobalFace`'s own docs for why only     /// `YMin`/`YMax`/`ZMin`/`ZMax` apply to every node while     /// `XMin`/`XMax` apply only to the first/last node respectively.     pub boundary_conditions: [Option<BoundaryCondition>; 6], } impl FederatedUnifiedEngine {     pub fn new(num_nodes: usize, global_bounds: Bounds, config: FederatedEngineConfig) -> Self {         assert!(num_nodes > 0, "FederatedUnifiedEngine::new requires at least one node");         let dx = global_bounds.width(0) / num_nodes as f64;         let mut nodes = Vec::with_capacity(num_nodes);         for i in 0..num_nodes {             let min_x = global_bounds.min[0] + i as f64 * dx;             let max_x = if i == num_nodes - 1 { global_bounds.max[0] } else { min_x + dx };             let local_bounds =                 Bounds::new([min_x, global_bounds.min[1], global_bounds.min[2]], [max_x, global_bounds.max[1], global_bounds.max[2]]);             let (fnx, fny, fnz) = config.field_grid;             let field = ConcentrationField::new(local_bounds, fnx, fny, fnz);             let (inx, iny, inz) = config.index_grid;             let index = SpatialIndex::new(local_bounds, inx, iny, inz);             // Gap 4: if a spatial diffusion-coefficient profile is             // configured, sample it once per cell (at each cell's             // center, in global coordinates) and keep the resulting             // per-cell array for the lifetime of the node — see             // `FederatedNode::diffusion_coeff_field`'s own docs for why             // this is precomputed rather than re-evaluated every tick.             let diffusion_coeff_field = config.heterogeneous_diffusion.map(|f| {                 let cell_size = field.cell_size();                 let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(fnx * fny * fnz);                 for iz in 0..fnz {                     for iy in 0..fny {                         for ix in 0..fnx {                             let x = local_bounds.min[0] + (ix as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[0];                             let y = local_bounds.min[1] + (iy as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[1];                             let z = local_bounds.min[2] + (iz as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[2];                             values.push(f([x, y, z]));                         }                     }                 }                 values             });             // Motion is clamped against the *global* domain (agents must             // be free to cross local slab seams — only the true outer             // edge should constrain their SDE motion, mirroring the same             // reasoning `mitonano_orchestrator`'s own node.rs applies to             // its toy agents).             let engine = UnifiedEngine::new(                 Vec::new(),                 config.agent_params,                 global_bounds,                 field,                 config.field_diffusion_coeff,                 index,                 config.interaction,                 config.dt,             );
