            nodes.push(FederatedNode { id: i, local_bounds, engine, agent_ids: Vec::new(), diffusion_coeff_field });         }         FederatedUnifiedEngine {             global_bounds,             nodes,             k_uptake: config.k_uptake,             tick: 0,             saturable_uptake: config.saturable_uptake,             chemotaxis: config.chemotaxis,             next_generated_id: config.fission.map(|f| f.next_generated_id).unwrap_or(0),             fission: config.fission,             fusion: config.fusion,             two_state: config.two_state,             heterogeneous_diffusion: config.heterogeneous_diffusion,             boundary_conditions: config.boundary_conditions,             field_grid: config.field_grid,             index_grid: config.index_grid,             cumulative_eliminated: 0.0,             cumulative_injected: 0.0,             cumulative_drained: 0.0,             migration_log: Vec::new(),         }     }     pub fn total_agent_count(&self) -> usize {         self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.len()).sum()     }     pub fn migration_log(&self) -> &[MigrationRecord] {         &self.migration_log     }     /// The id of the node whose local slab contains `x`, or `None` if `x`     /// is outside the global domain. Nudges a coordinate that exactly     /// equals the *global* x-max inward by an epsilon before the lookup —     /// the same fix `mitonano_orchestrator`'s own `domain_containing` bug     /// required (see that crate's `domain.rs` module docs), applied here     /// from the start rather than rediscovered the hard way a second     /// time.     fn node_for_x(&self, x: f64) -> Option<usize> {         const EPS: f64 = 1e-9;         let adjusted = if (x - self.global_bounds.max[0]).abs() < EPS { self.global_bounds.max[0] - EPS } else { x };         self.nodes.iter().find(|n| adjusted >= n.local_bounds.min[0] && adjusted < n.local_bounds.max[0]).map(|n| n.id)     }     /// Adds an agent with a caller-supplied `id` (see module docs on why     /// identity is tracked here rather than in `mitonano_core::Agent`     /// itself) to whichever node's local slab currently contains its     /// position. Panics if the position is outside the global domain.     pub fn add_agent(&mut self, id: u64, position: [f64; 3], payload: f64) {         let target = self.node_for_x(position[0]).expect("add_agent: position is outside the global domain");         self.nodes[target].engine.agents.push(Agent::new(position, payload));         self.nodes[target].agent_ids.push(id);     }     /// Advances the whole distributed simulation by exactly one tick.     ///     /// This does **not** call `UnifiedEngine::run` — it reimplements that     /// method's exact per-tick body (motion + field uptake/release/decay,     /// then index rebuild + local agent-agent interaction, then field     /// diffusion) directly against `UnifiedEngine`'s public fields, move     /// for move, identical to `unified_engine.rs`'s own loop. That's     /// deliberate, not a rewrite for its own sake: `run` is one atomic     /// call with no way to intervene mid-loop, and cross-node coupling has     /// to be inserted *between* phases — after every node's local     /// motion/interaction/diffusion, using a snapshot taken at the right     /// moment, not before or during. Reimplementing the sequence here is     /// what makes that possible without modifying the verbatim     /// `unified_engine.rs` at all.     ///     /// Per tick, in order:     ///     /// 1. **Motion + field coupling**, every node independently — exactly     ///    `UnifiedEngine::run`'s own per-agent loop, replicated.     /// 2. **Local agent-agent interaction**, every node independently —     ///    exactly `UnifiedEngine::run`'s own `index.rebuild` +     ///    `step_agent_interactions`, replicated, unchanged.     /// 3. **Cross-node agent-agent interaction** (the actual new     ///    physics): for every pair of nodes whose slabs are within     ///    `interaction.radius` of each other, every agent pair across     ///    the two nodes within `radius` exchanges payload with the same     ///    `k_exchange * (payload_j - payload_i) * dt` formula     ///    `mitonano_core::interaction` uses locally — computed from a     ///    snapshot taken *before* step 2 mutates any payload, so the     ///    result is independent of processing order and exactly     ///    conservative (`flux(i,j) == -flux(j,i)`, the same antisymmetry     ///    `mitonano_core::interaction`'s own docs describe).     /// 4. **Field diffusion**: every node's own interior-only `step`     ///    (exactly `mitonano_core::concentration`'s existing scheme,     ///    unchanged), followed by a **cross-node boundary correction**     ///    for every pair of *physically adjacent* nodes (diffusion,     ///    unlike interaction, only ever crosses one seam at a time) —     ///    computed from a pre-diffusion snapshot of both sides'     ///    boundary layers using the exact same `(mass_a - mass_b) /     ///    spacing^2` formula the interior scheme uses internally (see     ///    `ConcentrationField::boundary_mass`'s docs), applied with     ///    `add_boundary_mass` to both sides with opposite sign. Because     ///    both the interior step and the boundary correction read from     ///    the *same* pre-tick snapshot (the boundary snapshot is taken     ///    before any node's interior `step` runs), this isn't an     ///    approximate operator split — it's algebraically identical to     ///    what one monolithic grid spanning both nodes would compute in     ///    a single pass, verified directly in     ///    `tests/equivalence.rs`.     /// 5. **Migration**: any agent that ended up outside its own node's     ///    local slab (from step 1's motion) is moved into whichever     ///    node's slab now contains it — unchanged from the previous     ///    revision.     ///     /// With only cross-node coupling disabled (i.e. `num_nodes == 1`,     /// where steps 3-4's cross-node corrections are trivially empty),     /// this reduces to exactly the same sequence `UnifiedEngine::run`     /// performs — see `tests/conservation.rs`'s     /// `single_node_cluster_matches_a_bare_unified_engine_exactly` for     /// the regression test confirming that equivalence still holds after     /// this rewrite.     pub fn step<R: Rng + ?Sized>(&mut self, rng: &mut R) {         self.tick += 1;         let n = self.nodes.len();         // ---- Phase 1: mobility switching + motion + field         // release/uptake/decay, every node independently. Replicates         // unified_engine.rs's own per-agent loop body, with five opt-in         // substitutions/additions at exactly the points that verbatim         // file's fixed sequence cannot itself support (see         // mitonano_core::mobility's, mitonano_core::chemotaxis's,         // mitonano_core::kinetics's, and Agent::effective_params's own         // docs): each agent's own `phenotype` override is resolved first         // (falling back to the node's engine-wide default when unset) and         // used as the base for both motion and decay/release; mobility         // state is then rolled if `two_state` is configured (so         // `advance_position`, which is itself mobility-aware, sees this         // tick's *current* state, not last tick's); drift is augmented         // with the local field gradient if `chemotaxis` is configured;         // uptake resolves each agent's own `uptake_override` first (gap 3         // — see `Agent::uptake_override`'s own docs), falling back to the         // node's `saturable_uptake` config (Michaelis-Menten) or, failing         // that, the original linear `k_uptake` formula. With every         // agent's `phenotype`/`uptake_override` at their defaults and all         // config extensions `None`, this phase is byte-for-byte the same         // computation as before any of them existed — see         // `tests::single_node_cluster_matches_a_bare_unified_engine_exactly`.         for node in &mut self.nodes {             let params = node.engine.params;             let bounds = node.engine.bounds;             let dt = node.engine.dt;             let k_uptake = self.k_uptake;             for agent in &mut node.engine.agents {                 // Resolves this agent's own `phenotype` override if set,                 // otherwise falls back to the node's engine-wide default                 // — see `Agent::effective_params`'s own docs. Used as the                 // base for *both* motion and decay/release below, so a                 // phenotype override (e.g. a damaged mitochondrion's                 // faster clearance, or a different formulation's                 // mobility) affects both consistently, not just one.                 let base_params = agent.effective_params(&params);                 if let Some(two_state) = self.two_state {                     agent.mobility = maybe_toggle_mobility(agent.mobility, &two_state, dt, rng);                 }                 let step_params = match self.chemotaxis {                     Some(chemo) => {                         let gradient = node.engine.field.gradient(agent.position);                         let augmented_drift = chemotactic_drift(base_params.drift, gradient, chemo.coeff);                         AgentParams { drift: augmented_drift, ..base_params }                     }                     None => base_params,                 };                 agent.advance_position(&step_params, &bounds, dt, rng);                 let c_local = node.engine.field.sample(agent.position);                 let desired_uptake = match agent.uptake_override {                     Some(kinetics) => uptake_amount(&kinetics, c_local, dt),                     None => match self.saturable_uptake {                         Some(sat) => saturable_uptake_amount(&sat, c_local, dt),                         None => k_uptake * c_local * dt,                     },                 };                 let actual_uptake = node.engine.field.withdraw(agent.position, desired_uptake);                 agent.payload += actual_uptake;                 let (released, eliminated) = agent.decay_and_release(&base_params, dt);                 self.cumulative_eliminated += eliminated;                 if released > 0.0 {                     node.engine.field.deposit(agent.position, released);                 }             }         }         // ---- Snapshot every node's post-motion, pre-interaction agent         // state, for exact-antisymmetry cross-node exchange in phase 3.         let position_snapshots: Vec<Vec<[f64; 3]>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.position).collect()).collect();         let payload_snapshots: Vec<Vec<f64>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.payload).collect()).collect();         // ---- Phase 2: local agent-agent interaction, every node         // independently. Exactly unified_engine.rs's own         // index.rebuild + step_agent_interactions, unchanged.         for node in &mut self.nodes {             node.engine.index.rebuild(&node.engine.agents);             let interaction = node.engine.interaction;             let dt = node.engine.dt;             step_agent_interactions(&mut node.engine.agents, &node.engine.index, &interaction, dt);         }         // ---- Phase 3: cross-node agent-agent interaction — the actual         // new physics. Only node pairs whose slabs are within `radius`         // of each other can possibly have an interacting pair; since         // slabs are sorted along x, a gap larger than `radius` for pair         // (i, j) means every pair (i, j') with j' > j is farther still,         // so the inner loop can stop early exactly like         // mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator::exchange_boundaries does.         //         // Within a qualifying node pair, only agents actually close         // enough to the shared boundary to possibly be within `radius`         // of *anything* on the other side are checked — see         // `boundary_candidates_ge`/`boundary_candidates_le`'s docs. This         // is the ghost-agent-list fix closed in T3 (see module docs):         // previously this was a full O(agents_i * agents_j) cross         // product per qualifying node pair, checking every agent against         // every agent even when the vast majority couldn't possibly be         // within radius of the boundary at all. The distance check         // itself (and therefore the exact set of pairs found and the         // exact `flux` applied to each) is completely unchanged — this         // is a pure work-reduction refactor, not a physics change, and         // `tests/equivalence.rs` (unmodified) already re-verifies the         // result is bit-for-bit unaffected by this rewrite.         let mut cross_deltas: Vec<Vec<f64>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| vec![0.0; n.engine.agents.len()]).collect();         for i in 0..n {             let radius = self.nodes[i].engine.interaction.radius;             let k_exchange = self.nodes[i].engine.interaction.k_exchange;             let dt = self.nodes[i].engine.dt;             let radius_sq = radius * radius;             for j in (i + 1)..n {                 let gap = self.nodes[j].local_bounds.min[0] - self.nodes[i].local_bounds.max[0];                 if gap > radius {                     break;                 }                 // Node i is entirely to the left of node j (sorted                 // slabs), so the closest any point in node j can be to a                 // given agent in node i is exactly `node_j.min_x` — an                 // agent in i can only possibly reach *something* in node                 // j if it's within `radius` of that edge. Symmetrically                 // for node j's candidates against node i's far edge.                 let candidates_i = boundary_candidates_ge(&position_snapshots[i], self.nodes[j].local_bounds.min[0] - radius);                 let candidates_j = boundary_candidates_le(&position_snapshots[j], self.nodes[i].local_bounds.max[0] + radius);                 for &a_idx in &candidates_i {                     let pos_a = position_snapshots[i][a_idx];                     for &b_idx in &candidates_j {                         let pos_b = position_snapshots[j][b_idx];                         let dx = pos_a[0] - pos_b[0];                         let dy = pos_a[1] - pos_b[1];                         let dz = pos_a[2] - pos_b[2];                         if dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz <= radius_sq {                             let flux = k_exchange * (payload_snapshots[j][b_idx] - payload_snapshots[i][a_idx]) * dt;                             cross_deltas[i][a_idx] += flux;                             cross_deltas[j][b_idx] -= flux;                         }                     }                 }             }         }         for (node, deltas) in self.nodes.iter_mut().zip(cross_deltas.iter()) {             for (agent, d) in node.engine.agents.iter_mut().zip(deltas.iter()) {                 agent.payload += d;             }         }         // ---- Phase 4: field diffusion. Snapshot pre-diffusion boundary         // mass on both faces of every node first (diffusion, unlike         // interaction, only ever crosses one seam — no early-break scan         // needed, just each adjacent pair once), run every node's own         // interior diffusion (heterogeneous per-cell, via         // `step_heterogeneous`, if `diffusion_coeff_field` is set for         // that node — gap 4; otherwise the original uniform `step` call,         // unchanged), then apply the cross-node boundary correction         // computed from the snapshot — using each side's own boundary-         // layer diffusivity, harmonic-mean-averaged at the seam exactly         // the way `step_heterogeneous` averages at an ordinary interior         // face (see `mitonano_core::harmonic_mean`'s own docs), so a         // heterogeneous profile is treated consistently whether a given         // face happens to be inside one node or split across two.         let boundary_max: Vec<Vec<f64>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.field.boundary_mass(XFace::Max)).collect();         let boundary_min: Vec<Vec<f64>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.field.boundary_mass(XFace::Min)).collect();         let spacings_x: Vec<f64> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.field.cell_size()[0]).collect();         let (fnx, fny, fnz) = self.field_grid;         for node in &mut self.nodes {             let dt = node.engine.dt;             match &node.diffusion_coeff_field {                 Some(d_field) => node.engine.field.step_heterogeneous(dt, d_field),                 None => {                     let d = node.engine.field_diffusion_coeff;                     node.engine.field.step(dt, d);                 }             }         }         for i in 0..n.saturating_sub(1) {             let j = i + 1;             let dt = self.nodes[i].engine.dt;             let spacing = spacings_x[i]; // == spacings_x[j] by construction (uniform field_grid config)             let layer_len = boundary_max[i].len();             // Each side's own boundary-layer diffusivity: per-cell if             // that node has a heterogeneous profile, otherwise its             // single uniform scalar repeated for every face cell.             let d_i_layer: Vec<f64> = match &self.nodes[i].diffusion_coeff_field {                 Some(vals) => extract_x_layer(vals, fnx, fny, fnz, fnx - 1),                 None => vec![self.nodes[i].engine.field_diffusion_coeff; layer_len],             };             let d_j_layer: Vec<f64> = match &self.nodes[j].diffusion_coeff_field {                 Some(vals) => extract_x_layer(vals, fnx, fny, fnz, 0),                 None => vec![self.nodes[j].engine.field_diffusion_coeff; layer_len],             };             let mut flux = vec![0.0; layer_len];             for k in 0..layer_len {                 let mass_i = boundary_max[i][k];                 let mass_j = boundary_min[j][k];                 let d = harmonic_mean(d_i_layer[k], d_j_layer[k]);                 flux[k] = d * dt * (mass_i - mass_j) / (spacing * spacing);             }             let neg_flux: Vec<f64> = flux.iter().map(|f| -f).collect();             self.nodes[i].engine.field.add_boundary_mass(XFace::Max, &neg_flux);             self.nodes[j].engine.field.add_boundary_mass(XFace::Min, &flux);         }         // ---- Phase 4b: source/sink boundary conditions (gap 5). Applied         // only on faces that are genuinely part of the *global* domain         // boundary for a given node — YMin/YMax/ZMin/ZMax for every node         // (this crate's decomposition never splits along y or z), but         // XMin/XMax only for the very first and very last node         // respectively (every other node's x-faces are internal seams;         // injecting or draining mass there would fabricate mass at a         // fictitious boundary and silently break the mass-conservation         // identity every other test in this crate checks — see         // `GlobalFace`'s own docs).         let last_node_id = n.saturating_sub(1);         for node in &mut self.nodes {             let dt = node.engine.dt;             let applicable_faces: &[GlobalFace] = if node.id == 0 && node.id == last_node_id {                 &[GlobalFace::XMin, GlobalFace::XMax, GlobalFace::YMin, GlobalFace::YMax, GlobalFace::ZMin, GlobalFace::ZMax]             } else if node.id == 0 {                 &[GlobalFace::XMin, GlobalFace::YMin, GlobalFace::YMax, GlobalFace::ZMin, GlobalFace::ZMax]             } else if node.id == last_node_id {                 &[GlobalFace::XMax, GlobalFace::YMin, GlobalFace::YMax, GlobalFace::ZMin, GlobalFace::ZMax]             } else {                 &[GlobalFace::YMin, GlobalFace::YMax, GlobalFace::ZMin, GlobalFace::ZMax]             };             for &global_face in applicable_faces {                 if let Some(bc) = self.boundary_conditions[global_face.index()] {                     let core_face = global_face.to_core_face();                     if let Some(rate) = bc.injection_rate {                         self.cumulative_injected += node.engine.field.inject_at_face(core_face, rate, dt);                     }                     if let Some(k_drain) = bc.drain_rate {                         self.cumulative_drained += node.engine.field.drain_at_face(core_face, k_drain, dt);                     }                 }             }         }         // ---- Phase 5: fission (agent division). Each agent present         // *before* this phase runs independently rolls         // `mitonano_core::population::event_fires`; a daughter created         // by fission is deliberately not itself checked for fission this         // same tick (the loop bound `n_before` is captured before any         // daughters are pushed) — without that, a single very-high-rate         // tick could cascade through multiple "generations" within one         // dt, which no real timestep-based simulation should allow.         // Payload is split via `split_payload` (exactly conservative by         // construction — see that function's docs); the daughter starts         // at the parent's exact position and gets a fresh id from         // `next_generated_id`, which the caller is responsible for         // choosing above any id it ever passes to `add_agent` directly         // (see `FissionParams::next_generated_id`'s own docs).         //         // The daughter inherits the parent's `phenotype` exactly — a         // mitochondrion's damage state is expected to persist through         // division by default — *unless* `fission.mutation` is         // configured, in which case each division independently rolls         // `mutation_fires`; on a hit, the daughter's phenotype is         // replaced with `mutation.damaged_phenotype` instead of         // inherited. The parent itself is never mutated by this — only         // ever the newly-created daughter.         if let Some(fission) = self.fission {             for node in &mut self.nodes {                 let dt = node.engine.dt;                 let n_before = node.engine.agents.len();                 for i in 0..n_before {                     if event_fires(fission.rate, dt, rng) {                         let (first, second) = split_payload(node.engine.agents[i].payload, fission.payload_fraction);                         node.engine.agents[i].payload = first;                         let mut daughter = Agent::new(node.engine.agents[i].position, second);                         daughter.phenotype = match fission.mutation {                             Some(mutation) if mutation_fires(&mutation, rng) => Some(mutation.damaged_phenotype),                             _ => node.engine.agents[i].phenotype,                         };                         node.engine.agents.push(daughter);                         node.agent_ids.push(self.next_generated_id);                         self.next_generated_id += 1;                     }                 }             }         }         // ---- Phase 6: fusion (agent merging). Local pass first (as         // before — brute-force O(agents^2) pairwise scan per node,         // "revisit if fusion is ever used with large per-node         // populations" as previously noted), immediately followed by a         // cross-node pass closing gap 2 (fusion was previously strictly         // node-local, the one coupling mechanism phase 3's cross-node         // interaction and phase 4's cross-node diffusion didn't have an         // analogue for). Sequential composition, not simultaneous: an         // agent consumed by the local pass is already removed from its         // node by the time the cross-node pass starts, so it naturally         // can't also participate there — no extra cross-phase         // "consumed" bookkeeping needed to enforce "at most one fusion         // per agent per tick" across the two passes.         if let Some(fusion) = self.fusion {             for node in &mut self.nodes {                 let dt = node.engine.dt;                 let radius_sq = fusion.radius * fusion.radius;                 let n_agents = node.engine.agents.len();                 let mut consumed = vec![false; n_agents];                 let mut to_remove: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();                 for i in 0..n_agents {                     if consumed[i] {                         continue;                     }                     for j in (i + 1)..n_agents {                         if consumed[j] {                             continue;                         }                         let pa = node.engine.agents[i].position;                         let pb = node.engine.agents[j].position;                         let d2: f64 = (0..3).map(|k| (pa[k] - pb[k]).powi(2)).sum();                         if d2 <= radius_sq && event_fires(fusion.rate, dt, rng) {                             node.engine.agents[i].payload = merge_payload(node.engine.agents[i].payload, node.engine.agents[j].payload);                             consumed[i] = true;                             consumed[j] = true;                             to_remove.push(j);                             break; // agent i is consumed now; stop pairing it against further candidates                         }                     }                 }                 // Remove in descending index order so each swap_remove                 // can't invalidate an as-yet-unprocessed target index.                 to_remove.sort_unstable_by(|a, b| b.cmp(a));                 for idx in to_remove {                     node.engine.agents.swap_remove(idx);                     node.agent_ids.swap_remove(idx);                 }             }             // Cross-node fusion pass. Structured like phase 3's             // cross-node interaction (snapshot positions/payloads,             // early-break node-pair scan bounded by `fusion.radius`,             // boundary-candidate prefiltering) rather than like phase 3's             // *predecessor* (an unfiltered O(agents_i * agents_j) scan) —             // applying the T3 lesson from the start instead of needing a             // second pass to fix it later. Keeps the agent in the             // lower-indexed node, absorbs the higher-indexed node's             // agent's payload into it, and removes the latter.             let position_snapshots: Vec<Vec<[f64; 3]>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.position).collect()).collect();             let payload_snapshots: Vec<Vec<f64>> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.payload).collect()).collect();             let mut consumed: Vec<Vec<bool>> = position_snapshots.iter().map(|p| vec![false; p.len()]).collect();             let mut extra_payload: Vec<Vec<f64>> = position_snapshots.iter().map(|p| vec![0.0; p.len()]).collect();             let mut to_remove: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n];             let radius = fusion.radius;             let radius_sq = radius * radius;             let dt = self.nodes[0].engine.dt; // uniform across nodes by construction             for i in 0..n {                 for j in (i + 1)..n {                     let gap = self.nodes[j].local_bounds.min[0] - self.nodes[i].local_bounds.max[0];                     if gap > radius {                         break;                     }                     let candidates_i = boundary_candidates_ge(&position_snapshots[i], self.nodes[j].local_bounds.min[0] - radius);                     let candidates_j = boundary_candidates_le(&position_snapshots[j], self.nodes[i].local_bounds.max[0] + radius);                     for &a_idx in &candidates_i {                         if consumed[i][a_idx] {                             continue;                         }                         for &b_idx in &candidates_j {                             if consumed[j][b_idx] {                                 continue;                             }                             let pa = position_snapshots[i][a_idx];                             let pb = position_snapshots[j][b_idx];                             let dx = pa[0] - pb[0];                             let dy = pa[1] - pb[1];                             let dz = pa[2] - pb[2];                             let d2 = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz;                             if d2 <= radius_sq && event_fires(fusion.rate, dt, rng) {                                 extra_payload[i][a_idx] += payload_snapshots[j][b_idx];                                 consumed[i][a_idx] = true;                                 consumed[j][b_idx] = true;                                 to_remove[j].push(b_idx);                                 break;                             }                         }                     }                 }             }             for (node, extras) in self.nodes.iter_mut().zip(extra_payload.iter()) {                 for (agent, &extra) in node.engine.agents.iter_mut().zip(extras.iter()) {                     agent.payload += extra;                 }             }             for (node, removals) in self.nodes.iter_mut().zip(to_remove.iter_mut()) {                 removals.sort_unstable_by(|a, b| b.cmp(a));                 for &idx in removals.iter() {                     node.engine.agents.swap_remove(idx);                     node.agent_ids.swap_remove(idx);                 }             }         }         // ---- Phase 7: migration — unchanged from the previous revision.         let slab_bounds: Vec<(usize, f64, f64)> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| (n.id, n.local_bounds.min[0], n.local_bounds.max[0])).collect();         let global_max_x = self.global_bounds.max[0];         let node_for_x = |x: f64| -> Option<usize> {             const EPS: f64 = 1e-9;             let adjusted = if (x - global_max_x).abs() < EPS { global_max_x - EPS } else { x };             slab_bounds.iter().find(|(_, min_x, max_x)| adjusted >= *min_x && adjusted < *max_x).map(|(id, _, _)| *id)         };         let mut pending: Vec<(u64, Agent, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();         for node in &mut self.nodes {             let mut i = 0;             while i < node.engine.agents.len() {                 let agent = node.engine.agents[i];                 let x = agent.position[0];                 if x >= node.local_bounds.min[0] && x < node.local_bounds.max[0] {                     i += 1;                     continue;                 }                 let target = node_for_x(x)                     .expect("step: an agent's motion is bounds-reflected against the global domain and can never leave it");                 if target == node.id {                     // Half-open containment can transiently disagree with                     // itself right at a node's own far edge (mirrors                     // mitonano_orchestrator::node.rs's identical                     // reasoning) — not a migration.                     i += 1;                     continue;                 }                 let removed_agent = node.engine.agents.swap_remove(i);                 let removed_id = node.agent_ids.swap_remove(i);                 pending.push((removed_id, removed_agent, node.id, target));                 // Do not increment `i`: swap_remove moved a different                 // element into slot `i`, which still needs checking.             }         }         for (agent_id, agent, from_node, to_node) in pending {             self.nodes[to_node].engine.agents.push(agent);             self.nodes[to_node].agent_ids.push(agent_id);             self.migration_log.push(MigrationRecord { agent_id, from_node, to_node, tick: self.tick });         }     }     /// Aggregated mass-bookkeeping snapshot across every node's engine     /// and field — the distributed analogue of     /// `UnifiedEngine`'s own internal `stats` (private there; this is     /// the cross-node sum of the same quantities).     pub fn stats(&self) -> FederatedTickStats {         let total_agent_payload: f64 = self.nodes.iter().flat_map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter()).map(|a| a.payload).sum();         let total_field_mass: f64 = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.field.total_mass()).sum();         FederatedTickStats {             tick: self.tick,             total_agent_payload,             total_field_mass,             total_combined_mass: total_agent_payload + total_field_mass,             cumulative_eliminated: self.cumulative_eliminated,             cumulative_injected: self.cumulative_injected,             cumulative_drained: self.cumulative_drained,         }     }     /// Checks current per-node agent-count balance without modifying     /// anything. Same definition `mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator`'s     /// `maybe_rebalance` uses (`imbalance_ratio = max_count /     /// max(min_count, 1)`, avoiding a division by zero when the     /// least-loaded node is empty — a common case, not an edge case to     /// special-case away).     pub fn check_balance(&self, imbalance_threshold: f64) -> RebalanceReport {         let counts: Vec<usize> = self.nodes.iter().map(|n| n.engine.agents.len()).collect();         let (max_node, &max_count) = counts.iter().enumerate().max_by_key(|(_, &c)| c).expect("at least one node");         let (min_node, &min_count) = counts.iter().enumerate().min_by_key(|(_, &c)| c).expect("at least one node");         let imbalance_ratio = max_count as f64 / (min_count.max(1) as f64);         RebalanceReport { needs_rebalance: imbalance_ratio > imbalance_threshold, max_node, min_node, max_count, min_count, imbalance_ratio }     }     /// Checks balance, and if it exceeds `imbalance_threshold`, actually     /// repartitions the domain via [`FederatedUnifiedEngine::rebalance`]     /// and returns the *post*-rebalance report. Returns `None` if nothing     /// needed to change — mirrors     /// `mitonano_orchestrator::FederatedMitoNano::rebalance_if_needed`'s     /// exact contract.     pub fn rebalance_if_needed(&mut self, imbalance_threshold: f64) -> Option<RebalanceReport> {         let pre = self.check_balance(imbalance_threshold);         if !pre.needs_rebalance {             return None;         }         self.rebalance();         Some(self.check_balance(imbalance_threshold))     }     /// Closes gap 4 from `mitonano_federated`'s own prior revisions     /// ("no rebalancing... `mitonano_orchestrator`'s `rebalance` machinery     /// is not reused or reimplemented here"): repartitions the domain via     /// 1D median-cut on agents' current x positions — the *algorithm* is     /// ported directly from     /// `mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator::OrchestratorCoordinator::rebalance`,     /// against `mitonano_core` types rather than shared ones (see this     /// crate's top-level module docs for why the two crates' `Agent`     /// types were never unified: reconciling them would risk either of     /// the two 60+-test crates that already exist, for a benefit     /// achievable more cheaply by porting the one proven algorithm that     /// actually mattered here). Falls back to equal-width slabs — the     /// same safe default `new` uses — when there aren't enough distinct     /// agent positions to support `num_nodes - 1` genuinely distinct cut     /// points (mirroring     /// `mitonano_orchestrator::domain::DomainDecomposition::with_boundaries`'s     /// own degenerate-case handling), rather than installing a     /// zero-width slab.     ///     /// Unlike the orchestrator's version, this must also handle field     /// mass: every node here carries a real `ConcentrationField`, not     /// just agents, and simply discarding each old field when     /// reconstructing new slabs would silently destroy mass — breaking     /// every conservation guarantee this crate exists to prove. Before     /// building any new node, every old node's field is read out cell by     /// cell as `(x0, x1, mass)` intervals (per `(y, z)` slice, since y/z     /// resolution and extent never change), and each new cell's mass is     /// computed via [`remap_interval_mass`]'s conservative overlap     /// redistribution — exactly conservative by construction (checked     /// directly, not just argued, in     /// `tests::rebalance_conserves_total_combined_mass_exactly`).     fn rebalance(&mut self) {         let num_nodes = self.nodes.len();         if num_nodes <= 1 {             return;         }         // ---- Step 1: new slab boundaries via median-cut on agent         // x-positions (ported algorithm — see method docs above).         let mut all_agent_x: Vec<f64> = self.nodes.iter().flat_map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.position[0])).collect();         all_agent_x.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());         let mut boundaries: Vec<f64> = Vec::new();         if !all_agent_x.is_empty() {             let n_agents = all_agent_x.len();             for i in 1..num_nodes {                 let idx = (i * n_agents / num_nodes).clamp(1, n_agents - 1);                 boundaries.push((all_agent_x[idx - 1] + all_agent_x[idx]) / 2.0);             }         }         boundaries.retain(|&b| b > self.global_bounds.min[0] && b < self.global_bounds.max[0]);         boundaries.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());         boundaries.dedup_by(|a, b| (*a - *b).abs() < 1e-9);         let new_slab_x: Vec<(f64, f64)> = if boundaries.len() + 1 == num_nodes {             let mut cuts = vec![self.global_bounds.min[0]];             cuts.extend(boundaries.iter().copied());             cuts.push(self.global_bounds.max[0]);             (0..num_nodes).map(|i| (cuts[i], cuts[i + 1])).collect()         } else {             // Degenerate case: fall back to equal-width slabs, the same             // safe default `new` uses at construction.             let dx = self.global_bounds.width(0) / num_nodes as f64;             (0..num_nodes)                 .map(|i| {                     let min_x = self.global_bounds.min[0] + i as f64 * dx;                     let max_x = if i == num_nodes - 1 { self.global_bounds.max[0] } else { min_x + dx };                     (min_x, max_x)                 })                 .collect()         };         // ---- Step 2: read every old node's field mass, cell by cell,         // as (x0, x1, mass) intervals, grouped per (y, z) slice (y/z         // extent and resolution are unchanged by a rebalance — only the         // x-axis decomposition is).         let (fnx, fny, fnz) = self.field_grid;         let mut old_cells_per_yz: Vec<Vec<(f64, f64, f64)>> = vec![Vec::new(); fny * fnz];         for node in &self.nodes {             let cell_size = node.engine.field.cell_size();             for iz in 0..fnz {                 for iy in 0..fny {                     let y = node.local_bounds.min[1] + (iy as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[1];                     let z = node.local_bounds.min[2] + (iz as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[2];                     let slice_idx = iy + iz * fny;                     for ix in 0..fnx {                         let x0 = node.local_bounds.min[0] + ix as f64 * cell_size[0];                         let x1 = x0 + cell_size[0];                         let x_center = x0 + 0.5 * cell_size[0];                         let mass = node.engine.field.sample([x_center, y, z]) * cell_size[0] * cell_size[1] * cell_size[2];                         old_cells_per_yz[slice_idx].push((x0, x1, mass));                     }                 }             }         }         // ---- Step 3: gather every agent (with id) before discarding         // the old nodes.         let mut all_agents: Vec<(u64, Agent)> = Vec::new();         for node in &self.nodes {             for (&id, &agent) in node.agent_ids.iter().zip(node.engine.agents.iter()) {                 all_agents.push((id, agent));             }         }         // ---- Step 4: build new nodes over the new slabs, remapping         // field mass conservatively and re-placing every agent by its         // already-known (unchanged) position. Physics parameters are         // uniform across nodes by construction (see `FederatedEngineConfig`),         // so any existing node (here, node 0, guaranteed to exist since         // num_nodes > 1) is a valid source for them.         let (inx, iny, inz) = self.index_grid;         let params = self.nodes[0].engine.params;         let field_diffusion_coeff = self.nodes[0].engine.field_diffusion_coeff;         let interaction = self.nodes[0].engine.interaction;         let dt = self.nodes[0].engine.dt;         let mut new_nodes = Vec::with_capacity(num_nodes);         for i in 0..num_nodes {             let (min_x, max_x) = new_slab_x[i];             let local_bounds =                 Bounds::new([min_x, self.global_bounds.min[1], self.global_bounds.min[2]], [max_x, self.global_bounds.max[1], self.global_bounds.max[2]]);             let mut field = ConcentrationField::new(local_bounds, fnx, fny, fnz);             let cell_size = field.cell_size();             for iz in 0..fnz {                 for iy in 0..fny {                     let slice_idx = iy + iz * fny;                     let y = local_bounds.min[1] + (iy as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[1];                     let z = local_bounds.min[2] + (iz as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[2];                     for ix in 0..fnx {                         let new_x0 = local_bounds.min[0] + ix as f64 * cell_size[0];                         let new_x1 = new_x0 + cell_size[0];                         let mass = remap_interval_mass(&old_cells_per_yz[slice_idx], new_x0, new_x1);                         if mass > 0.0 {                             let x_center = new_x0 + 0.5 * cell_size[0];                             field.deposit([x_center, y, z], mass);                         }                     }                 }             }             let index = SpatialIndex::new(local_bounds, inx, iny, inz);             // Motion stays clamped against the *global* domain, exactly             // as at initial construction — see `new`'s own docs.             let engine = UnifiedEngine::new(Vec::new(), params, self.global_bounds, field, field_diffusion_coeff, index, interaction, dt);             // Gap 4: re-sample the diffusion profile for this node's new             // slab boundaries — a rebalance shifts where each node's             // territory actually is, so the precomputed per-cell array             // from construction time is stale and must be rebuilt the             // same way `new` builds it initially, not reused as-is.             let diffusion_coeff_field = self.heterogeneous_diffusion.map(|f| {                 let cell_size = engine.field.cell_size();                 let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(fnx * fny * fnz);                 for iz in 0..fnz {                     for iy in 0..fny {                         for ix in 0..fnx {                             let x = local_bounds.min[0] + (ix as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[0];                             let y = local_bounds.min[1] + (iy as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[1];                             let z = local_bounds.min[2] + (iz as f64 + 0.5) * cell_size[2];                             values.push(f([x, y, z]));                         }                     }                 }                 values             });             new_nodes.push(FederatedNode { id: i, local_bounds, engine, agent_ids: Vec::new(), diffusion_coeff_field });         }         for (id, agent) in all_agents {             let x = agent.position[0];             const EPS: f64 = 1e-9;             let adjusted = if (x - self.global_bounds.max[0]).abs() < EPS { self.global_bounds.max[0] - EPS } else { x };             let target = new_slab_x                 .iter()                 .position(|&(min_x, max_x)| adjusted >= min_x && adjusted < max_x)                 .expect("rebalance: agent position could not be placed in the new slab layout");             new_nodes[target].engine.agents.push(agent);             new_nodes[target].agent_ids.push(id);         }         self.nodes = new_nodes;     } } /// Conservatively redistributes each `(x0, x1, mass)` old cell's mass /// into the single new cell `[new_x0, new_x1)`, in proportion to the /// overlap between the old cell's x-range and the new cell's x-range — /// the uniform-density-within-cell assumption, the standard technique for /// remapping a finite-volume quantity onto a different, non-overlapping /// grid. Summed across every new cell that overlaps a given old cell, the /// overlap fractions sum to exactly `1.0` (the new grid fully covers the /// same span the old grid did), so total mass is conserved exactly modulo /// floating-point rounding — checked directly, not just argued, in /// `tests::rebalance_conserves_total_combined_mass_exactly`. fn remap_interval_mass(old_cells: &[(f64, f64, f64)], new_x0: f64, new_x1: f64) -> f64 {     let mut total = 0.0;     for &(ox0, ox1, mass) in old_cells {         let width = ox1 - ox0;         if width <= 0.0 {             continue;         }         let overlap = (ox1.min(new_x1) - ox0.max(new_x0)).max(0.0);         if overlap > 0.0 {             total += mass * (overlap / width);         }     }     total } #[cfg(test)] mod tests {     use super::*;     use mitonano_core::agent::MobilityState;     use mitonano_core::kinetics::UptakeKinetics;     use mitonano_core::population::MutationParams;     use rand::rngs::StdRng;     use rand::SeedableRng;     fn default_config() -> FederatedEngineConfig {         FederatedEngineConfig {             agent_params: AgentParams { drift: [0.3, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.15, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.2 },             interaction: InteractionParams { radius: 1.0, k_exchange: 0.2 },             field_diffusion_coeff: 0.1,             dt: 0.01,             k_uptake: 0.2,             field_grid: (4, 3, 3),             index_grid: (4, 3, 3),             saturable_uptake: None,             chemotaxis: None,             fission: None,             fusion: None,             two_state: None,             heterogeneous_diffusion: None,             boundary_conditions: [None; 6],         }     }     fn global_bounds() -> Bounds {         Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0])     }     #[test]     fn new_creates_the_requested_number_of_nodes_with_correct_slab_bounds() {         let engine = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, global_bounds(), default_config());         assert_eq!(engine.nodes.len(), 4);         assert_eq!(engine.nodes[0].local_bounds.min[0], 0.0);         assert_eq!(engine.nodes[0].local_bounds.max[0], 10.0);         assert_eq!(engine.nodes[3].local_bounds.max[0], 40.0);     }     #[test]     fn add_agent_places_it_in_the_correct_node() {         let mut engine = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, global_bounds(), default_config());         engine.add_agent(1, [25.0, 0.0, 0.0], 5.0); // slab [20,30) -> node 2         assert_eq!(engine.nodes[2].engine.agents.len(), 1);         assert_eq!(engine.nodes[2].agent_ids[0], 1);         assert_eq!(engine.total_agent_count(), 1);     }     #[test]     #[should_panic(expected = "outside the global domain")]     fn add_agent_rejects_a_position_outside_the_global_domain() {         let mut engine = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(2, global_bounds(), default_config());         engine.add_agent(1, [999.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);     }     #[test]     fn stepping_conserves_total_agent_count_across_real_physics_and_migrations() {         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params.drift = [2.0, 0.0, 0.0]; // fast enough to reliably cross a 10-unit slab within the tick budget below         let mut engine = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, global_bounds(), config);         for i in 0..20 {             engine.add_agent(i, [1.0 + i as f64 * 0.05, 0.0, 0.0], 5.0);         }         let expected = engine.total_agent_count();         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(7);         for _ in 0..1000 {             engine.step(&mut rng);             assert_eq!(engine.total_agent_count(), expected, "real physics + migration must conserve agent count");         }         assert!(!engine.migration_log().is_empty(), "drift of 2.0*dt=0.02/tick over 1000 ticks (20 units total) across 10-unit-wide slabs should produce migrations");     }     /// Confirms migration is actually happening to the *correct* target,     /// not just that counts stay conserved — a specific agent, tracked by     /// id, must end up in the node whose slab its final position falls     /// in.     #[test]     fn a_specific_agent_migrates_to_the_node_matching_its_final_position() {         let mut engine = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, global_bounds(), default_config());         engine.add_agent(42, [1.0, 0.0, 0.0], 5.0);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(99);         for _ in 0..500 {             engine.step(&mut rng);         }         // Find agent 42 wherever it ended up.         let mut found = None;         for node in &engine.nodes {             if let Some(pos) = node.agent_ids.iter().position(|&id| id == 42) {                 found = Some((node.id, node.engine.agents[pos].position[0]));             }         }         let (node_id, x) = found.expect("agent 42 must exist in exactly one node");         assert!(x >= engine.nodes[node_id].local_bounds.min[0] && x < engine.nodes[node_id].local_bounds.max[0] || (x - engine.global_bounds.max[0]).abs() < 1e-6);     }     #[test]     fn boundary_candidate_filters_shrink_a_spread_out_population() {         // 1000 agents spread uniformly across a wide span; only those         // within a narrow band near the threshold should qualify — this         // is the property that makes the phase-3 optimization actually         // reduce work rather than just add bookkeeping overhead.         let positions: Vec<[f64; 3]> = (0..1000).map(|i| [i as f64 * 0.1, 0.0, 0.0]).collect(); // x in [0, 99.9]         let ge_candidates = boundary_candidates_ge(&positions, 95.0); // only the top ~5% should qualify         assert!(             ge_candidates.len() < positions.len() / 10,             "expected a small fraction of 1000 agents near the boundary, got {}",             ge_candidates.len()         );         assert!(ge_candidates.iter().all(|&i| positions[i][0] >= 95.0));         let le_candidates = boundary_candidates_le(&positions, 5.0); // only the bottom ~5% should qualify         assert!(             le_candidates.len() < positions.len() / 10,             "expected a small fraction of 1000 agents near the boundary, got {}",             le_candidates.len()         );         assert!(le_candidates.iter().all(|&i| positions[i][0] <= 5.0));     }     #[test]     fn boundary_candidate_filters_include_every_qualifying_index_exactly() {         // Correctness, not just size: every index whose x satisfies the         // threshold must appear, and none that don't.         let positions = vec![[1.0, 0.0, 0.0], [5.0, 0.0, 0.0], [5.0, 1.0, 1.0], [9.9, 0.0, 0.0], [10.0, 0.0, 0.0]];         let ge = boundary_candidates_ge(&positions, 5.0);         assert_eq!(ge, vec![1, 2, 3, 4]); // x = 5.0, 5.0, 9.9, 10.0 all >= 5.0         let le = boundary_candidates_le(&positions, 5.0);         assert_eq!(le, vec![0, 1, 2]); // x = 1.0, 5.0, 5.0 all <= 5.0     }     #[test]     fn single_node_cluster_matches_a_bare_unified_engine_exactly() {         let config = default_config();         let bounds = global_bounds();         // Federated, 1 node.         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         fed.add_agent(1, [5.0, 0.0, 0.0], 3.0);         fed.add_agent(2, [8.0, 1.0, -1.0], 4.0);         let mut rng_fed = StdRng::seed_from_u64(555);         for _ in 0..50 {             fed.step(&mut rng_fed);         }         let fed_stats = fed.stats();         // Bare UnifiedEngine, identical setup.         let field = ConcentrationField::new(bounds, config.field_grid.0, config.field_grid.1, config.field_grid.2);         let index = SpatialIndex::new(bounds, config.index_grid.0, config.index_grid.1, config.index_grid.2);         let agents = vec![Agent::new([5.0, 0.0, 0.0], 3.0), Agent::new([8.0, 1.0, -1.0], 4.0)];         let mut bare = UnifiedEngine::new(agents, config.agent_params, bounds, field, config.field_diffusion_coeff, index, config.interaction, config.dt);         let mut rng_bare = StdRng::seed_from_u64(555);         let history = bare.run(50, config.k_uptake, &mut rng_bare);         let bare_last = history.last().unwrap();         assert!((fed_stats.total_agent_payload - bare_last.total_agent_payload).abs() < 1e-9);         assert!((fed_stats.total_field_mass - bare_last.total_field_mass).abs() < 1e-9);         assert!((fed_stats.cumulative_eliminated - bare_last.cumulative_eliminated).abs() < 1e-9);     }     /// Confirms `saturable_uptake` is actually wired in, not just a     /// config field that's silently ignored: a high concentration should     /// produce visibly *less* uptake under saturable kinetics than under     /// linear kinetics (which grows without bound), for parameters where     /// `v_max` caps well below what the linear formula would give.     #[test]     fn saturable_uptake_config_produces_less_uptake_than_linear_at_high_concentration() {         let bounds = global_bounds();         let high_c_field_grid = (1, 1, 1); // one big cell, so a single deposit sets a known uniform concentration         // Linear config (default: saturable_uptake = None).         let mut linear_config = default_config();         linear_config.field_grid = high_c_field_grid;         linear_config.index_grid = high_c_field_grid;         linear_config.k_uptake = 0.5;         linear_config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         let mut linear = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, linear_config);         linear.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100_000.0); // huge concentration         linear.add_agent(1, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 0.0);         // Saturable config: same everything else, but v_max caps uptake         // far below what the linear formula would give at this concentration.         let mut saturable_config = linear_config;         saturable_config.saturable_uptake = Some(SaturableUptakeParams { v_max: 1.0, k_m: 1.0 });         let mut saturable = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, saturable_config);         saturable.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100_000.0);         saturable.add_agent(1, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 0.0);         let mut rng_a = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         let mut rng_b = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         linear.step(&mut rng_a);         saturable.step(&mut rng_b);         let linear_uptake = linear.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].payload;         let saturable_uptake = saturable.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].payload;         assert!(             saturable_uptake < linear_uptake,             "saturable uptake ({saturable_uptake}) should be far less than linear uptake ({linear_uptake}) at this huge concentration"         );         // Saturable uptake in one dt=0.01 step must stay under v_max*dt=0.01.         assert!(saturable_uptake <= saturable_config.saturable_uptake.unwrap().v_max * saturable_config.dt + 1e-12);     }     /// Confirms `chemotaxis` is actually wired in: with a strong     /// concentration gradient and a large positive coefficient, an     /// agent's motion should be measurably biased toward higher     /// concentration compared to the same scenario with chemotaxis off —     /// checked as a population-level mean displacement (individual     /// agents still have random SDE noise superimposed).     #[test]     fn chemotaxis_config_biases_agent_motion_toward_higher_concentration() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([-20.0, -5.0, -5.0], [20.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.4, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         config.k_uptake = 0.0; // isolate motion; no uptake to complicate the field or payload         let nx = 41;         config.field_grid = (nx, 1, 1);         config.index_grid = (1, 1, 1);         config.chemotaxis = Some(ChemotaxisParams { coeff: 8.0 });         let n_replicates = 300;         let n_steps = 40;         let mut final_x_sum = 0.0;         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(2026);         for _ in 0..n_replicates {             let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);             // A single point deposit is a delta function -- under a             // piecewise-constant field, its concentration is exactly zero             // everywhere except its own cell, so an agent starting even a             // few cells away would feel no gradient at all. Build an             // explicit, smooth linear ramp instead (increasing toward             // +x), the same technique             // mitonano_core::concentration's own gradient tests use, so             // the whole domain has a real, known-direction gradient for             // the agent to actually respond to.             let cell_width = bounds.width(0) / nx as f64;             for i in 0..nx {                 let x = bounds.min[0] + (i as f64 + 0.5) * cell_width;                 let concentration = 5.0 + 0.5 * x; // slope 0.5, always positive across this domain                 fed.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([x, 0.0, 0.0], concentration * cell_width * bounds.width(1) * bounds.width(2));             }             fed.add_agent(1, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             for _ in 0..n_steps {                 fed.step(&mut rng);             }             final_x_sum += fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].position[0];         }         let mean_final_x = final_x_sum / n_replicates as f64;         // No drift and a symmetric start mean the driftless/no-chemotaxis         // expectation is exactly 0; any statistically significant         // positive mean is attributable to chemotaxis pulling agents         // toward higher concentration (+x direction, matching the ramp's         // positive slope).         assert!(             mean_final_x > 0.5,             "mean final x-position ({mean_final_x}) should be measurably biased toward higher concentration (+x)"         );     }     /// The headline correctness requirement for rebalancing: total     /// combined mass (every node's agent payloads + every node's field     /// mass) must be exactly conserved by a rebalance, not just     /// approximately — the field-mass remapping in particular is new,     /// nontrivial logic (`mitonano_orchestrator`'s own rebalance never     /// had to solve this, since its agents carry no field at all) and     /// deserves a direct check, not just an argument from the algebra.     #[test]     fn rebalance_conserves_total_combined_mass_exactly() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [100.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.field_grid = (6, 3, 3);         config.index_grid = (6, 3, 3);         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, bounds, config);         // All agents clustered in node 0's slab -> maximally imbalanced.         for i in 0..40 {             fed.add_agent(i, [5.0 + i as f64 * 0.4, 0.0, 0.0], 3.0);         }         // Field mass spread unevenly too, concentrated where the agents are.         fed.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 500.0);         fed.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([20.0, 0.0, 0.0], 300.0);         let total_before = fed.stats().total_combined_mass;         let report = fed.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).expect("this scenario should need rebalancing");         let total_after = fed.stats().total_combined_mass;         let rel_err = (total_after - total_before).abs() / total_before;         assert!(rel_err < 1e-9, "total_before={total_before} total_after={total_after} rel_err={rel_err}");         assert!(!report.needs_rebalance, "well-separated agents should balance evenly across 4 nodes");         assert_eq!(fed.total_agent_count(), 40, "no agent should be lost or duplicated by rebalancing");     }     /// Confirms rebalancing actually improves the agent-count balance,     /// not just that it runs without error — before, all agents in one     /// node; after, spread roughly evenly.     #[test]     fn rebalance_actually_improves_agent_count_balance() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [100.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let config = default_config();         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(5, bounds, config);         for i in 0..50 {             fed.add_agent(i, [1.0 + i as f64 * 0.3, 0.0, 0.0], 2.0);         }         let pre = fed.check_balance(1.5);         assert!(pre.needs_rebalance, "all 50 agents in one node should be flagged imbalanced");         let post = fed.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).unwrap();         assert!(!post.needs_rebalance, "after rebalancing, 5 nodes should each get ~10 agents");         for node in &fed.nodes {             assert!(node.engine.agents.len() >= 8 && node.engine.agents.len() <= 12, "node {} has {} agents, expected ~10", node.id, node.engine.agents.len());         }     }     #[test]     fn rebalance_if_needed_returns_none_when_already_balanced() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let config = default_config();         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(2, bounds, config);         for i in 0..10 {             fed.add_agent(i, [2.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             fed.add_agent(100 + i, [22.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);         }         assert!(fed.rebalance_if_needed(1.5).is_none());     }     /// Degenerate case (ported from the same scenario     /// `mitonano_orchestrator::coordinator`'s own rebalance test covers):     /// many agents at the exact same x-position can't be separated by any     /// 1D cut, so rebalancing must fall back safely (equal-width slabs)     /// rather than panic or produce a zero-width slab — and, the part     /// orchestrator's version never had to prove, field mass must still     /// come through the remap conserved exactly even when the fallback     /// path is the one that runs.     #[test]     fn rebalance_survives_many_agents_at_the_exact_same_position() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [100.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.field_grid = (6, 3, 3);         config.index_grid = (6, 3, 3);         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(4, bounds, config);         for i in 0..50 {             fed.add_agent(i, [37.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0); // identical position         }         fed.nodes[1].engine.field.deposit([45.0, 0.0, 0.0], 200.0);         let total_before = fed.stats().total_combined_mass;         let result = fed.rebalance_if_needed(1.5);         assert!(result.is_some(), "a cluster with all agents in one node should trigger a rebalance attempt");         let total_after = fed.stats().total_combined_mass;         assert!(             (total_after - total_before).abs() / total_before < 1e-9,             "mass must be conserved even in the degenerate fallback case: before={total_before} after={total_after}"         );         assert_eq!(fed.total_agent_count(), 50);     }     /// The headline fission requirement: population count must actually     /// grow (not just "not panic"), and total payload — the sum across     /// every agent, with field interaction disabled here specifically to     /// isolate fission's own conservation property from everything     /// else's — must be exactly conserved throughout, not merely     /// approximately, since `split_payload` guarantees this by     /// construction (see its own docs).     #[test]     fn fission_config_grows_population_and_conserves_total_payload_exactly() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.k_uptake = 0.0; // isolate fission's own conservation from field exchange         config.agent_params.k_elim = 0.0;         config.agent_params.k_release = 0.0;         config.fission = Some(FissionParams { rate: 2.0, payload_fraction: 0.5, next_generated_id: 1_000_000, mutation: None });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         for i in 0..20 {             fed.add_agent(i, [5.0 + i as f64, 0.0, 0.0], 4.0);         }         let count0 = fed.total_agent_count();         let payload0: f64 = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.payload).sum();         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(555);         for _ in 0..30 {             fed.step(&mut rng);         }         let count_final = fed.total_agent_count();         let payload_final: f64 = fed.nodes.iter().flat_map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter()).map(|a| a.payload).sum();         assert!(count_final > count0, "fission at rate=2.0 over 30 ticks should have grown the population (started at {count0}, ended at {count_final})");         assert!(             (payload_final - payload0).abs() / payload0 < 1e-9,             "total payload must be exactly conserved by fission: before={payload0} after={payload_final}"         );     }     /// The fusion analogue: population must actually shrink, and total     /// payload must be exactly conserved (`merge_payload` is a plain     /// sum — exact by construction).     #[test]     fn fusion_config_shrinks_population_and_conserves_total_payload_exactly() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.k_uptake = 0.0;         config.agent_params.k_elim = 0.0;         config.agent_params.k_release = 0.0;         config.agent_params.diffusion_coeff = 0.0; // keep agents close together so they stay within fusion range         config.agent_params.drift = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0];         config.fusion = Some(FusionParams { radius: 2.0, rate: 3.0 });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         // A tight cluster: every pair starts within the fusion radius.         for i in 0..15 {             fed.add_agent(i, [10.0 + i as f64 * 0.1, 0.0, 0.0], 3.0);         }         let count0 = fed.total_agent_count();         let payload0: f64 = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.payload).sum();         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(777);         for _ in 0..20 {             fed.step(&mut rng);         }         let count_final = fed.total_agent_count();         let payload_final: f64 = fed.nodes.iter().flat_map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter()).map(|a| a.payload).sum();         assert!(count_final < count0, "fusion at rate=3.0 among a tight cluster over 20 ticks should have shrunk the population (started at {count0}, ended at {count_final})");         assert!(             (payload_final - payload0).abs() / payload0 < 1e-9,             "total payload must be exactly conserved by fusion: before={payload0} after={payload_final}"         );     }     /// Confirms a fission daughter is placed exactly at its parent's     /// position (not, say, left at the origin or some other default) and     /// gets a distinct, freshly-generated id — using a rate so high that     /// a single agent fissions within the first tick essentially with     /// certainty, for a deterministic-enough check without relying on     /// large-sample statistics.     #[test]     fn fission_daughter_starts_at_the_parents_exact_position_with_a_fresh_id() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         config.k_uptake = 0.0;         config.fission = Some(FissionParams { rate: 1000.0, payload_fraction: 0.5, next_generated_id: 777_000, mutation: None });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         fed.add_agent(42, [12.5, 1.0, -2.0], 8.0);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         fed.step(&mut rng);         assert_eq!(fed.total_agent_count(), 2, "rate=1000 over one dt=0.01 tick should fission with overwhelming probability");         let positions: Vec<[f64; 3]> = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.position).collect();         // Zero motion (diffusion_coeff=0, drift=0) means both parent and         // daughter should be at *exactly* the original position.         for p in &positions {             assert_eq!(*p, [12.5, 1.0, -2.0]);         }         let ids = &fed.nodes[0].agent_ids;         assert!(ids.contains(&42), "original agent id must survive");         assert!(ids.iter().any(|&id| id >= 777_000), "daughter must get a fresh id from next_generated_id");         assert_eq!(ids.len(), 2);         assert_ne!(ids[0], ids[1], "parent and daughter must have distinct ids");     }     /// Sanity check that fission and fusion can be configured     /// simultaneously without panicking or breaking conservation — not a     /// claim that they cancel out exactly (they operate on different     /// agents/pairs), only that total payload is still exactly conserved     /// through both operating in the same run.     #[test]     fn fission_and_fusion_can_both_be_active_in_the_same_run() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [60.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.k_uptake = 0.0;         config.agent_params.k_elim = 0.0;         config.agent_params.k_release = 0.0;         config.fission = Some(FissionParams { rate: 0.5, payload_fraction: 0.5, next_generated_id: 500_000, mutation: None });         config.fusion = Some(FusionParams { radius: 1.5, rate: 0.5 });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         for i in 0..20 {             fed.add_agent(i, [5.0 + i as f64 * 0.5, 0.0, 0.0], 5.0);         }         let payload0: f64 = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents.iter().map(|a| a.payload).sum();         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(9001);         for _ in 0..50 {             fed.step(&mut rng);         }         let payload_final: f64 = fed.nodes.iter().flat_map(|n| n.engine.agents.iter()).map(|a| a.payload).sum();         assert!(             (payload_final - payload0).abs() / payload0 < 1e-9,             "total payload must be exactly conserved with both fission and fusion active: before={payload0} after={payload_final}"         );         assert!(fed.total_agent_count() > 0, "population should not have been wiped out entirely");     }     /// Confirms `two_state` actually toggles agent mobility through the     /// real federated wiring (not just `mitonano_core::mobility`'s own     /// primitives in isolation) — using `k_on` large enough that an     /// initially-`Mobile` agent should become `Bound` within the first     /// tick with overwhelming probability, for a deterministic-enough     /// check without relying on large-sample statistics.     #[test]     fn two_state_config_actually_toggles_agent_mobility() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.two_state = Some(TwoStateParams { k_on: 1000.0, k_off: 0.0 });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         fed.add_agent(1, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 5.0);         assert_eq!(fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].mobility, MobilityState::Mobile, "sanity: should start Mobile");         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         fed.step(&mut rng);         assert_eq!(             fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].mobility,             MobilityState::Bound,             "k_on=1000.0 over one dt=0.01 tick should switch to Bound with overwhelming probability"         );     }     /// The core physical claim this feature exists to implement: a     /// `Bound` agent's position must not change (even with strong drift     /// and diffusion configured), while its payload still exchanges with     /// the field exactly as a `Mobile` agent's would — binding models     /// spatial immobilization specifically, not a change in biochemistry     /// (see `mitonano_core::mobility`'s own module docs).     #[test]     fn bound_agents_do_not_move_but_still_exchange_payload_with_the_field() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [5.0, 5.0, 5.0], diffusion_coeff: 2.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         config.k_uptake = 0.5;         // k_off=0.0 (and k_on=0.0, since we set mobility directly below)         // means the agent's forced Bound state can never spontaneously         // change during this test.         config.two_state = Some(TwoStateParams { k_on: 0.0, k_off: 0.0 });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         fed.nodes[0].engine.field.deposit([10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1000.0); // concentration for uptake to draw from         fed.add_agent(1, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 0.0);         fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].mobility = MobilityState::Bound;         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         for _ in 0..10 {             fed.step(&mut rng);         }         assert_eq!(fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].position, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], "a bound agent must not move despite strong drift and diffusion");         assert!(fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].payload > 0.0, "a bound agent must still take up payload from the field it's sitting on");     }     /// The population-level closed-form behavior     /// `mitonano_core::mobility`'s own tests already prove for the raw     /// primitives, now confirmed through the actual `FederatedEngineConfig`     /// wiring: a population with `two_state` configured should show     /// measurably *less* spread than an otherwise-identical population     /// with `two_state: None`, since bound agents contribute zero     /// displacement variance while bound.     #[test]     fn two_state_config_reduces_population_spread_relative_to_always_mobile() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([-40.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut base_config = default_config();         base_config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.6, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         base_config.k_uptake = 0.0;         let n_replicates = 300;         let n_steps = 60;         let mut always_mobile_config = base_config;         always_mobile_config.two_state = None;         let mut two_state_config = base_config;         two_state_config.two_state = Some(TwoStateParams { k_on: 2.0, k_off: 2.0 }); // p_mobile_eq=0.5         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(4242);         let mut sum_sq_always_mobile = 0.0;         let mut sum_sq_two_state = 0.0;         for _ in 0..n_replicates {             let mut fed_a = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, always_mobile_config);             fed_a.add_agent(1, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             let mut fed_b = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, two_state_config);             fed_b.add_agent(1, [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], 1.0);             for _ in 0..n_steps {                 fed_a.step(&mut rng);                 fed_b.step(&mut rng);             }             let xa = fed_a.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].position[0];             let xb = fed_b.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].position[0];             sum_sq_always_mobile += xa * xa;             sum_sq_two_state += xb * xb;         }         let msd_always_mobile = sum_sq_always_mobile / n_replicates as f64;         let msd_two_state = sum_sq_two_state / n_replicates as f64;         assert!(             msd_two_state < msd_always_mobile,             "two-state population MSD ({msd_two_state}) should be measurably less than the always-mobile baseline ({msd_always_mobile})"         );     }     /// Confirms `Agent::phenotype` actually changes physics through the     /// real `step()` wiring (not just `Agent::effective_params` in     /// isolation, which `mitonano_core`'s own tests already cover): two     /// agents in the same node, one with a phenotype override giving it a     /// much higher clearance rate, should visibly diverge in payload     /// after real ticks.     #[test]     fn phenotype_override_actually_changes_agent_physics_through_the_real_wiring() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.02, k_release: 0.02 }; // total rate 0.04         config.k_uptake = 0.0;         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         fed.add_agent(1, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100.0); // default phenotype (None) -> uses config.agent_params         fed.add_agent(2, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 100.0);         let override_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.4, k_release: 0.0 }; // total rate 0.4, 10x faster         fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[1].phenotype = Some(override_params);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         let n_steps = 100;         for _ in 0..n_steps {             fed.step(&mut rng);         }         let t = n_steps as f64 * config.dt;         let default_payload = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].payload;         let overridden_payload = fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[1].payload;         // Compare against the actual closed-form exponential decay for         // each rate (decay_and_release uses the exact Poisson formula —         // see mitonano_core's own closed-form tests), rather than an         // arbitrary threshold guess.         let expected_default = 100.0 * (-0.04 * t).exp();         let expected_overridden = 100.0 * (-0.4 * t).exp();         assert!(             (default_payload - expected_default).abs() / expected_default < 0.05,             "default-phenotype payload={default_payload} vs closed-form={expected_default}"         );         assert!(             (overridden_payload - expected_overridden).abs() / expected_overridden < 0.05,             "overridden-phenotype payload={overridden_payload} vs closed-form={expected_overridden}"         );         assert!(             overridden_payload < default_payload * 0.8,             "agent with a 10x-faster clearance override ({overridden_payload}) should have visibly outpaced the default-phenotype agent ({default_payload})"         );     }     /// The fission/heterogeneity composition point the biological roadmap     /// review named directly: by default (no `mutation` configured), a     /// fission daughter must inherit the parent's `phenotype` exactly,     /// not silently reset to the node's default physics.     #[test]     fn fission_daughter_inherits_the_parents_phenotype_by_default() {         let bounds = Bounds::new([0.0, -5.0, -5.0], [40.0, 5.0, 5.0]);         let mut config = default_config();         config.agent_params = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.0, k_release: 0.0 };         config.k_uptake = 0.0;         config.fission = Some(FissionParams { rate: 1000.0, payload_fraction: 0.5, next_generated_id: 900_000, mutation: None });         let mut fed = FederatedUnifiedEngine::new(1, bounds, config);         let damaged_phenotype = AgentParams { drift: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], diffusion_coeff: 0.0, k_elim: 0.3, k_release: 0.0 };         fed.add_agent(1, [10.0, 0.0, 0.0], 10.0);         fed.nodes[0].engine.agents[0].phenotype = Some(damaged_phenotype);         let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(1);         fed.step(&mut rng);         assert_eq!(fed.total_agent_count(), 2, "rate=1000 over one tick should fission with overwhelming probability");
